\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{1})
,
\chi\equiv 1
on
B_{3/4}
, and define

p^{\rm rem}=2\mathcal{R}_{a}\mathcal{R}_{3}(\chi u_{a}u_{3})+\mathcal{R}_{3}\mathcal{R}_{3}(\chi u_{3}^{2}).

(A.18)

Set

P=p-p^{\rm rem}.

(A.19)

The part
P
is the pressure with the explicit
u_{3}
-generated Calderon–Zygmund component removed.

Lemma A.3

(Vertical pressure remainder)
.

If
\Phi(1)\leq M
and
C_{3}(1)=\delta\leq 1
, then

\|p^{\rm rem}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{3/4})}\leq C(M)\delta^{1/3}.

The same estimate holds on every smaller interior cylinder, with a constant depending on the separation from the boundary.

Proof.

The boundedness of Riesz transforms on
L^{3/2}
gives

\|p^{\rm rem}\|_{L^{3/2}}\leq C\|u_{h}u_{3}\|_{L^{3/2}}+C\|u_{3}^{2}\|_{L^{3/2}}.

By Holder’s inequality,

\|u_{h}u_{3}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}\leq\|u_{h}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\|u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq C(M)\delta^{1/3},

and

\|u_{3}^{2}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{1})}=\|u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}^{2}=\delta^{2/3}\leq\delta^{1/3}.

∎

A.3.2
Weighted vertical pressure compatibility

Let

Q=\omega_{h}\times I_{3}\times I_{t}\Subset Q_{3/4},\qquad I_{3}=(a,b).

Choose
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(I_{3})
with
\int_{I_{3}}\zeta=1
. For a scalar
f
, set

\langle f\rangle_{\zeta}(x_{h},t)=\int_{I_{3}}\zeta(y)f(x_{h},y,t)\,dy,\qquad\Pi_{\zeta}f=f-\langle f\rangle_{\zeta}.

For
\varphi_{h}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{2})
, define

f_{\varphi}=\nabla_{h}\!\cdot\varphi_{h},

g_{\varphi}(x_{h},x_{3},t)=f_{\varphi}(x_{h},x_{3},t)-\zeta(x_{3})\int_{I_{3}}f_{\varphi}(x_{h},y,t)\,dy.

Then
\int_{I_{3}}g_{\varphi}\,dx_{3}=0
. Set

B_{\varphi}(x_{h},x_{3},t)=\int_{a}^{x_{3}}g_{\varphi}(x_{h},s,t)\,ds.

Because
g_{\varphi}
has zero vertical mean and is supported away from the endpoints,
B_{\varphi}
is compactly supported in the vertical variable and
\partial_{3}B_{\varphi}=g_{\varphi}
. Define

\|\varphi_{h}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\zeta}(Q)}=\|(\partial_{t}+\Delta)B_{\varphi}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q)}+\|\nabla_{h}B_{\varphi}\|_{L^{3}(Q)}+\|\partial_{3}B_{\varphi}\|_{L^{3}(Q)}.

Lemma A.4

(Weighted vertical pressure compatibility)
.

For every product cylinder
Q\Subset Q_{3/4}
and every
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(I_{3})
with
\int\zeta=1
,

\|\nabla_{h}\Pi_{\zeta}P\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\zeta}^{\prime}(Q)}\leq C(M,Q,\zeta)\delta^{1/3},

where
P=p-p^{\rm rem}
.

Proof.

For
\varphi_{h}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q;\mathbb{R}^{2})
, integration by parts gives

\langle\nabla_{h}\Pi_{\zeta}P,\varphi_{h}\rangle=\langle\partial_{3}P,B_{\varphi}\rangle.

(A.20)

Indeed,

\langle\nabla_{h}\Pi_{\zeta}P,\varphi_{h}\rangle=-\int_{Q}\Pi_{\zeta}P\,f_{\varphi}\,dx\,dt=-\int_{Q}P\,g_{\varphi}\,dx\,dt=-\int_{Q}P\,\partial_{3}B_{\varphi}\,dx\,dt.

Since
B_{\varphi}
is compactly supported in the vertical variable, this equals
\langle\partial_{3}P,B_{\varphi}\rangle
.

The vertical momentum equation gives

\partial_{3}P=-\partial_{t}u_{3}+\Delta u_{3}-\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(u_{h}u_{3})-\partial_{3}(u_{3}^{2})-\partial_{3}p^{\rm rem}.

Pairing with
B_{\varphi}
, moving derivatives to the test function, and using
Lemma

A.3
,

\displaystyle|\langle\partial_{3}P,B_{\varphi}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{3}\|_{L^{3}}\|(\partial_{t}+\Delta)B_{\varphi}\|_{L^{3/2}}+\|u_{h}u_{3}\|_{L^{3/2}}\|\nabla_{h}B_{\varphi}\|_{L^{3}}

\displaystyle+\|u_{3}^{2}\|_{L^{3/2}}\|\partial_{3}B_{\varphi}\|_{L^{3}}+\|p^{\rm rem}\|_{L^{3/2}}\|\partial_{3}B_{\varphi}\|_{L^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,Q,\zeta)\delta^{1/3}\|\varphi_{h}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{\zeta}(Q)}.

Taking the supremum over
\varphi_{h}
proves the lemma.
∎

A.4
Low-frequency horizontal preparation

This section proves the coarse solenoidal preparation. The estimates are local in product cylinders. Let

Q^{\prime}=\omega^{\prime}\times I_{3}^{\prime}\times I_{t}^{\prime}\Subset Q_{*}=\omega_{*}\times I_{3}\times I_{t}\Subset Q_{3/4},

where
\omega_{*}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is a smooth bounded domain. Let
S_{\ell}
be a spatial mollifier at scale
0<\ell<\ell_{0}(Q^{\prime},Q_{*})
, so that
S_{\ell}f
in
Q^{\prime}
only depends on values of
f
in
Q_{*}
.

Lemma A.5

(Coarse tail estimate)
.

If
\Phi(1)\leq M
, then

\|u-S_{\ell}u\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\ell^{1/6}.

Proof.

The local energy bound gives

u\in L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}(Q_{*})\cap L_{t}^{2}H_{x}^{1}(Q_{*}),

with norm bounded by
C(M,Q_{*})
. Hence

\|u-S_{\ell}u\|_{L^{2}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C\ell\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(Q_{*})}\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\ell.

The same bound and interpolation imply
u\in L^{10/3}(Q_{*})
, and therefore

\|u-S_{\ell}u\|_{L^{10/3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*}).

Interpolating
L^{2}
and
L^{10/3}
, using

\frac{1}{3}=\frac{1}{6}\frac{1}{2}+\frac{5}{6}\frac{3}{10},

gives the result.
∎

Lemma A.6

(Low-frequency horizontal solenoidal preparation)
.

Assume
\Phi(1)\leq M
and
C_{3}(1)=\delta\leq 1
. For a.e.
(x_{3},t)
, let
\phi^{\ell}(\cdot,x_{3},t)
solve

\left\{\begin{aligned} -\Delta_{h}\phi^{\ell}&=\nabla_{h}\!\cdot S_{\ell}u_{h}&&\text{in }\omega_{*},\\
\phi^{\ell}&=0&&\text{on }\partial\omega_{*}.\end{aligned}\right.

Define

B_{h}^{\ell}=\nabla_{h}\phi^{\ell},\qquad U_{h}^{\ell}=S_{\ell}u_{h}+B_{h}^{\ell},\qquad U^{\ell}=(U_{h}^{\ell},0).

Then

\nabla_{h}\!\cdot U_{h}^{\ell}=0\qquad\text{in }Q_{*}.

Moreover,

\|u-U^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\left(\ell^{1/6}+\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3}\right).

(A.21)

For each integer
k\geq 0
,

\|\nabla^{k}U_{h}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C_{k}(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\left(\ell^{-k}+\ell^{-k-1}\delta^{1/3}\right).

(A.22)

Proof.

Since
u
is divergence-free,

\nabla_{h}\!\cdot S_{\ell}u_{h}=S_{\ell}(\nabla_{h}\!\cdot u_{h})=-S_{\ell}\partial_{3}u_{3}.

By construction,

\nabla_{h}\!\cdot B_{h}^{\ell}=\Delta_{h}\phi^{\ell}=-\nabla_{h}\!\cdot S_{\ell}u_{h}.

Thus
\nabla_{h}\!\cdot U_{h}^{\ell}=0
.

The smoothing tail is controlled by
Lemma

A.5
. For the correction, elliptic estimates for the horizontal Dirichlet problem give, for a.e.
(x_{3},t)
,

\|B_{h}^{\ell}(\cdot,x_{3},t)\|_{L^{3}(\omega_{*})}\leq C\|\nabla_{h}\!\cdot S_{\ell}u_{h}(\cdot,x_{3},t)\|_{L^{3}(\omega_{*})}.

Hence

\displaystyle\|B_{h}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|S_{\ell}\partial_{3}u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}=C\|\partial_{3}S_{\ell}u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}

\displaystyle\leq C\ell^{-1}\|u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}=C\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3}.

Since

u_{h}-U_{h}^{\ell}=(u_{h}-S_{\ell}u_{h})-B_{h}^{\ell},

we obtain the horizontal part of (
A.21
). The vertical part satisfies

\|u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq\delta^{1/3}\leq\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3},

for
0<\ell<1
. This proves (
A.21
).

The derivative estimates for the mollified part follow from the standard mollifier bounds:

\|\nabla^{k}S_{\ell}u_{h}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C_{k}\ell^{-k}\|u_{h}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}\leq C_{k}(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\ell^{-k}.

For the Helmholtz correction we use the elliptic equation rather than a separate frequency-localization statement for
B_{h}^{\ell}
. The case
k=0
has already been proved:

\|B_{h}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C\|S_{\ell}\partial_{3}u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}\leq C\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3}.

For
k\geq 1
, horizontal elliptic regularity applied to

-\Delta_{h}\phi^{\ell}=-S_{\ell}\partial_{3}u_{3},\qquad B_{h}^{\ell}=\nabla_{h}\phi^{\ell},

after differentiating in the remaining spatial variables, gives

\|\nabla^{k}B_{h}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C_{k}\|\nabla^{k-1}S_{\ell}\partial_{3}u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}\leq C_{k}\ell^{-k-1}\|u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{1})}\leq C_{k}\ell^{-k-1}\delta^{1/3}.

Combining this with the estimate for
S_{\ell}u_{h}
proves (
A.22
).
∎

Remark A.7
.

The use of a bounded horizontal Dirichlet problem avoids the singular horizontal Fourier multiplier
|\xi_{h}|^{-1}
at
\xi_{h}=0
. In a full manuscript one covers a ball by finitely many product charts and patches the construction on a slightly smaller target cylinder.

A.5
The coarse residual equation

We first derive an exact residual identity for the uncorrected coarse field
S_{\ell}u_{h}
. Then we explain how the horizontal correction from
Lemma

A.6
changes only the
u_{3}
-small residual, at the cost of further finite powers of
\ell^{-1}
.

A.5.1
Uncorrected coarse residual

Let

V_{h}^{\ell}=S_{\ell}u_{h}.

Define the vertically averaged pressure

\bar{P}^{\ell}(x_{h},t)=\langle S_{\ell}P\rangle_{\zeta}(x_{h},t),

(A.23)

where
P=p-p^{\rm rem}
and
\zeta
is fixed as in
Section

A.3
. Set

R^{\ell}=V_{h}^{\ell}\otimes V_{h}^{\ell}-S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h}).

(A.24)

Also set

F_{\delta}^{\ell}=-\partial_{3}S_{\ell}(u_{3}u_{h})-\nabla_{h}(S_{\ell}p-\bar{P}^{\ell}).

(A.25)

Proposition A.2

(Coarse residual equation, uncorrected form)
.

In
Q^{\prime}
, the field
V_{h}^{\ell}=S_{\ell}u_{h}
satisfies

\partial_{t}V_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta V_{h}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(V_{h}^{\ell}\otimes V_{h}^{\ell})+\nabla_{h}\bar{P}^{\ell}=\nabla_{h}\!\cdot R^{\ell}+F_{\delta}^{\ell},

(A.26)

and

\nabla_{h}\!\cdot V_{h}^{\ell}=g^{\ell},\qquad g^{\ell}=-S_{\ell}\partial_{3}u_{3}.

(A.27)

Moreover,

\|R^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\ell^{1/6},

(A.28)

while, for a residual norm
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(Q^{\prime})
containing both
L^{3/2}
and the weighted pressure dual norm from
Lemma

A.4
,

\|F_{\delta}^{\ell}\|_{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*},\zeta)\ell^{-N}\delta^{1/3}

(A.29)

for some finite
N
. Finally,

\|g^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}_{t}W^{-1,3}_{x}(Q^{\prime})}+\ell\|g^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C(Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\delta^{1/3}.

(A.30)

Proof.

The horizontal equation in divergence form is

\partial_{t}u_{h}-\Delta u_{h}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})+\partial_{3}(u_{3}u_{h})+\nabla_{h}p=0.

Applying
S_{\ell}
gives

\partial_{t}S_{\ell}u_{h}-\Delta S_{\ell}u_{h}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})+\partial_{3}S_{\ell}(u_{3}u_{h})+\nabla_{h}S_{\ell}p=0.

Adding and subtracting
\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(V_{h}^{\ell}\otimes V_{h}^{\ell})
and replacing
\nabla_{h}S_{\ell}p
by
\nabla_{h}\bar{P}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}(S_{\ell}p-\bar{P}^{\ell})
, we obtain (
A.26
). The identity (
A.27
) follows from incompressibility.

For (
A.28
), use the commutator estimate

\|R^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C\|u_{h}-S_{\ell}u_{h}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}\|u_{h}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})},

and then
Lemma

A.5
.

For the vertical transport part,

\|\partial_{3}S_{\ell}(u_{3}u_{h})\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C\ell^{-1}\|u_{3}u_{h}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{*})}\leq C(M)\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3}.

For the pressure part, write

S_{\ell}p-\bar{P}^{\ell}=S_{\ell}P-\langle S_{\ell}P\rangle_{\zeta}+S_{\ell}p^{\rm rem}.

The term
S_{\ell}p^{\rm rem}
is estimated using
Lemma

A.3
:

\|\nabla_{h}S_{\ell}p^{\rm rem}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C\ell^{-1}\|p^{\rm rem}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{*})}\leq C(M)\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3}.

The remaining term is controlled by
Lemma

A.4
; smoothing loses only a finite power of
\ell^{-1}
in the chosen dual norm. This proves (
A.29
).

Finally,

\|g^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}=\|S_{\ell}\partial_{3}u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C\ell^{-1}\|u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}\leq C\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3},

and in negative norm,

\|S_{\ell}\partial_{3}u_{3}\|_{L^{3}_{t}W^{-1,3}_{x}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C\|S_{\ell}u_{3}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}\leq C\delta^{1/3}.

∎

A.5.2
Solenoidal corrected residual

The field required for the two-shadow energy identity must be horizontally divergence-free. Let
U_{h}^{\ell}=S_{\ell}u_{h}+B_{h}^{\ell}
be the field from
Lemma

A.6
. The correction
B_{h}^{\ell}
is controlled by
\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3}
, so inserting
U_{h}^{\ell}
in place of
V_{h}^{\ell}
changes the equation only through terms containing at least one small vertical factor, except for the time derivative of the horizontal Helmholtz correction. We therefore isolate the exact residual admissibility needed later.

Definition A.1

(Solenoidal-correction residual admissibility)
.

On each fixed interior product cylinder
Q^{\prime}\Subset Q_{*}
, an admissible finite-power residual norm
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}(Q^{\prime})
is any energy-dual norm stable under one spatial derivative and under the localized weak–strong pairing used in
Proposition

A.4
; for instance one may work with a finite sum of components of the form

L^{3/2}_{t}W^{-1,3/2}_{x}(Q^{\prime})+L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{x}(Q^{\prime})+L^{1}_{t}W^{-2,1}_{x}(Q^{\prime})\qquad\text{(model components)}

after fixing the interior cutoffs. The solenoidal correction is called residual-admissible if

\|(\partial_{t}-\Delta)B_{h}^{\ell}\|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\ell^{-N}\delta^{1/3}

(A.31)

for some finite
N
. With a time-regularized preparation this follows directly with a finite parabolic smoothing loss. With the purely spatial preparation of
Lemma

A.6
, (
A.31
) is retained as part of the prepared residual package rather than inferred from the local energy bounds alone.

Proposition A.3

(Coarse residual equation, solenoidal form under residual admissibility)
.

Let
U^{\ell}=(U_{h}^{\ell},0)
be the prepared horizontal field from
Lemma

A.6
, and assume the solenoidal-correction residual admissibility (
A.31
). Then, after modifying the pressure by an admissible local scalar
P^{\ell}
, one has in
Q^{\prime}

\partial_{t}U_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta U_{h}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(U_{h}^{\ell}\otimes U_{h}^{\ell})+\nabla_{h}P^{\ell}=\nabla_{h}\!\cdot\widetilde{R}^{\ell}+\widetilde{F}_{\delta}^{\ell},\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot U_{h}^{\ell}=0.

(A.32)

Moreover,

\|\widetilde{R}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\ell^{1/6},

and

\|\widetilde{F}_{\delta}^{\ell}\|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\ell^{-N}\delta^{1/3}

for a finite-power residual norm
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
adapted to the localized weak–strong estimate.

Proof.

Write
U_{h}^{\ell}=V_{h}^{\ell}+B_{h}^{\ell}
. Starting from (
A.26
), move the terms generated by
B_{h}^{\ell}
to the right-hand side. The linear correction is exactly

\partial_{t}B_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta B_{h}^{\ell},

and is controlled in
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
by (
A.31
). This is the only place where time regularity of the horizontal Helmholtz correction is needed; no unconditional estimate for this term is asserted for a merely spatial mollifier.

For the nonlinear terms,

\displaystyle\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(U_{h}^{\ell}\otimes U_{h}^{\ell})-\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(V_{h}^{\ell}\otimes V_{h}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(V_{h}^{\ell}\otimes B_{h}^{\ell}+B_{h}^{\ell}\otimes V_{h}^{\ell}+B_{h}^{\ell}\otimes B_{h}^{\ell}).

Each term contains at least one factor of
B_{h}^{\ell}
. By
Lemma

A.6
,

\|B_{h}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}}\leq C\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3},\qquad\|V_{h}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}}\leq C(M),

and higher derivatives of
V_{h}^{\ell}
cost only finite powers of
\ell^{-1}
. Thus the nonlinear correction belongs to
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
with size
C\ell^{-N}\delta^{1/3}
. The original Reynolds commutator remains of size
\ell^{1/6}
, possibly after enlarging
\widetilde{R}^{\ell}
by harmless
B
-free commutators. This proves the proposition.
∎

Remark A.8
.

Proposition

A.3
is deliberately conditional on
Definition

A.1
. The low-frequency divergence correction and the nonlinear
B_{h}^{\ell}
-terms are directly finite-power small; the possible gap is the time derivative of the spatial Helmholtz correction. In the final theorem this admissibility is included in the prepared covariance-form residual package, together with the prepared pressure closure. The key structural conclusion remains that the non-small Reynolds commutator is separated from every residual carrying the factor
\delta^{1/3}
.

A.6
Variance-buffered two-shadow stability

The role of this section is to isolate the stability mechanism that keeps the Reynolds commutator out of the high exponential factor. The commutator bookkeeping is close in spirit to the Constantin–E–Titi proof of Onsager energy conservation, adapted here to a localized horizontal variance balance
[
11
]
. The construction has two layers. First, one proves an Onsager-type variance identity for the uncorrected coarse field

\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}=S_{\ell}u_{h}.

Only after this exact identity has been obtained does one replace
\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}
by the horizontally solenoidal prepared field

U_{h}^{\ell}=\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}+B_{h}^{\ell}.

The correction
B_{h}^{\ell}
is generated by the small component
u_{3}
, and therefore every term containing it belongs to the finite-power
\ell^{-1}\delta^{b}
residual class. This order of proof is important: the covariance tensor is attached canonically to
S_{\ell}u_{h}
, not to the corrected field.

Throughout this section we work first in a smooth interior model setting. For suitable weak solutions the formulas are used only in the weak horizontal-defect admissible sense stated in
Remark

A.9
. In particular, we do not use the full local energy inequality to assert an unconditional nonnegative defect for the horizontal energy alone. The limiting horizontal defect is either assumed to be a nonnegative measure or is retained with a controlled signed contribution in the relative entropy inequality.

A.6.1
Covariance stress and unresolved variance

Let

\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}=S_{\ell}u_{h}.

Recall that the uncorrected Reynolds commutator from
Proposition

A.2
is

R^{\ell}=\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}\otimes\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}-S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h}).

We use the positive covariance convention

\tau^{\ell}:=S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})-\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}\otimes\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}=-R^{\ell}.

(A.33)

Thus
\tau^{\ell}
is nonnegative definite in the horizontal variables. The corresponding unresolved variance is

\kappa^{\ell}=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\tau^{\ell}=\frac{1}{2}\left(S_{\ell}|u_{h}|^{2}-|S_{\ell}u_{h}|^{2}\right).

(A.34)

We also set

D_{h}^{\ell}=S_{\ell}|\nabla u_{h}|^{2}-|\nabla\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}|^{2}.

(A.35)

Lemma A.7

(Positivity and size of the covariance stress)
.

Assume that
S_{\ell}
is a nonnegative normalized spatial mollifier. Then, for almost every
(x,t)
, the tensor
\tau^{\ell}(x,t)
is nonnegative definite in the horizontal variables and
\kappa^{\ell}\geq 0
. Moreover, on every interior cylinder
Q^{\prime}\Subset Q_{*}
,

\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\ell^{1/6},

(A.36)

and

\int_{I_{0}}\int_{B^{\prime}}\kappa^{\ell}(x,t)\,dx\,dt\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*})\ell^{2}

(A.37)

for each compact time interval
I_{0}
contained in the time projection of
Q^{\prime}
. Consequently there exists a good time
s_{\ell}\in I_{0}
such that

\int_{B^{\prime}}\kappa^{\ell}(x,s_{\ell})\,dx\leq C(M,Q^{\prime},Q_{*},I_{0})\ell^{2}.

(A.38)

Proof.

For any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, Jensen’s inequality gives

\xi\cdot\tau^{\ell}\xi=S_{\ell}\bigl((\xi\cdot u_{h})^{2}\bigr)-\bigl(S_{\ell}(\xi\cdot u_{h})\bigr)^{2}\geq 0.

Thus
\tau^{\ell}\geq 0
and
\kappa^{\ell}\geq 0
. The estimate (
A.36
) is exactly (
A.28
), since
\tau^{\ell}=-R^{\ell}
. Finally, the standard covariance identity gives

\int_{B^{\prime}}\kappa^{\ell}(x,t)\,dx\leq C\ell^{2}\int_{B_{*}}|\nabla u_{h}(x,t)|^{2}\,dx,

up to harmless cutoff errors depending on the separation of
B^{\prime}
from
\partial B_{*}
. Integrating in time and using the local energy bound gives (
A.37
). The good-time estimate (
A.38
) follows by averaging over
I_{0}
.
∎

With the positive covariance convention, the uncorrected coarse equation is

\partial_{t}\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}\otimes\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell})+\nabla_{h}\bar{P}^{\ell}=-\nabla_{h}\!\cdot\tau^{\ell}+F_{\delta}^{\ell}.

(A.39)

Equivalently,

\partial_{t}\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}\otimes\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}+\tau^{\ell})+\nabla_{h}\bar{P}^{\ell}=F_{\delta}^{\ell}.

The equality should be read first for the uncorrected coarse field. In the actual local argument, the divergence defect of
S_{\ell}u_{h}
and the horizontal correction
B_{h}^{\ell}
are placed in the
u_{3}
-small residual class, as in
Proposition

A.3
.

A.6.2
Localized Onsager variance identity

The following lemma is the localized version of the Onsager variance balance needed in the relative entropy argument
[
11
]
. The point is that the exact identity is first derived for
\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}=S_{\ell}u_{h}
. The replacement by the corrected field
U_{h}^{\ell}
is made only afterward, and the resulting error is small because
B_{h}^{\ell}
is generated by
u_{3}
.

Remark A.9

(Weak-solution status of the horizontal variance identity)
.

For smooth solutions the identity below is an algebraic localized Onsager balance. For suitable weak solutions it is used in the regularized sense under the additional requirement that the horizontal energy defect produced by the limiting procedure is a nonnegative measure, or else that the signed defect can be retained and controlled in the relative entropy inequality. Thus every later use of this identity should be read as conditional on this weak horizontal-defect admissibility.

Lemma A.8

(Localized Onsager variance identity)
.

Let

Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q^{\prime}\Subset Q_{*}\Subset Q_{3/4}

be fixed interior product cylinders, and let

\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q^{\prime}),\qquad 0\leq\phi\leq 1,\qquad\phi\equiv 1\quad\text{on }Q_{\rm tar}.

Assume
\Phi(1)\leq M
,
C_{3}(1)=\delta\leq 1
, and let
U_{h}^{\ell}=\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}+B_{h}^{\ell}
be the horizontally solenoidal prepared field of
Lemma

A.6
. Then, in the sense of distributions in time,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int\phi\kappa^{\ell}\,dx+\int\phi D_{h}^{\ell}\,dx+\int\phi\,S_{\ell}\mu_{h}\,dx

\displaystyle=-\int\phi\,\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}U_{h}^{\ell}\,dx

(A.40)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{E}_{\rm cut}^{\ell}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\ell}(t).

Here
\mu_{h}
denotes the horizontal defect measure arising from the regularized horizontal energy balance. In the smooth case
\mu_{h}=0
; in the suitable weak setting, its nonnegativity or controlled signed form is part of the conditional weak horizontal-defect admissibility stated in
Remark

A.9
. The error terms satisfy, after possibly increasing
N
and decreasing
b>0
,

\int|\mathcal{E}_{\rm cut}^{\ell}(t)|\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6},

(A.41)

and

\int|\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\ell}(t)|\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(A.42)

Equivalently, if one uses the Reynolds sign convention

R^{\ell}=\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}\otimes\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}-S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})=-\tau^{\ell},

then the main term in (
A.40
) is
+\int\phi R^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}U_{h}^{\ell}\,dx
.

Proof.

We first derive the identity for the uncorrected field
\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}=S_{\ell}u_{h}
in a smooth setting. In the suitable weak setting this derivation is interpreted after regularization and only under the weak horizontal-defect admissibility of
Remark

A.9
. Under that hypothesis the limiting horizontal defect term is either nonnegative and kept on the left-hand side, or is retained with the controlled sign required by the relative entropy estimate.

The horizontal equation in divergence form is

\partial_{t}u_{h}-\Delta u_{h}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})+\nabla_{h}p=-\partial_{3}(u_{3}u_{h}).

(A.43)

After applying
S_{\ell}
, subtracting and adding the resolved flux, one obtains

\partial_{t}\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}\otimes\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell})+\nabla_{h}S_{\ell}p=-\nabla_{h}\!\cdot\tau^{\ell}-\partial_{3}S_{\ell}(u_{3}u_{h}).

(A.44)

The last term will be placed in the
\delta
-small error class.

Consider the fine horizontal energy density
|u_{h}|^{2}/2
and the resolved horizontal energy density
|\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}|^{2}/2
. Applying
S_{\ell}
to the fine horizontal energy balance and subtracting the resolved energy balance obtained from (
A.44
) gives

\partial_{t}\kappa^{\ell}-\Delta\kappa^{\ell}+D_{h}^{\ell}+S_{\ell}\mu_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot J_{h}^{\ell}=-\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}+G_{\delta}^{\ell}.

(A.45)

Here
J_{h}^{\ell}
is the horizontal variance flux, consisting of the third-order kinetic commutator and the pressure flux commutator, and
G_{\delta}^{\ell}
contains all terms with at least one factor generated by vertical transport or the pressure remainder. More explicitly, the kinetic part of
J_{h}^{\ell}
has the schematic form

S_{\ell}\left(\frac{|u_{h}|^{2}}{2}u_{h}\right)-\frac{|\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}|^{2}}{2}\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}-\tau^{\ell}\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell},

while the pressure part is

S_{\ell}(pu_{h})-S_{\ell}p\,\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}.

The precise expression of
J_{h}^{\ell}
is immaterial below; only its commutator structure is used.

Multiplying (
A.45
) by
\phi
, integrating in space, and integrating by parts gives

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int\phi\kappa^{\ell}\,dx+\int\phi D_{h}^{\ell}\,dx+\int\phi S_{\ell}\mu_{h}\,dx

\displaystyle=-\int\phi\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}\,dx

(A.46)

\displaystyle+\int\kappa^{\ell}(\partial_{t}\phi+\Delta\phi)\,dx+\int J_{h}^{\ell}\cdot\nabla_{h}\phi\,dx+\int\phi G_{\delta}^{\ell}\,dx.

The first two error terms on the right define
\mathcal{E}_{\rm cut}^{\ell}
. The last term, together with the correction error introduced below, defines
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\ell}
.

We estimate the cutoff terms. By the covariance identity and the local energy bound,

\int_{Q^{\prime}}\kappa^{\ell}\,dx\,dt\leq C\ell^{2}\int_{Q_{*}}|\nabla u_{h}|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C_{M}\ell^{2}\leq C_{M}\ell^{1/6}.

The kinetic flux is estimated by the standard third-order commutator bound

\|J_{h,{\rm kin}}^{\ell}\|_{L^{1}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C\|u_{h}-S_{\ell}u_{h}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}\|u_{h}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}^{2}+C\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{\prime})}\|\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})},

and hence, by
Lemma

A.5
and (
A.36
),

\|J_{h,{\rm kin}}^{\ell}\|_{L^{1}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}.

For the pressure flux, write
p=P+p^{\rm rem}
. The part involving
p^{\rm rem}
is assigned to
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\ell}
, using
Lemma

A.3
. The principal part involving
P
is controlled in the harmonic-pressure quotient. On the cutoff layer, the local Calderon–Zygmund part of
P
is generated by
u_{h}\otimes u_{h}
, so the same commutator estimate and
Lemma

A.5
give an
O(\ell^{1/6})
bound. The remaining spatially harmonic part satisfies the interior estimate

\|P_{\rm harm}-S_{\ell}P_{\rm harm}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell.

Thus

\|J_{h,{\rm pr}}^{\ell}\|_{L^{1}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

Since
\phi
is fixed and supported away from the boundary of
Q_{*}
, this proves (
A.41
) for the cutoff part.

We now estimate the
\delta
-small terms. The vertical transport contribution obeys

\|\partial_{3}S_{\ell}(u_{3}u_{h})\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C\ell^{-1}\|u_{3}u_{h}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{*})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3}.

The pressure remainder satisfies

\|p^{\rm rem}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{*})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\delta^{1/3}

by
Lemma

A.3
, and the weighted pressure compatibility estimate
Lemma

A.4
places the corresponding horizontal pressure defect in the same finite-power class after smoothing. Therefore all vertical-transport and pressure-remainder terms are bounded by

C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}

after increasing
N
and decreasing
b
.

It remains to replace the uncorrected field by the prepared solenoidal field. Since

U_{h}^{\ell}=\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}+B_{h}^{\ell},

we have

-\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}\bar{U}_{h}^{\ell}=-\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}U_{h}^{\ell}+\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}B_{h}^{\ell}.

By
Lemma

A.6
,

\|\nabla_{h}B_{h}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{1/3},

and by (
A.36
),

\int_{Q^{\prime}}|\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}B_{h}^{\ell}|\,dx\,dt\leq\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q^{\prime})}\|\nabla_{h}B_{h}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q^{\prime})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

This term is absorbed into
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\ell}
. Combining the preceding estimates yields (
A.40
), (
A.41
), and (
A.42
).
∎

Remark A.10

(Sign convention)
.

The positive covariance convention (
A.33
) gives the main term

-\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}U_{h}^{\ell}

in the variance identity. If one writes the Reynolds stress as
R^{\ell}=-\tau^{\ell}
, the same term becomes
+R^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}U_{h}^{\ell}
. The relative entropy argument below uses the positive covariance convention throughout.

A.6.3
Conditional variance-buffered relative entropy

We now record the precise way in which the localized Onsager identity feeds into the logarithmic projection problem. This is a conditional stability statement, in the same broad family as weak–strong comparison arguments used in recent epsilon-regularity work
[
38
]
: once a strict rough shadow has been selected with a good-time relative-energy preparation and a buffered gradient bound, the Reynolds covariance contributes additively and the remaining small-component residuals may be propagated through the high factor.

Let

Q_{\theta/4}\Subset Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}\Subset Q_{\rm prep}\Subset Q_{3/4}

be fixed interior cylinders. Assume that the prepared field satisfies the covariance-form equation

\partial_{t}U_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta U_{h}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(U_{h}^{\ell}\otimes U_{h}^{\ell})+\nabla_{h}P^{\ell}=-\nabla_{h}\!\cdot\tau^{\ell}+G_{\delta}^{\ell},\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot U_{h}^{\ell}=0,

(A.47)

where

\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\rm prep})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6},\qquad\|G_{\delta}^{\ell}\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(Q_{\rm prep})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(A.48)

Here
G_{\delta}^{\ell}
contains vertical transport, the horizontal correction, localization, and the strict pressure-compatibility defect. The norm
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
is an energy-dual residual norm adapted to the cutoff argument.

Hypothesis A.1

(Localized solenoidal testing and pressure-cutoff admissibility)
.

For every cutoff
\phi
used in the variance-buffered relative entropy estimate, the localized comparison may be tested in a pressure-free horizontal solenoidal form. More precisely, for every horizontally divergence-free difference field
W_{h}
there is, on each fixed horizontal product chart, a linear correction
\mathcal{B}_{\phi}[W_{h}]
such that

\Phi_{\phi}[W_{h}]:=\phi W_{h}+\mathcal{B}_{\phi}[W_{h}],\qquad\nabla_{h}\cdot\Phi_{\phi}[W_{h}]=0,

\mathcal{B}_{\phi}[W_{h}]
is supported in the cutoff layer, and the standard horizontal Bogovskii bounds hold on the fixed chart. Equivalently, the pressure-cutoff contribution generated by replacing the naive test
\phi W_{h}
with
\Phi_{\phi}[W_{h}]
is admissible in the following integrated form: for every comparison interval
(s,0)
,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{0}|\mathcal{E}_{\rm pc}^{\ell}(t)|\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{s}^{0}\int\phi|\nabla W^{\ell}|^{2}\,dx\,dt+C_{\varepsilon,\phi}\int_{s}^{0}H_{\phi}^{\ell}(t)\,dt

(A.49)

\displaystyle+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

The constants depend only on the fixed chart, the cutoff family,
M
, and
\theta
, apart from the displayed
\varepsilon
-dependence. In smooth product charts this is the standard localized horizontal Bogovskii cancellation of the pressure term; for suitable weak solutions it is part of the prepared pressure-covariance closure and is not asserted unconditionally from the spatial mollification alone.

Lemma A.9

(Energy-to-
L^{3}
interpolation on fixed cylinders)
.

Let
Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}
be fixed interior cylinders and let
W
be supported after the usual cutoff localization. If

\sup_{t}\|W(t)\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm sh})}^{2}+\int_{Q_{\rm sh}}|\nabla W|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq R,\qquad 0<R\leq 1,

then

\|W\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\rm tar})}\leq C_{\theta}R^{1/2}.

Consequently, an energy-level contribution
A_{\ell}
yields an
L^{3}
-contribution
A_{\ell}^{1/2}
, and an energy-level covariance contribution
\ell^{1/6}
yields at least
\ell^{1/12}
after passage to
L^{3}
.

Proof.

The parabolic interpolation inequality in three space dimensions gives

\|W\|_{L^{10/3}(Q_{\rm sh})}\leq C\|W\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(Q_{\rm sh})}^{2/5}\|\nabla W\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\rm sh})}^{3/5}\leq CR^{1/2}.

Since
Q_{\rm tar}
has fixed finite measure and
10/3>3
, Holder’s inequality gives

\|W\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\rm tar})}\leq C_{\theta}\|W\|_{L^{10/3}(Q_{\rm sh})}\leq C_{\theta}R^{1/2}.

The two stated consequences are obtained by substituting
R=A_{\ell}
and
R=\ell^{1/6}
, respectively.
∎

Proposition A.4

(Variance-buffered reduction to the log-compatible projection)
.

Assume (
A.47
), (
A.48
), the localized Onsager identity of
Lemma

A.8
, and the localized solenoidal-testing/pressure-cutoff admissibility of
A.1
. Suppose that there exists a strict limiting shadow
(V,Q)
on
Q_{\rm sh}
,

V=(V_{h},0),\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot V_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}Q=0,

\partial_{t}V_{h}-\Delta V_{h}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(V_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}Q=0,

(A.50)

with the buffered gradient bound

\int_{s}^{0}\|\nabla_{h}V(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rm sh})}\,dt\leq C(M,\theta),

(A.51)

and with the good-time preparation at level
A_{\ell}\in(0,1]
,

\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|U^{\ell}(s)-V(s)|^{2}\,dx+\int\phi\kappa^{\ell}(s)\,dx\leq A_{\ell}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(A.52)

Then there exist exponents
a_{E}>0
and
a_{\rm cov}\in(0,1/6]
, depending only on the fixed interpolation exponents, such that

\|U^{\ell}-V\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}\leq C_{M,\theta}A_{\ell}^{a_{E}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{\rm cov}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

(A.53)

If, in addition, the local pressure reconstruction estimate of
Proposition

A.5
holds, then there is
h^{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}(Q_{\theta/4})
such that

\displaystyle\|U^{\ell}-V\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}+\|P^{\ell}-Q-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}

(A.54)

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\theta}A_{\ell}^{a_{E}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{\rm cov}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

Thus the separated log-compatible estimate follows from the localized Onsager identity, the buffered rough-shadow bound, pressure reconstruction, and a subcritical good-time preparation of the selected strict shadow.

Proof.

Set

W^{\ell}=U^{\ell}-V.

The naive localized test
\phi W^{\ell}
is not horizontally divergence-free, because
\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(\phi W^{\ell})=W^{\ell}\cdot\nabla_{h}\phi
. We therefore do not use it directly. By
A.1
, replace it by the horizontal solenoidal localized test

\Phi^{\ell}:=\Phi_{\phi}[W_{h}^{\ell}]=\phi W_{h}^{\ell}+\mathcal{B}_{\phi}[W_{h}^{\ell}],\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot\Phi^{\ell}=0.

The pressure term
\nabla_{h}(P^{\ell}-Q)
then pairs to zero. The terms produced by the Bogovskii correction and by commuting the cutoff through the localized comparison are supported in the buffer layer; they are denoted by
\mathcal{E}_{\rm pc}^{\ell}
and are controlled by (
A.49
). With this pressure-free localization understood, the principal nonlinear term is the same as in the formal test by
\phi W^{\ell}
. Using
\nabla_{h}\!\cdot U_{h}^{\ell}=\nabla_{h}\!\cdot V_{h}=0
, it is bounded by

\left|\int\phi\,(W^{\ell}\cdot\nabla_{h}V)\cdot W^{\ell}\,dx\right|\leq\|\nabla_{h}V\|_{L^{\infty}}\int\phi|W^{\ell}|^{2}\,dx+\mathcal{C}_{\rm cut}^{\ell}(t).

(A.55)

The covariance term produced by
-\nabla_{h}\!\cdot\tau^{\ell}
is

\int\phi\,\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}W^{\ell}\,dx=\int\phi\,\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}U_{h}^{\ell}\,dx-\int\phi\,\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}V\,dx.

The first term on the right is exactly cancelled by adding the localized Onsager identity (
A.40
). Define the variance-corrected relative energy

H_{\phi}^{\ell}(t)=\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|W^{\ell}(x,t)|^{2}\,dx+\int\phi\kappa^{\ell}(x,t)\,dx.

(A.56)

After the cancellation, one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}H_{\phi}^{\ell}(t)+c\int\phi|\nabla W^{\ell}|^{2}\,dx+\int\phi D_{h}^{\ell}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla_{h}V\|_{L^{\infty}}H_{\phi}^{\ell}(t)

(A.57)

\displaystyle+\left|\langle G_{\delta}^{\ell},\Phi^{\ell}\rangle\right|+|\mathcal{E}_{\rm cut}^{\ell}(t)|+|\mathcal{E}_{\rm pc}^{\ell}(t)|+|\mathcal{E}_{\delta}^{\ell}(t)|.

Indeed, since
\tau^{\ell}\geq 0
,

\left|\int\phi\,\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}V\,dx\right|\leq\|\nabla_{h}V\|_{L^{\infty}}\int\phi\operatorname{tr}\tau^{\ell}\,dx=2\|\nabla_{h}V\|_{L^{\infty}}\int\phi\kappa^{\ell}\,dx.

The residual term is estimated in the energy-dual norm by Young’s inequality:

|\langle G_{\delta}^{\ell},\Phi^{\ell}\rangle|\leq\frac{c}{2}\int\phi|\nabla W^{\ell}|^{2}\,dx+C\|G_{\delta}^{\ell}\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}^{2}.

Using (
A.48
), (
A.41
), (
A.49
), and (
A.42
), choosing
\varepsilon
small in (
A.49
), and increasing
N
and decreasing
b
if necessary, (
A.57
) gives

\frac{d}{dt}H_{\phi}^{\ell}(t)+c\int\phi|\nabla W^{\ell}|^{2}\,dx\leq C\|\nabla_{h}V\|_{L^{\infty}}H_{\phi}^{\ell}(t)+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}

(A.58)

in the integrated-in-time sense. Gronwall’s inequality, (
A.51
), and (
A.52
) imply

\sup_{s<t<0}H_{\phi}^{\ell}(t)+\int_{s}^{0}\int\phi|\nabla W^{\ell}|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}A_{\ell}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(A.59)

In the subsequent localized projection or smooth-shadow step, the genuinely one-component residuals may pass through a finite-power stability constant and a factor
e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}
. The covariance contribution does not pass through that factor because it has already been absorbed into
H_{\phi}^{\ell}
. Thus the conservative form of (
A.59
) is

\sup_{s<t<0}H_{\phi}^{\ell}(t)+\int_{s}^{0}\int\phi|\nabla W^{\ell}|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}A_{\ell}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

(A.60)

Applying the explicit interpolation lemma
Lemma

A.9
to (
A.60
) gives, on the fixed target cylinder,

\|W^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\left(A_{\ell}+\ell^{1/6}+\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}\right)^{1/2}.

The first two terms therefore contribute, for instance,
A_{\ell}^{1/2}
and
\ell^{1/12}
. The last term retains the separated logarithmic form after renaming the finite loss and the small exponent, since

\left(\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}\right)^{1/2}\leq\ell^{-N^{\prime}}e^{C^{\prime}_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N^{\prime}}}\delta^{b^{\prime}}

for suitable positive
N^{\prime},b^{\prime}
. Thus one may take explicit admissible choices such as
a_{E}=1/2
and
a_{\rm cov}=1/12
, or smaller positive values if later localization losses are absorbed. This proves (
A.53
).

The pressure estimate follows from
Proposition

A.5
. Indeed,

\|P^{\ell}-Q-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}\leq C_{M,\theta}\|U^{\ell}-V\|_{L^{3}}+C_{M,\theta}\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

Using (
A.53
), (
A.36
), and
\ell^{1/6}\leq\ell^{a_{\rm cov}}
for
0<\ell<1
after decreasing
a_{\rm cov}\leq 1/6
, gives (
A.54
).
∎

Remark A.11

(Scope and remaining selection input)
.

Proposition

A.4
does not construct the strict shadow and does not prove the good-time preparation (
A.52
). It shows that, once such a strict rough shadow is available, the covariance part of the error contributes additively. The buffered gradient estimate needed in the Gronwall argument is supplied later by
Lemma

A.10
for any strict shadow with the appropriate local bound. The remaining input is therefore the quantitative selection of a strict shadow satisfying the subcritical good-time variance-corrected preparation.

A.6.4
The smooth shadow and the high exponential factor

The proposition above compares the prepared coarse field with a rough strict shadow. To enter the limiting class used in the harmonic-pressure excess, one may still introduce a smooth shadow
V^{\ell}
satisfying

\partial_{t}V^{\ell}-\Delta V^{\ell}+(V^{\ell}\cdot\nabla_{h})V^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}Q^{\ell}=0,\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot V^{\ell}=0.

(A.61)

The usual two-shadow identity is

(V\cdot\nabla_{h})V-(V^{\ell}\cdot\nabla_{h})V^{\ell}=(V^{\ell}\cdot\nabla_{h})(V-V^{\ell})+((V-V^{\ell})\cdot\nabla_{h})V.

(A.62)

When (
A.62
) is tested by
V-V^{\ell}
, the first term cancels and the coefficient left in the energy inequality is
\nabla_{h}V
, not
\nabla_{h}V^{\ell}
. This is the second cancellation needed in the logarithmic program.

The residuals genuinely involving
u_{3}
may still be propagated through a smooth-shadow or localized weak–strong estimate whose constants lose finite powers of
\ell^{-1}
and may contain an exponential
e^{C\ell^{-N}}
. The variance-buffered estimate ensures that the Reynolds commutator does not enter that exponential.

A.6.5
Pressure reconstruction after variance-buffered comparison

Proposition A.5

(Harmonic pressure reconstruction)
.

Assume that the variance-buffered comparison gives, on an interior target cylinder,

\|U^{\ell}-v^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}}\leq A_{\ell}+A_{\delta},

where
A_{\ell}
is the additive covariance contribution and
A_{\delta}
contains the residuals carrying a positive power of
\delta
. Suppose also that the coarse pressure
P^{\ell}
and the limiting pressure
q^{\ell}
are represented by local Calderon–Zygmund formulas associated with
U^{\ell}
and
v^{\ell}
, and that the Reynolds stress satisfies

\|\widetilde{R}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}.

Then there exists a spatially harmonic function
h^{\ell}
such that

\|P^{\ell}-q^{\ell}-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}\leq C_{M,\theta}(A_{\ell}+A_{\delta})+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}.

(A.63)

Proof.

In an interior cylinder, write

P^{\ell}=\mathcal{R}_{a}\mathcal{R}_{b}(U_{a}^{\ell}U_{b}^{\ell}-\widetilde{R}_{ab}^{\ell})+h_{U},\qquad q^{\ell}=\mathcal{R}_{a}\mathcal{R}_{b}(v_{a}^{\ell}v_{b}^{\ell})+h_{v},

where
a,b\in\{1,2\}
, and where
h_{U},h_{v}
are spatially harmonic. Let
h^{\ell}=h_{U}-h_{v}
. Calderon–Zygmund estimates and Hölder’s inequality give

\displaystyle\|P^{\ell}-q^{\ell}-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|U^{\ell}\otimes U^{\ell}-v^{\ell}\otimes v^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}+C\|\widetilde{R}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|U^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}}+\|v^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}})\|U^{\ell}-v^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}}+C\ell^{1/6}.

The local
L^{3}
-bounds are controlled by the prepared scale-invariant size, so (
A.63
) follows.
∎

A.6.6
Why a bare Stokes stress removal lemma is not enough

A tempting alternative is to solve

\partial_{t}Z^{\ell}-\Delta Z^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\pi^{\ell}=\nabla_{h}\!\cdot R^{\ell},\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot Z^{\ell}=0,

and try to prove
\|Z^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}}\lesssim\|R^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}
. This estimate is false at the level of parabolic scaling: an order-one parabolic potential maps
L^{15/8}_{t,x}
to
L^{3}_{t,x}
, while
L^{3/2}_{t,x}
is insufficient. Thus one would need an additional estimate such as

\|R^{\ell}\|_{L^{15/8}_{t,x}}\lesssim\ell^{a}.

The energy-class commutator structure does not give such an estimate. What it gives is an
L^{3/2}
-small pressure contribution and an unresolved variance. The variance-buffered relative energy above uses exactly this structure and avoids the false Stokes-potential upgrade.

A.7
From residual splitting to a conditional prepared estimate

The estimates above identify the mechanism by which a logarithmic rate would follow. The Reynolds commutator is no longer treated as an arbitrary forcing; it is an unresolved covariance and contributes through the variance-corrected relative energy. The residuals generated by the vertical component, the horizontal solenoidal correction, localization, and pressure compatibility remain in a finite-power class of the form
\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}
.

There is, however, one further quantitative input that is not supplied by the compactness modulus of
Proposition

A.1
. One has to choose a strict limiting shadow with a good-time variance-corrected relative-energy preparation at a finite positive power of the coarse scale. The second part of this work refines the following subcritical form and studies its geometric content.

Intermediate Target A.2

(Subcritical quantitative strict shadow selection)
.

Let
U^{\ell}
be the prepared solenoidal field from
Proposition

A.3
, with associated prepared pressure
P^{\ell}
. Fix an interior chain of cylinders

Q_{\theta/4}\Subset Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}\Subset Q_{\rm prep}\Subset Q_{3/4}.

Assume that the solenoidal residual equation of
Proposition

A.3
holds on
Q_{\rm prep}
, and that the prepared pressure satisfies the strict-compatibility estimate

\|\partial_{3}P^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}_{t}W^{-1,3/2}_{x}(Q_{\rm prep})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(A.64)

The subcritical quantitative strict shadow selection principle asserts that there exist an exponent

0<\mu<\frac{1}{6},

a strict limiting-system pair

(V^{\ell},Q^{\ell})\in\mathcal{L}_{M}(Q_{\rm sh}),

and a good time
s_{\ell}
in the lower buffer layer such that, for a cutoff
\phi
supported in
Q_{\rm sh}
and equal to one on
Q_{\rm tar}
,

\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|U^{\ell}(s_{\ell})-V^{\ell}(s_{\ell})|^{2}\,dx+\int\phi\kappa^{\ell}(s_{\ell})\,dx\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(A.65)

The pair
(V^{\ell},Q^{\ell})
is required to be obtained by a localized strict harmonic projection in the pressure quotient. No exact trace condition
V^{\ell}(s_{\ell})=U^{\ell}(s_{\ell})
is imposed.

Remark A.12

(Status of the selection principle)
.

A.2
is the remaining quantitative input in the Appendix
A
logarithmic program. The compactness-level projection of
Propositions

A.1
and
A.1
gives a strict comparison object with an abstract modulus in
L^{3}\times L^{3/2}
, but it does not provide the good-time
L^{2}
variance-corrected preparation (
A.65
) with an explicit subcritical finite-power rate. Appendix
B
proves the corresponding abstract-modulus good-time statement and reduces this finite-power rate to singular-stratum strict curve selection.

Proposition A.6

(Variance-buffered prepared estimate under strict shadow selection)
.

Assume
A.2
, and let
a_{E}>0
and
a_{\rm cov}\in(0,1/6]
be the exponents from
Proposition

A.4
. Set

a_{*}:=\min\left\{a_{\rm cov},\mu a_{E}\right\}>0.

Then there exists a spatially harmonic function
h^{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}(Q_{\theta/4})
such that

\displaystyle\|U^{\ell}-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}+\|P^{\ell}-Q^{\ell}-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}

(A.66)

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{*}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

Moreover, the Reynolds contribution is additive: the covariance term
C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}
, which is absorbed into
C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{*}}
, is not multiplied by the high factor
e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}
.

Proof.

The prepared coarse equation is written in the positive covariance convention as

\partial_{t}U_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta U_{h}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(U_{h}^{\ell}\otimes U_{h}^{\ell})+\nabla_{h}P^{\ell}=-\nabla_{h}\!\cdot\tau^{\ell}+G_{\delta}^{\ell},\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot U_{h}^{\ell}=0,

with

\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\rm prep})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6},\qquad\|G_{\delta}^{\ell}\|_{Z^{\prime}(Q_{\rm prep})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

The second estimate collects the vertical transport terms, the horizontal solenoidal correction, localization, and the strict pressure-compatibility defect (
A.64
).

By
Lemma

A.10
, the selected strict shadow satisfies

\int_{s_{\ell}}^{0}\|\nabla_{h}V^{\ell}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rm sh})}\,dt\leq C(M,\theta).

(A.67)

The good-time preparation is exactly (
A.65
). Applying the conditional variance-buffered stability estimate of
Proposition

A.4
with
A_{\ell}=C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}
gives

\|U^{\ell}-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{*}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

(A.68)

The covariance contribution has already been absorbed into the variance-corrected relative energy. Hence it contributes through
\kappa^{\ell}
and
\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}
, and it is not propagated by the high factor; after the definition of
a_{*}
, it is included in the additive term
C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{*}}
.

Finally,
Proposition

A.5
gives a spatially harmonic correction
h^{\ell}
such that

\|P^{\ell}-Q^{\ell}-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\|U^{\ell}-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}+C_{M,\theta}\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\rm prep})}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

Together with (
A.68
), (
A.36
), and
\ell^{1/6}\leq\ell^{a_{\rm cov}}\leq\ell^{a_{*}}
for
0<\ell<1
, this proves (
A.66
).
∎

Hypothesis A.2

(Prepared pressure closure)
.

For the solenoidal prepared pair
(U^{\ell},P^{\ell})
on the inner comparison cylinder there exists a spatially harmonic correction
h_{\rm prep}^{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}(Q_{\theta/4})
such that

\|p-P^{\ell}-h_{\rm prep}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\bigl(\ell^{1/6}+\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}\bigr).

(A.69)

Moreover the same
P^{\ell}
is the pressure appearing in the covariance-form equation and satisfies the vertical compatibility estimate required in (
A.64
), and the solenoidal correction satisfies the residual admissibility condition of
Definition

A.1
. This hypothesis is the precise pressure–residual package needed to pass from the prepared comparison pair back to the original Navier–Stokes pressure; the preceding pressure decomposition proves the small
u_{3}
-generated remainder but does not by itself identify
P
and
P^{\ell}
, or control the time derivative of the spatial Helmholtz correction, without this closure.

Remark A.13

(Status of the prepared pressure package)
.

The estimates in
Section

A.3
prove the small Calderon–Zygmund pressure remainder generated by terms containing
u_{3}
. They do not, by themselves, prove that one and the same scalar
P^{\ell}
simultaneously serves as the pressure in the covariance-form equation, satisfies the vertical compatibility estimate, reconstructs the original Navier–Stokes pressure modulo
\mathcal{H}
with the bound (
A.69
), and controls the linear residual of the solenoidal Helmholtz correction in the sense of (
A.31
). These requirements are bundled in
A.2
and are part of the structural inputs of the main theorem.

Proposition A.7

(Conditional prepared harmonic approximation)
.

Assume the subcritical quantitative strict shadow selection principle
A.2
and the prepared pressure closure
A.2
. Then the prepared logarithmic shadow estimate in
A.1
holds with
a=a_{*}
, after possibly decreasing
b
and increasing
N
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

A.6
,

\|u-U^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}\leq C_{M,\theta}(\ell^{1/6}+\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3}).

By the prepared pressure closure
A.2
, there exists
h_{\rm prep}^{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}(Q_{\theta/4})
such that

\|p-P^{\ell}-h_{\rm prep}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\bigl(\ell^{1/6}+\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}\bigr).

Combining these estimates with (
A.66
), replacing the harmonic correction by
h^{\ell}+h_{\rm prep}^{\ell}
, and decreasing the exponent
b
if necessary, yields

\displaystyle\|u-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}+\|p-Q^{\ell}-h^{\ell}-h_{\rm prep}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{*}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

Let

R_{\ell}:=C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{*}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

The last display says that the
L^{3}
-norm and the
L^{3/2}
-norm are bounded by
R_{\ell}
. Therefore the fixed-scale excess is bounded by

C_{\theta}\bigl(R_{\ell}^{3}+R_{\ell}^{3/2}\bigr).

For
q\in\{3/2,3\}
, the elementary inequality

(A+B)^{q}\leq C_{q}(A^{q}+B^{q})

and the bounds
0<\ell<1
,
0<\delta\leq 1
show that
R_{\ell}^{q}
is again bounded by a separated expression of the same form, after replacing
a_{*},b,N,C_{M,\theta}
by harmless positive constants. After multiplying by the fixed scale factor
\theta^{-2}
, this gives (
A.14
).
∎

A.8
The limiting class and decay

For completeness we record the role of the strict limiting system. The vertical vorticity

\omega=\partial_{1}v_{2}-\partial_{2}v_{1}

solves

\partial_{t}\omega-\Delta\omega+v_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h}\omega=0,

which has no vortex-stretching term. This no-stretching structure explains why the strict class is expected to have quantitative interior smoothing, paralleling the role of two-dimensional and anisotropic structures in one-component regularity criteria
[
20
,
21
,
30
,
37
]
. In the final theorem the corresponding quantitative smoothing/decay package is kept as a structural input. Once local boundedness is available, the CKN quantity decays.

Proposition A.8

(Limiting-class decay under the strict smoothing package)
.

Let
(v,q)
be a strict limiting-system suitable solution in
Q_{1/2}
satisfying
\Phi_{v}(1/2)\leq M
. Assume the strict smoothing package gives the local boundedness and the at-least-linear interior pressure-oscillation decay needed below, with constants depending only on
M
. Equivalently, on a smaller fixed interior cylinder one may assume

\|v\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla q\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(M),

up to replacing this displayed bound by any standard pressure-oscillation estimate implying
D_{q}(r)\leq C(M)r
. Then there exist
K_{\mathrm{LS}}(M)\geq 1
and
r_{\mathrm{LS}}(M)>0
such that

\Psi_{v}(r)=C_{v}(r)+D_{q}(r)\leq K_{\mathrm{LS}}(M)r,\qquad 0<r<r_{\mathrm{LS}}(M).

Proof.

Under the strict smoothing package,
v
is locally bounded in an interior cylinder, with constants depending on
M
. Then

C_{v}(r)=r^{-2}\int_{Q_{r}}|v|^{3}\,dx\,dt\leq C(M)r^{3}\leq C(M)r.

For the pressure, decompose

q=q_{\rm loc}+q_{\rm harm},\qquad q_{\rm loc}=\mathcal{R}_{a}\mathcal{R}_{b}(\chi v_{a}v_{b}),\qquad a,b\in\{1,2\}.

The pressure part is covered by the pressure-oscillation component of the strict smoothing package. For example, if the displayed local
L^{\infty}
bound on
\nabla q
is available, then

|q(x,t)-(q)_{B_{r}}(t)|\leq C(M)r

for
x\in B_{r}
, and hence

D_{q}(r)=r^{-2}\int_{Q_{r}}|q-(q)_{B_{r}}(t)|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt\leq C(M)r^{9/2}\leq C(M)r

for
0<r<1
. The same conclusion follows from any equivalent at-least-linear pressure-oscillation estimate included in the strict package. Combining the velocity and pressure bounds proves the result.
∎

Lemma A.10

(Buffered strict two-and-a-half-dimensional smoothing)
.

Let

Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}\Subset Q_{\rm buf}\Subset Q_{\rm prep}

be a fixed interior chain of cylinders. Let
(V,Q)
be a strict limiting-system solution in
Q_{\rm prep}
, namely

V=(V_{h},0),\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot V_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}Q=0,

and

\partial_{t}V_{h}-\Delta V_{h}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(V_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}Q=0\quad\text{in }Q_{\rm prep}.

Assume that

\Phi_{V}(Q_{\rm prep})\leq M.

Assume, in addition, the strict limiting smoothing package of
1.1
on this fixed cylinder chain; equivalently, the local boundedness, pressure-oscillation control, and scalar-vorticity smoothing estimates used below are available with constants depending only on
M
and
\theta
. Let
s
be a good time in the lower buffer layer, chosen so that

\|\nabla V(s)\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm prep})}^{2}\leq C(M,\theta).

(A.70)

Then

\int_{s}^{0}\|\nabla_{h}V(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rm tar})}\,dt\leq C(M,\theta).

(A.71)

After shrinking the cylinder chain, the same estimate holds with
B_{\rm tar}
replaced by the spatial ball denoted
B_{\rm sh}
in the shadow comparison argument.

Proof.

Set

\omega=\partial_{1}V_{2}-\partial_{2}V_{1}.

Taking the horizontal curl of the strict limiting system gives

\partial_{t}\omega-\Delta\omega+V_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h}\omega=0.

(A.72)

The stretching term is absent. The proof consists of a scalar smoothing step for (
A.72
) and a horizontal div–curl recovery step.

First, by the strict limiting smoothing package just assumed, equivalently by the local boundedness component of
1.1
together with the standard interior bootstrap, the scale-invariant bound on
V
gives

\|V_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\rm buf})}\leq C(M,\theta).

(A.73)

Moreover, (
A.70
) and
|\omega|\leq|\nabla V|
imply

\|\omega(s)\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm prep})}\leq C(M,\theta).

(A.74)

Since
V_{h}
is bounded on
Q_{\rm buf}
and
\nabla_{h}\!\cdot V_{h}=0
, (
A.72
) is a uniformly parabolic scalar drift–diffusion equation with bounded divergence-free drift. The local De Giorgi–Nash–Moser estimate and the interior parabolic Holder estimate for bounded drift give horizontal Holder smoothing. More precisely, there exist
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
and

\gamma=\frac{3}{4}+\frac{\alpha}{2}<1

such that, for
s<t<0
,

\|\omega(t)\|_{C_{h}^{\alpha}(B_{\rm sh})}\leq C(M,\theta)\bigl(1+(t-s)^{-\gamma}\bigr).

(A.75)

Here
C_{h}^{\alpha}
denotes the Holder norm in the horizontal variables. The exponent is consistent with the heat scaling: the
L^{2}
–to–
L^{\infty}
smoothing in three space dimensions gives a factor
(t-s)^{-3/4}
, and the local Holder seminorm on a cylinder of radius comparable to
(t-s)^{1/2}
costs an additional factor
(t-s)^{-\alpha/2}
. Choosing
\alpha<1/2
makes
\gamma<1
.

We now recover
\nabla_{h}V_{h}
from the horizontal div–curl system. For each fixed
(x_{3},t)
,

\nabla_{h}\!\cdot V_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{1}V_{2}-\partial_{2}V_{1}=\omega.

Let
D_{\rm tar}\Subset D_{\rm sh}
be the horizontal projections of
B_{\rm tar}\Subset B_{\rm sh}
. The local two-dimensional div–curl Schauder estimate yields

\|\nabla_{h}V_{h}(\cdot,x_{3},t)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\rm tar})}\leq C\left(\|V_{h}(\cdot,x_{3},t)\|_{L^{\infty}(D_{\rm sh})}+\|\omega(\cdot,x_{3},t)\|_{C^{\alpha}(D_{\rm sh})}\right).

(A.76)

Indeed, after multiplying by a horizontal cutoff equal to one on
D_{\rm tar}
, one solves the corresponding local stream-function equation and applies the two-dimensional Schauder estimate; the cutoff terms are controlled by the local
L^{\infty}
bound for
V_{h}
.

Taking the supremum over
x_{3}
and using (
A.73
) and (
A.75
), we obtain

\|\nabla_{h}V_{h}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rm tar})}\leq C(M,\theta)\bigl(1+(t-s)^{-\gamma}\bigr),\qquad\gamma<1.

(A.77)

Since
V=(V_{h},0)
, integrating (
A.77
) in time gives

\int_{s}^{0}\|\nabla_{h}V(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rm tar})}\,dt\leq C(M,\theta)\int_{s}^{0}\bigl(1+(t-s)^{-\gamma}\bigr)\,dt\leq C(M,\theta),

because
\gamma<1
. This proves (
A.71
).
∎

A.9
Role in the final dependency chain

The preceding sections give the closing summary of the first-stage program. They separate the logarithmic approximation mechanism into proved components and one remaining quantitative selection principle. The terminology in this section is deliberately global: the issue is not the existence of a strict comparison object, which follows from compactness, but the selection of such an object with a good-time relative-energy preparation at an explicit finite-power scale.

A.9.1
Established and conditional components

The following ingredients have been established in the preceding sections.

First,
Lemma

A.1
gives the qualitative strict projection for genuine Navier–Stokes solutions, and
Lemma

A.2
gives its localized rough-shadow analogue. The finite-scale consequence is the compactness-level strict projection modulus in
Proposition

A.1
. Applied to prepared coarse trajectories, it gives

\displaystyle\dist_{\rm har}\bigl((U^{\ell},P^{\ell}),\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}(Q_{\theta/4})\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\Omega_{M,\theta}\left(C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}\right),

provided that the prepared pressure has the corresponding vertical compatibility defect. This result gives the legitimacy of strict shadows in the harmonic-pressure quotient, with an abstract modulus.

Second,
Lemmas

A.6
,
A.2
and
A.3
provide the low-frequency horizontal preparation and residual splitting. The non-small residual is the Reynolds commutator of size
O(\ell^{1/6})
; the remaining residuals carry a positive power of
\delta=C_{3}(1)
, at the cost of finite powers of
\ell^{-1}
.

Third,
Lemma

A.8
proves the localized Onsager variance identity. With the positive covariance convention

\tau^{\ell}=S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})-S_{\ell}u_{h}\otimes S_{\ell}u_{h}\geq 0,\qquad\kappa^{\ell}=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\tau^{\ell},

the dangerous term involving
\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}U_{h}^{\ell}
is absorbed into the time derivative of the unresolved variance. This is the algebraic reason why the Reynolds commutator can contribute additively.

Fourth,
Proposition

A.4
proves a conditional relative entropy estimate: if a strict shadow has already been selected with a good-time preparation and a buffered gradient bound, then the separated velocity estimate follows.
Lemma

A.10
supplies the buffered gradient bound for any strict shadow with the appropriate local bound, using the no-stretching vorticity equation, local Hölder smoothing, and horizontal div–curl Schauder recovery.
Proposition

A.5
then reconstructs the pressure in the harmonic quotient.

A.9.2
Remaining quantitative input

The remaining unproved input in Appendix
A
is
A.2
, the subcritical quantitative strict shadow selection principle. It asks for a strict limiting pair
(V^{\ell},Q^{\ell})
and a good time
s_{\ell}
such that

\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|U^{\ell}(s_{\ell})-V^{\ell}(s_{\ell})|^{2}\,dx+\int\phi\kappa^{\ell}(s_{\ell})\,dx\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b},\qquad 0<\mu<\frac{1}{6}.

This estimate is stronger than the compactness-level harmonic projection modulus but weaker than the earlier
\ell^{2}
target. The compactness-level result gives closeness in an integrated
L^{3}\times L^{3/2}
topology with an abstract modulus; it does not automatically provide an explicit good-time
L^{2}
variance-corrected preparation. Appendix
B
proves the corresponding abstract-modulus good-time selection, introduces the thick good-time refinement, and reduces the finite-power estimate above to singular-stratum strict curve selection. Appendix
C
is the geometric continuation of this reduction: it studies when the blow-up direction belongs to the integrable tangent cone of the strict trace class.

A.9.3
Conditional logarithmic consequence

Conditional on
A.2
,
Proposition

A.6
gives the separated prepared estimate

\displaystyle\|U^{\ell}-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}+\|P^{\ell}-Q^{\ell}-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{*}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b},\qquad a_{*}>0.

The Reynolds commutator appears only through an additive finite power of
\ell
. The high exponential factor is reserved for residuals that carry a positive power of the small component. Consequently, under the selection principle,
Proposition

A.7
verifies
A.1
; then
Theorems

A.2
and
A.3
give the logarithmic harmonic-pressure approximation, logarithmic CKN decay, and logarithmic regularity-radius lower bound.

A.10
Conclusion

This appendix isolates a concrete reduction toward a logarithmic one-component approximation theorem. The components proved here are the low-frequency horizontal preparation, the coarse residual splitting, the compactness-level strict projection in the harmonic-pressure quotient, the localized Onsager variance identity in the smooth or weak-defect-admissible sense, the conditional variance-buffered relative entropy estimate, the buffered smoothing of strict limiting shadows, and the harmonic pressure reconstruction.

The main structural point is the treatment of the Reynolds commutator. By writing

\tau^{\ell}=S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})-S_{\ell}u_{h}\otimes S_{\ell}u_{h}

and adding the unresolved variance

\kappa^{\ell}=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\tau^{\ell}

to the relative energy, the dangerous high-frequency term involving
\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}S_{\ell}u_{h}
is cancelled by the Onsager variance identity. The remaining stress is paired only with the gradient of a strict rough shadow. Thus the Reynolds commutator is structurally compatible with an additive contribution of size
O(\ell^{1/6})
.

The logarithmic theorem is not claimed unconditionally in this form. It is reduced to the subcritical quantitative strict shadow selection principle stated in
A.2
. This principle asks for a strict comparison pair, obtained through the harmonic-pressure projection, that also satisfies an explicit good-time variance-corrected relative-energy preparation of size
\ell^{\mu}+\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}
,
0<\mu<1/6
. Appendix
B
shows that this is the correct finite-rate gate and reduces it to singular-stratum strict curve selection for the nonlinear pressure-compatibility constraint. Appendix
C
develops the corresponding singular-stratum analysis, while keeping the remaining moving-base trace-flatness and majorant inputs explicit. Once the required strict curve-selection input is supplied, the logarithmic harmonic-pressure approximation and the logarithmic lower bound for the regularity radius follow by the optimization in
\ell
already recorded in
Theorems

A.2
and
A.3
.

Appendix B
Good-time selection and sharp-branch blow-up

B.1
Introduction

We consider suitable weak solutions of the three-dimensional incompressible Navier–Stokes equations, in the Leray–Hopf framework and the Caffarelli–Kohn–Nirenberg local energy setting
[
1
,
2
,
7
]

\partial_{t}u-\Delta u+(u\cdot\nabla)u+\nabla p=0,\qquad\nabla\cdot u=0,

(B.1)

in the unit parabolic cylinder

Q_{1}=B_{1}(0)\times(-1,0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}.

For
0<r\leq 1
define the scale-invariant quantities

\displaystyle A(r)

\displaystyle=\esssup_{-r^{2}<t<0}\frac{1}{r}\int_{B_{r}}|u(x,t)|^{2}\,dx,

\displaystyle E(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\int_{Q_{r}}|\nabla u|^{2}\,dx\,dt,

\displaystyle C(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{Q_{r}}|u|^{3}\,dx\,dt,

\displaystyle D(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{Q_{r}}|p-(p)_{B_{r}}(t)|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt,

\displaystyle C_{3}(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{Q_{r}}|u_{3}|^{3}\,dx\,dt,

and set

\Phi(r)=A(r)+E(r)+C(r)+D(r),\qquad\Psi(r)=C(r)+D(r).

Motivated by the partial regularity and epsilon-regularity theory
[
7
,
13
,
14
,
23
,
25
,
29
]
, the logarithmic finite-scale program begins from the qualitative theorem

\Phi(1)\leq M,\qquad C_{3}(1)\leq\varepsilon_{*}(M)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad r_{\mathrm{reg}}(0,0)\geq\rho_{*}(M).

In the one-component regularity direction
[
20
,
21
,
30
,
31
,
35
,
37
]
, the proof of this theorem compares the solution with the strict two-and-a-half-dimensional limiting class

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}v_{h}-\Delta v_{h}+(v_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h})v_{h}+\nabla_{h}q=0,\\
\nabla_{h}\cdot v_{h}=0,\\
\partial_{3}q=0,\end{cases}\qquad v=(v_{h},0).

(B.2)

The pressure comparison is made in the quotient by spatially harmonic functions,

p\approx q+h,\qquad\Delta h(\cdot,t)=0,

because local pressure decompositions only compactify the Calderon–Zygmund part strongly
[
8
,
16
,
29
,
34
]
. The harmonic part may oscillate sharply in time while remaining bounded in scale-invariant
L^{3/2}
oscillation.

Appendix
A
of this program developed the low-frequency preparation and variance-buffered stability mechanism. For a smoothing scale
0<\ell\ll 1
, one constructs a horizontally solenoidal field

U^{\ell}=(U_{h}^{\ell},0),\qquad\nabla_{h}\cdot U_{h}^{\ell}=0,

which satisfies the preparation estimate

\|u-U^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}}\leq C_{M,\theta}\bigl(\ell^{1/6}+\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3}\bigr),\qquad\delta=C_{3}(1).

(B.3)

The prepared horizontal field solves, on fixed interior cylinders, a covariance-form approximate strict equation

\partial_{t}U_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta U_{h}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(U_{h}^{\ell}\otimes U_{h}^{\ell})+\nabla_{h}P^{\ell}=-\nabla_{h}\cdot\tau^{\ell}+G_{\delta}^{\ell}.

(B.4)

Here

\tau^{\ell}=S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})-S_{\ell}u_{h}\otimes S_{\ell}u_{h},\qquad\kappa^{\ell}=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\tau^{\ell}.

(B.5)

The tensor
\tau^{\ell}
is nonnegative definite in the horizontal variables, and
\kappa^{\ell}
is the corresponding unresolved variance. One has the estimates

\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6},

(B.6)

\int_{I_{-}}\int\varphi\kappa^{\ell}\,dx\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{2},

(B.7)

and

\|G_{\delta}^{\ell}\|_{Z^{\prime}}+\|\partial_{3}P^{\ell}\|_{Y^{\prime}}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(B.8)

The precise residual norms
Z^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}
are fixed energy-dual norms adapted to the localized weak–strong estimate and the vertical pressure-compatibility estimate; this viewpoint is aligned with recent quantitative and weak–strong approaches to epsilon regularity
[
36
,
38
]
.

The relative entropy argument of Appendix
A
uses the localized Onsager variance identity, in the spirit of the Constantin–E–Titi commutator mechanism
[
11
]
. When the difference equation between
U^{\ell}
and a strict shadow
V
is tested by
U^{\ell}-V
, the term involving
\tau^{\ell}:\nabla_{h}U_{h}^{\ell}
is cancelled by the time derivative of
\int\varphi\kappa^{\ell}
. Thus the correct slice energy is

\mathcal{E}_{\varphi}^{\ell}(s;U^{\ell},V)=\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|U^{\ell}(x,s)-V(x,s)|^{2}\,dx+\int\varphi\kappa^{\ell}(x,s)\,dx.

(B.9)

This appendix focuses on selecting
V
and
s
so that (
B.9
) is small.

The first point of the paper is that the good-time selection must be restricted to times at which the unresolved variance is small. If one minimizes over all
s\in I_{-}
, the average bound (
B.7
) does not prevent a minimizer from occurring at a bad time. We therefore define the good-time set

\mathcal{G}_{\ell}=\left\{s\in I_{-}:\int\varphi\kappa^{\ell}(x,s)\,dx\leq C_{G,M,\theta}\ell^{2}\right\},

(B.10)

where the constant
C_{G,M,\theta}
is chosen large enough so that
\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
has positive measure bounded below independently of
\ell
.

The second point is that the stronger target scale
\ell^{2}
is not needed for the logarithmic theorem. The logarithmic theorem only needs a positive power of
\ell
. We therefore formulate the finite-power good-time selection principle in the subcritical form

\mathcal{E}_{\varphi}^{\ell}(s_{\ell};U^{\ell},V^{\ell})\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b},\qquad 0<\mu<\frac{1}{6}.

(B.11)

The restriction
\mu<1/6
is not cosmetic. It is exactly what makes the normalized cutoff and covariance errors vanish in the blow-up proof. Indeed, if the distance scale
m_{\ell}
satisfies

m_{\ell}\gg\ell^{\mu}+\ell^{-N}\delta^{b},

then (
B.6
) implies

\frac{\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}}{m_{\ell}^{1/2}}\lesssim\frac{\ell^{1/6}}{m_{\ell}^{1/2}}\ll\ell^{1/6-\mu/2}\to 0,

and the localized variance cutoff error of size
O(\ell^{1/6})
satisfies

\frac{\ell^{1/6}}{m_{\ell}}\ll\ell^{1/6-\mu}\to 0.

Thus the subcritical exponent removes the scale mismatch that would arise from insisting on the stronger
\ell^{2}
selection scale with the available covariance estimates.

The results of this appendix are organized as follows.
Section

B.2
records the strict harmonic projection and the prepared covariance-form estimates.
Section

B.3
proves the abstract-modulus covariance-calibrated good-time selection theorem and adds a thick good-time refinement.
Section

B.4
formulates the subcritical finite-power selection principle and records that it is the single remaining gate for the logarithmic theorem.
Section

B.5
proves that this principle implies logarithmic harmonic-pressure approximation and logarithmic CKN decay.
Section

B.6
gives the corrected strict-shadow blow-up argument: the normalized covariance and residual errors vanish at subcritical scales, trace non-loss is obtained from a high-frequency drop argument, and tangent lifting is reformulated as a tangent-cone metric regularity problem for the strict shadow trace manifold.
Section

B.7
distinguishes the unconditional conclusions, the conditional implications, and the remaining geometric-analytic inputs.

B.2
Setup inherited from the analytic preparation appendix

Throughout the paper we fix an interior chain of cylinders

Q_{\theta/4}\Subset Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}\Subset Q_{\rm str}\Subset Q_{\rm prep}\Subset Q_{3/4},\qquad 0<\theta<\frac{1}{2}.

(B.12)

We also fix a cutoff

\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{\rm sh}),\qquad 0\leq\varphi\leq 1,\qquad\varphi\equiv 1\text{ on }Q_{\rm tar}.

(B.13)

The lower time buffer is denoted by
I_{-}
. All constants may depend on
M
,
\theta
, and the cylinder chain.

B.2.1
The strict limiting class and harmonic-pressure quotient

For any interior cylinder
Q_{*}
in the fixed cylinder chain, let
H(Q_{*})
be the class of functions
h\in L^{3/2}(Q_{*})
such that

\Delta h(\cdot,t)=0\quad\text{in the spatial section of }Q_{*}

for a.e. time. Let
L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{*})
be the class of strict limiting-system solutions
(V,Q)
satisfying

V=(V_{h},0),\qquad\nabla_{h}\cdot V_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}Q=0,

(B.14)

and

\partial_{t}V_{h}-\Delta V_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}Q=0

(B.15)

in
Q_{*}
, with a fixed bound

\Phi_{V}(Q_{*})\leq K_{0}(M,\theta).

(B.16)

The harmonic-pressure distance from a pair
(U,P)
to this class is

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{har}}((U,P),L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{*})):=\inf_{(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{*})}\inf_{h\in H(Q_{*})}\Big(

\displaystyle\|U-V\|_{L^{3}(Q_{*})}

(B.17)

\displaystyle+\|P-Q-h\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{*})}\Big).

Proposition B.1

(Strict harmonic projection modulus)
.

Let
(U,P)
be a horizontally solenoidal approximate strict trajectory on
Q_{\rm prep}
satisfying

U=(U_{h},0),\qquad\nabla_{h}\cdot U_{h}=0,

\partial_{t}U_{h}-\Delta U_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(U_{h}\otimes U_{h})+\nabla_{h}P=\nabla_{h}\cdot R+F,

and assume the uniform local energy-pressure bound

\|U\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}+\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}}+\|U\|_{L^{3}}+\|P-(P)_{B}(t)\|_{L^{3/2}}\leq C(M,\theta).

Set

e(U,P;R,F)=\|R\|_{L^{3/2}}+\|F\|_{L_{t}^{3/2}W_{x}^{-1,3/2}}+\|\partial_{3}P\|_{L_{t}^{3/2}W_{x}^{-1,3/2}}.

(B.18)

Then there exists a nondecreasing modulus
\Omega_{\mathrm{str}}
, with

\Omega_{\mathrm{str}}(s)\to 0\quad\text{as }s\downarrow 0,

such that

\operatorname{dist}_{\mathrm{har}}((U,P),L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm sh}))\leq\Omega_{\mathrm{str}}\bigl(e(U,P;R,F)\bigr).

(B.19)

Remark B.1
.

This is the compactness-level strict projection statement, using standard compactness for suitable weak solutions and local pressure decompositions
[
9
,
29
,
34
]
. It gives a strict shadow in the space-time
L^{3}\times L^{3/2}
harmonic-pressure topology. It does not by itself provide a finite-power good-time
L^{2}
preparation.

B.2.2
Prepared covariance-form trajectories

We use the following explicit conditional package, whose low-frequency components are proved in Appendix
A
and whose pressure closure is stated there as
A.2
.

Hypothesis B.1

(Prepared covariance-form pressure package)
.

Let
(u,p)
be a suitable weak solution in
Q_{1}
satisfying

\Phi(1)\leq M,\qquad C_{3}(1)=\delta\leq 1.

For every sufficiently small
0<\ell<\ell_{0}(M,\theta)
there exists a prepared horizontal field
U^{\ell}=(U_{h}^{\ell},0)
and a prepared pressure
P^{\ell}
such that

\nabla_{h}\cdot U_{h}^{\ell}=0\quad\text{in }Q_{\rm prep},

(B.20)

\|u-U^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\rm prep})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\bigl(\ell^{1/6}+\ell^{-1}\delta^{1/3}\bigr),

(B.21)

and there exists a spatially harmonic correction
h_{\rm prep}^{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}(Q_{\rm prep})
such that

\|p-P^{\ell}-h_{\rm prep}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\rm prep})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\bigl(\ell^{1/6}+\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}\bigr).

(B.22)

Moreover,
U^{\ell}
satisfies the covariance-form equation

\partial_{t}U_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta U_{h}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(U_{h}^{\ell}\otimes U_{h}^{\ell})+\nabla_{h}P^{\ell}=-\nabla_{h}\cdot\tau^{\ell}+G_{\delta}^{\ell}

(B.23)

in
Q_{\rm prep}
, where
\tau^{\ell}
and
\kappa^{\ell}
are given by (
B.5
). The estimates

\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\rm prep})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6},

(B.24)

\int_{I_{-}}\int\varphi\kappa^{\ell}\,dx\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{2},

(B.25)

and

\|G_{\delta}^{\ell}\|_{Z^{\prime}(Q_{\rm prep})}+\|\partial_{3}P^{\ell}\|_{Y^{\prime}(Q_{\rm prep})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}

(B.26)

hold for some fixed exponents
N>0
and
b>0
. The package also includes the solenoidal-correction residual admissibility (
A.31
) on the corresponding preparation cylinders.

Remark B.2
.

The estimates in
B.1
are recorded as a structural hypothesis. Appendix
A
proves the low-frequency preparation, the directly estimated pieces of the residual splitting, and compactness-level pressure facts, but the assertion that one and the same
P^{\ell}
simultaneously satisfies the covariance equation, the vertical compatibility estimate, the solenoidal-correction residual admissibility, and the harmonic pressure approximation (
B.22
) is kept as an explicit closure input. The exponent
1/6
in (
B.21
) and (
B.24
) is not essential. It represents a positive power obtained from energy-class smoothing and interpolation. The subcritical selection principle below only needs a positive exponent
a_{0}>0
; for definiteness we retain
a_{0}=1/6
.

B.2.3
Variance-buffered stability

The relative entropy estimate of Appendix
A
is used in the following form.

Proposition B.2

(Variance-buffered stability from good-time preparation)
.

Let
(U^{\ell},P^{\ell},\tau^{\ell})
satisfy (
B.23
)–(
B.26
). Let
(V^{\ell},Q^{\ell})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm sh})
be a strict shadow satisfying the buffered gradient bound

\int_{s_{\ell}}^{0}\|\nabla_{h}V^{\ell}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rm sh})}\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}.

(B.27)

Assume that, for some
A_{\ell}>0
,

\mathcal{E}_{\varphi}^{\ell}(s_{\ell};U^{\ell},V^{\ell})\leq A_{\ell}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(B.28)

Then, after possibly increasing
N
and decreasing
b
, there exist
a_{E}>0
and
a_{\rm cov}\in(0,1/6]
such that

\|U^{\ell}-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}\leq C_{M,\theta}A_{\ell}^{a_{E}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{\rm cov}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

(B.29)

Furthermore, there exists
h^{\ell}\in H(Q_{\theta/4})
such that

\displaystyle\|U^{\ell}-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}+\|P^{\ell}-Q^{\ell}-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}

(B.30)

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\theta}A_{\ell}^{a_{E}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{\rm cov}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

Remark B.3
.

The precise exponents
a_{E}
and
a_{\rm cov}
depend on the parabolic interpolation used to pass from the variance-corrected energy control to
L^{3}_{t,x}
control. Their values are immaterial. The essential point is that the selected-time energy and the covariance energy tail both give finite positive powers, which is sufficient for logarithmic optimization.

B.2.4
Automatic gradient buffer for strict shadows

The variance-buffered stability estimate requires the selected strict shadow to satisfy an integrated horizontal Lipschitz bound. This is the strict limiting-class smoothing package assumed in the main theorem and recorded in Appendix
A
; its heuristic source is the no-stretching vorticity equation, local parabolic smoothing, and horizontal div–curl recovery. We record only the form used below.

Proposition B.3

(Strict limiting-class smoothing package recorded in Appendix
A
)
.

Let
(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
, namely

V=(V_{h},0),\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot V_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}Q=0,

and

\partial_{t}V_{h}-\Delta V_{h}+\nabla_{h}\!\cdot(V_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}Q=0\qquad\text{in }Q_{\rm str}.

Assume

\Phi_{V}(Q_{\rm str})\leq K_{0}(M,\theta).

Then

\int_{s}^{0}\|\nabla_{h}V(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rm sh})}\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}\qquad\text{for every }s\in I_{-}.

Moreover, after shrinking the fixed cylinder chain if necessary, the same package gives the interior bounds on
V
and
\nabla_{h}Q
needed in the localized weak–strong and high-frequency arguments below.

Proof.

This is precisely the strict limiting-class smoothing estimate assumed in the main theorem and recorded in Appendix
A
. It reflects the no-stretching structure of the strict two-and-a-half-dimensional system and the classical parabolic smoothing philosophy used throughout local regularity theory
[
7
,
13
,
29
]
. The underlying mechanism uses the scalar vorticity equation

\partial_{t}\omega-\Delta\omega+V_{h}\cdot\nabla_{h}\omega=0,\qquad\omega=\partial_{1}V_{2}-\partial_{2}V_{1},

which contains no vortex-stretching term. Local smoothing for this scalar drift-diffusion equation, together with the horizontal div–curl Schauder recovery of
\nabla_{h}V_{h}
, gives an integrable-in-time
L^{\infty}
bound on
\nabla_{h}V
on the smaller cylinder. All constants depend only on the fixed local bound and on the cylinder chain.
∎

Corollary B.1

(Automatic gradient buffer)
.

Every strict shadow selected from
L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
