satisfies the buffered gradient condition required in the variance-buffered stability estimate.

Proof.

Apply
Proposition

B.3
.
∎

B.3
Good-time covariance-calibrated selection with abstract modulus

This section proves the unconditional compactness-level selection statement. It also introduces a strengthened thick good-time set. The thick set is not needed for the abstract modulus, but it is useful for the later blow-up argument because it controls the unresolved variance on short forward time intervals, rather than only at a single slice.

B.3.1
The good-time set

Fix the lower buffer interval
I_{-}
. Set

B_{\ell}(s):=\int\varphi\kappa^{\ell}(x,s)\,dx.

By (
B.25
), choose
C_{G,M,\theta}
sufficiently large so that

\mathcal{G}_{\ell}:=\left\{s\in I_{-}:B_{\ell}(s)\leq C_{G,M,\theta}\ell^{2}\right\}

(B.31)

satisfies

|\mathcal{G}_{\ell}|\geq c_{G,M,\theta}>0.

(B.32)

Indeed, this follows from Markov’s inequality applied to (
B.25
), after increasing
C_{G,M,\theta}
.

Definition B.1

(Covariance-calibrated good-time distance)
.

For a prepared trajectory
(U^{\ell},P^{\ell},\tau^{\ell})
, define

\displaystyle d^{\ell}_{\mathrm{cc},g}(U^{\ell},P^{\ell},\tau^{\ell})^{2}:=\inf_{s\in\mathcal{G}_{\ell}}\inf_{(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|U^{\ell}(x,s)-V(x,s)|^{2}\,dx+\int\varphi\kappa^{\ell}(x,s)\,dx\bigg].

(B.33)

The pressure does not appear explicitly in the slice energy, but the admissible shadows are selected from the harmonic-pressure projection class.

Theorem B.1

(Abstract-modulus covariance-calibrated selection)
.

Let
(U^{\ell},P^{\ell},\tau^{\ell})
be a prepared covariance-form trajectory satisfying
B.1
. Then there exists a nondecreasing modulus
\Omega_{\mathrm{cc}}
, with
\Omega_{\mathrm{cc}}(s)\to 0
as
s\downarrow 0
, such that

d^{\ell}_{\mathrm{cc},g}(U^{\ell},P^{\ell},\tau^{\ell})^{2}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{2}+\Omega_{\mathrm{cc}}\Bigl(C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}\Bigr).

(B.34)

Proof.

By the strict harmonic projection modulus, applied to (
B.23
) and the defect estimates (
B.24
)–(
B.26
), there exist
(V^{\ell},Q^{\ell})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
and
h^{\ell}\in H(Q_{\rm str})
such that

\|U^{\ell}-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\rm str})}+\|P^{\ell}-Q^{\ell}-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\rm str})}\leq\Omega_{\mathrm{str}}(e_{\ell}),

(B.35)

where

e_{\ell}:=C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(B.36)

Set

A_{\ell}(t)=\int\varphi|U^{\ell}(x,t)-V^{\ell}(x,t)|^{3}\,dx.

Since
|\mathcal{G}_{\ell}|\geq c_{G,M,\theta}
, the integral estimate (
B.35
) gives

\int_{\mathcal{G}_{\ell}}A_{\ell}(t)\,dt\leq\|U^{\ell}-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\rm sh})}^{3}\leq\Omega_{\mathrm{str}}(e_{\ell})^{3}.

Hence there exists
s_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
such that

A_{\ell}(s_{\ell})\leq C_{M,\theta}\Omega_{\mathrm{str}}(e_{\ell})^{3}.

(B.37)

By the definition of
\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
,

B_{\ell}(s_{\ell})\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{2}.

(B.38)

On the fixed support of
\varphi
, Holder’s inequality gives

\int\varphi|U^{\ell}(s_{\ell})-V^{\ell}(s_{\ell})|^{2}\,dx\leq C_{M,\theta}A_{\ell}(s_{\ell})^{2/3}\leq C_{M,\theta}\Omega_{\mathrm{str}}(e_{\ell})^{2}.

Combining this estimate with (
B.38
), we obtain

\mathcal{E}_{\varphi}^{\ell}(s_{\ell};U^{\ell},V^{\ell})\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{2}+C_{M,\theta}\Omega_{\mathrm{str}}(e_{\ell})^{2}.

Thus (
B.34
) holds with

\Omega_{\mathrm{cc}}(s)=C_{M,\theta}\Omega_{\mathrm{str}}(s)^{2}.

∎

Remark B.4
.

This theorem is the rigorous compactness-level good-time selection statement. It corrects the unrestricted minimization problem by forcing the time to lie in
\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
. The price is the abstract modulus
\Omega_{\mathrm{cc}}
, which is not sufficient for a logarithmic rate.

B.3.2
Thick good times

For the later blow-up argument we also introduce a one-sided thick version of the good-time set. Let
I_{-}^{\circ}\Subset I_{-}
be a fixed lower buffer interval, and let
h_{0}>0
be chosen so that
[s,s+h]\subset I_{-}
whenever
s\in I_{-}^{\circ}
and
0<h<h_{0}
. Define

\mathcal{M}^{+}_{I_{-}}f(s):=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<h<h_{0}\\
[s,s+h]\subset I_{-}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{h}\int_{s}^{s+h}|f(\tau)|\,d\tau.

(B.39)

For a large constant
K_{G,M,\theta}
set

\mathcal{G}_{\ell}^{\sharp}:=\left\{s\in I_{-}^{\circ}:\mathcal{M}^{+}_{I_{-}}B_{\ell}(s)\leq K_{G,M,\theta}\ell^{2}\right\}.

(B.40)

Lemma B.1

(Positive measure and forward variance control)
.

There exist
K_{G,M,\theta}
and
c_{G,M,\theta}>0
, independent of
\ell
, such that

|\mathcal{G}_{\ell}^{\sharp}|\geq c_{G,M,\theta}.

(B.41)

Moreover, if
s\in\mathcal{G}_{\ell}^{\sharp}
, then for every admissible
h>0
,

\int_{s}^{s+h}\int\varphi\kappa^{\ell}(x,\tau)\,dx\,d\tau\leq K_{G,M,\theta}\ell^{2}h.

(B.42)

Proof.

By (
B.25
),

\int_{I_{-}}B_{\ell}(s)\,ds\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{2}.

The one-sided Hardy–Littlewood maximal inequality gives

\left|\left\{s\in I_{-}^{\circ}:\mathcal{M}^{+}_{I_{-}}B_{\ell}(s)>K\ell^{2}\right\}\right|\leq\frac{C}{K\ell^{2}}\int_{I_{-}}B_{\ell}(s)\,ds\leq\frac{C_{M,\theta}}{K}.

Choosing
K=K_{G,M,\theta}
sufficiently large gives (
B.41
). The estimate (
B.42
) follows directly from the definition of
\mathcal{M}^{+}_{I_{-}}
.
∎

Definition B.2

(Sharp covariance-calibrated distance)
.

Define

\displaystyle d^{\ell,\sharp}_{\mathrm{cc},g}(U^{\ell},P^{\ell},\tau^{\ell})^{2}:=\essinf_{s\in\mathcal{G}_{\ell}^{\sharp}}\inf_{(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})}\bigg[\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|U^{\ell}(x,s)-V(x,s)|^{2}\,dx+\int\varphi\kappa^{\ell}(x,s)\,dx\bigg].

(B.43)

Proposition B.4

(Abstract selection over thick good times)
.

There exists a nondecreasing modulus
\Omega_{\mathrm{cc}}^{\sharp}
, with
\Omega_{\mathrm{cc}}^{\sharp}(s)\to 0
as
s\downarrow 0
, such that

d^{\ell,\sharp}_{\mathrm{cc},g}(U^{\ell},P^{\ell},\tau^{\ell})^{2}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{2}+\Omega_{\mathrm{cc}}^{\sharp}\Bigl(C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}\Bigr).

(B.44)

Proof.

The proof is identical to the proof of
Theorem

B.1
, with
\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
replaced by
\mathcal{G}_{\ell}^{\sharp}
. The only property used in the averaging step is a positive lower bound for the measure of the admissible time set, which is provided by
Lemma

B.1
. For a.e.
s\in\mathcal{G}_{\ell}^{\sharp}
one also has
B_{\ell}(s)\leq K_{G,M,\theta}\ell^{2}
at Lebesgue points. Therefore the same Holder estimate gives (
B.44
) with
\Omega_{\mathrm{cc}}^{\sharp}(s)=C_{M,\theta}\Omega_{\mathrm{str}}(s)^{2}
.
∎

Remark B.5

(Why the thick set is useful)
.

Since
\mathcal{G}_{\ell}^{\sharp}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
up to harmless changes of constants, a finite-power estimate for
d^{\ell,\sharp}_{\mathrm{cc},g}
implies
B.1
. The advantage of the sharp formulation is that a sharp almost minimizer may be chosen at a density point of the near-minimizing set, while (
B.42
) controls the variance on short forward time intervals. This is exactly the structure needed to attack trace loss by a short-time parabolic smoothing argument.

B.4
The subcritical finite-power selection principle

The logarithmic theorem requires a finite-power replacement for the abstract modulus in
Theorem

B.1
. A stronger formulation would use
\ell^{2}
as the selection scale. The final reduction uses the weaker but sufficient subcritical scale.

Intermediate Target B.1

(Subcritical covariance-calibrated finite-power selection)
.

There exists an exponent

0<\mu<\frac{1}{6}

(B.45)

with the following property. For every prepared covariance-form trajectory satisfying
B.1
, there exist

s_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}_{\ell},\qquad(V^{\ell},Q^{\ell})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str}),

such that

\mathcal{E}_{\varphi}^{\ell}(s_{\ell};U^{\ell},V^{\ell})\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(B.46)

Remark B.6

(Automatic gradient buffer)
.

The buffered gradient bound required in
Proposition

B.2
is not an additional selection requirement. By
Corollary

B.1
, every strict shadow selected from
L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
, with

Q_{\rm sh}\Subset Q_{\rm str},

satisfies

\int_{s_{\ell}}^{0}\|\nabla_{h}V^{\ell}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\rm sh})}\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}.

Thus
B.1
only needs to select
s_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
and
(V^{\ell},Q^{\ell})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
satisfying the covariance-calibrated finite-power slice estimate.

Remark B.7

(Why
\ell^{\mu}
is enough)
.

A logarithmic optimization only needs a positive power of
\ell
on the non-small part of the error. If the final separated estimate has the form

X_{\theta/4}^{\mathrm{har}}(u,p;M)\leq C\ell^{a}+C\ell^{-N}e^{C\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b},\qquad a>0,

then choosing
\ell\sim|\log\delta|^{-1/N}
gives

X_{\theta/4}^{\mathrm{har}}(u,p;M)\leq C|\log\delta|^{-a/N}.

Thus the exponent
\mu
may be small. The restriction
\mu<1/6
is chosen to match the covariance and cutoff estimates in the blow-up argument of
Section

B.6
.

Remark B.8

(Relation to the stronger finite-power selection)
.

The stronger formulation would demand

\mathcal{E}_{\varphi}^{\ell}(s_{\ell};U^{\ell},V^{\ell})\leq C\ell^{2}+C\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

That estimate is stronger than necessary and is not compatible with the currently available stress bound
\|\tau^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}}\lesssim\ell^{1/6}
in a direct blow-up proof. The subcritical target (
B.46
) is weaker, still finite-power, and still sufficient for the logarithmic theorem.

Remark B.9

(The single remaining gate)
.

The implication proved in the next section shows that the logarithmic theorem is reduced to
B.1
. Thus the strict-shadow problem is no longer the size of the covariance stress, the harmonic-pressure comparison, or the CKN decay step. The only missing finite-rate input is the subcritical metric estimate

d^{\ell}_{\mathrm{cc},g}(U^{\ell},P^{\ell},\tau^{\ell})^{2}\leq C_{M,\theta}\bigl(\ell^{\mu}+\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}\bigr),\qquad 0<\mu<1/6.

The stronger sharp version with
d^{\ell,\sharp}_{\mathrm{cc},g}
is a convenient form for the blow-up analysis, and it immediately implies the original formulation because the admissible time set is smaller.

B.5
Logarithmic approximation from subcritical selection

We now show that
B.1
is sufficient to close the logarithmic rate.

Theorem B.2

(Prepared logarithmic estimate under subcritical selection)
.

Assume
B.1
and
B.1
. Then there exist constants
a_{*}>0
,
N_{*}>0
,
b_{*}>0
, and
C_{M,\theta}\geq 1
such that, for every sufficiently small
0<\ell<\ell_{0}(M,\theta)
, there exist
(V^{\ell},Q^{\ell})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\theta/4})
and
h^{\ell}\in H(Q_{\theta/4})
with

\displaystyle\|u-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}+\|p-Q^{\ell}-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}

(B.47)

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{*}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N_{*}}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N_{*}}}\delta^{b_{*}}.

Proof.

By
B.1
,

\mathcal{E}_{\varphi}^{\ell}(s_{\ell};U^{\ell},V^{\ell})\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

Apply the variance-buffered stability estimate,
Proposition

B.2
, with

A_{\ell}=C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}.

This gives, for some
a_{E}>0
and
a_{\rm cov}\in(0,1/6]
,

\displaystyle\|U^{\ell}-V^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}+\|P^{\ell}-Q^{\ell}-h^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}

(B.48)

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu a_{E}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{\rm cov}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

The preparation estimate (
B.21
) and the pressure closure (
B.22
) give

\|u-U^{\ell}\|_{L^{3}(Q_{\theta/4})}+\|p-P^{\ell}-h_{\rm prep}^{\ell}\|_{L^{3/2}(Q_{\theta/4})}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{1/6}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b},

after decreasing
b
and increasing
N
if necessary. Combining this with (
B.48
), and replacing the final harmonic correction by
h^{\ell}+h_{\rm prep}^{\ell}
, proves (
B.47
), with

a_{*}:=\min\{a_{\rm cov},\mu a_{E}\}>0,

and after renaming
N,b
as
N_{*},b_{*}
.
∎

Corollary B.2

(Logarithmic harmonic-pressure approximation)
.

Assume
B.1
and
B.1
. Then there exist constants
C_{M,\theta}\geq 1
,
\sigma>0
, and
\delta_{M,\theta}\in(0,1)
such that every suitable weak solution in
Q_{1}
satisfying

\Phi(1)\leq M,\qquad 0<\delta=C_{3}(1)\leq\delta_{M,\theta},

obeys

X_{\theta/4}^{\mathrm{har}}(u,p;M)\leq C_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma}.

(B.49)

Proof.

By
Theorem

B.2
, the sum of the relevant
L^{3}
and
L^{3/2}
norms is bounded by

R_{\ell}:=C\ell^{a_{*}}+C\ell^{-N_{*}}e^{C\ell^{-N_{*}}}\delta^{b_{*}}.

Since
X_{\theta/4}^{\mathrm{har}}
is defined by integrals rather than by norms,

X_{\theta/4}^{\mathrm{har}}(u,p;M)\leq C_{\theta}\bigl(R_{\ell}^{3}+R_{\ell}^{3/2}\bigr).

After increasing the finite-power loss and the exponential constant, and after renaming the positive exponents, this has the same separated form:

X_{\theta/4}^{\mathrm{har}}(u,p;M)\leq C\ell^{a_{*}}+C\ell^{-N_{*}}e^{C\ell^{-N_{*}}}\delta^{b_{*}}.

Let
L=|\log\delta|
. For
\delta
sufficiently small, choose

\ell=\left(\frac{2C}{b_{*}L}\right)^{1/N_{*}}.

Then
C\ell^{-N_{*}}=b_{*}L/2
, and hence

e^{C\ell^{-N_{*}}}\delta^{b_{*}}=e^{b_{*}L/2}e^{-b_{*}L}=e^{-b_{*}L/2}=\delta^{b_{*}/2}.

Also

\ell^{a_{*}}\leq CL^{-a_{*}/N_{*}},\qquad\ell^{-N_{*}}e^{C\ell^{-N_{*}}}\delta^{b_{*}}\leq CL\delta^{b_{*}/2}\leq CL^{-a_{*}/N_{*}}

for
L
large. Thus (
B.49
) holds with

\sigma=\frac{a_{*}}{N_{*}}.

∎

B.5.1
Harmonic-pressure comparison decay

We now make explicit the comparison step which converts harmonic-pressure approximation into decay of the CKN quantity. For a scalar function
f
set

D_{f}(r):=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{Q_{r}}|f-(f)_{B_{r}}(t)|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt.

Lemma B.2

(Oscillation decay for spatially harmonic pressures)
.

Let
0<r\leq R/2
. Suppose
h\in L^{3/2}(Q_{R})
and

\Delta h(\cdot,t)=0\quad\text{in }B_{R}\quad\text{for a.e. }t\in(-R^{2},0).

Then

D_{h}(r)\leq C\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{5/2}D_{h}(R).

The constant
C
is universal.

Proof.

Fix a time
t
for which
h(\cdot,t)
is harmonic in
B_{R}
. The interior gradient estimate for harmonic functions gives

\|\nabla h(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R/2})}\leq CR^{-3}\|h(\cdot,t)-(h)_{B_{R}}(t)\|_{L^{3/2}(B_{R})}.

Hence, for
0<r\leq R/2
,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|h-(h)_{B_{r}}(t)|^{3/2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{3+3/2}\|\nabla h(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r})}^{3/2}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{9/2}R^{-9/2}\int_{B_{R}}|h-(h)_{B_{R}}(t)|^{3/2}\,dx.

Integrating over
t\in(-r^{2},0)
, enlarging the time interval to
(-R^{2},0)
, and multiplying by
r^{-2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle D_{h}(r)

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-2}r^{9/2}R^{-9/2}\int_{Q_{R}}|h-(h)_{B_{R}}(t)|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle=C\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{5/2}D_{h}(R).

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma B.3

(Strict-shadow decay)
.

Let
R=\theta/4
. There exists
C_{M,\theta}\geq 1
such that every
(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{R})
satisfies

C_{V}(r)+D_{Q}(r)\leq C_{M,\theta}r

for all
0<r\leq R/2
.

Proof.

This is the fixed-scale decay consequence of the regularity theory for the strict limiting system. Indeed, strict solutions satisfy

V=(V_{h},0),\qquad\nabla_{h}\cdot V_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}Q=0,

and the horizontal curl obeys a scalar drift-diffusion equation without three-dimensional vortex stretching. The local regularity package for this system gives

\|V\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R/2})}+\|\nabla Q\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R/2})}\leq C_{M,\theta}.

Consequently,

C_{V}(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{Q_{r}}|V|^{3}\,dx\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}r^{3}\leq C_{M,\theta}r,

and, by the mean-value estimate for the pressure,

D_{Q}(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{Q_{r}}|Q-(Q)_{B_{r}}(t)|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}r^{9/2}\leq C_{M,\theta}r.

The proof is complete.
∎

Proposition B.5

(Harmonic-pressure comparison decay)
.

Let
R=\theta/4
. Let
(u,p)
be a suitable weak solution in
Q_{1}
satisfying

\Phi(1)\leq M.

Suppose that for some
(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{R})
, some
h\in H(Q_{R})
, and some
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
, one has the integral excess bound

R^{-2}\int_{Q_{R}}|u-V|^{3}\,dx\,dt+R^{-2}\int_{Q_{R}}|p-Q-h|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt\leq\varepsilon.

Equivalently, the same conclusion holds if the sum of the corresponding
L^{3}
and
L^{3/2}
norms is bounded by a positive power of
\varepsilon
, after renaming
\varepsilon
. Then there exists
r_{\rm cmp}=r_{\rm cmp}(M,\theta)\in(0,R/2]
such that

\Psi(r)\leq C_{M,\theta}r+C_{M,\theta}r^{-2}\varepsilon

for every
0<r\leq r_{\rm cmp}
.

Proof.

Let
e_{p}:=p-Q-h
. We first estimate the velocity part. Since
|u|^{3}\leq C|V|^{3}+C|u-V|^{3}
, we have, for
0<r\leq R/2
,

\displaystyle C(r)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{Q_{r}}|u|^{3}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq CC_{V}(r)+Cr^{-2}\int_{Q_{r}}|u-V|^{3}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\theta}r+Cr^{-2}\int_{Q_{R}}|u-V|^{3}\,dx\,dt.

The integral excess hypothesis gives

\int_{Q_{R}}|u-V|^{3}\,dx\,dt\leq C_{\theta}\varepsilon.

Thus

C(r)\leq C_{M,\theta}r+C_{M,\theta}r^{-2}\varepsilon.

For the pressure part, for each time
t
,

p-(p)_{B_{r}}(t)=\bigl(Q-(Q)_{B_{r}}(t)\bigr)+\bigl(h-(h)_{B_{r}}(t)\bigr)+\bigl(e_{p}-(e_{p})_{B_{r}}(t)\bigr).

Therefore,

D(r)\leq CD_{Q}(r)+CD_{h}(r)+Cr^{-2}\int_{Q_{r}}|e_{p}|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt.

The strict-shadow decay lemma gives
D_{Q}(r)\leq C_{M,\theta}r
. For the error term, the integral excess hypothesis gives

\int_{Q_{r}}|e_{p}|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt\leq\int_{Q_{R}}|e_{p}|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt\leq C_{\theta}\varepsilon.

It remains to control
D_{h}(r)
. By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle D_{h}(R)

\displaystyle\leq CD_{p}(R)+CD_{Q}(R)+CR^{-2}\int_{Q_{R}}|e_{p}|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C_{M,\theta}.

Here we used the nesting consequence of
\Phi(1)\leq M
, the definition of
L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{R})
, the fixed scale
R=\theta/4
, and
\varepsilon\leq 1
. Applying
Lemma

B.2
, we obtain

D_{h}(r)\leq C_{M,\theta}\left(\frac{r}{R}\right)^{5/2}\leq C_{M,\theta}r,

after increasing
C_{M,\theta}
, since
R=\theta/4
is fixed.

Combining the estimates for
C(r)
and
D(r)
gives

\Psi(r)=C(r)+D(r)\leq C_{M,\theta}r+C_{M,\theta}r^{-2}\varepsilon.

This proves the proposition.
∎

Corollary B.3

(Logarithmic finite-scale decay and radius)
.

Assume
B.1
and
B.1
. Then there exist constants
C_{M,\theta}\geq 1
,
\sigma>0
, and
\delta_{M,\theta}\in(0,1)
such that, if

\Phi(1)\leq M,\qquad 0<\delta=C_{3}(1)\leq\delta_{M,\theta},

then

\Psi(r)\leq C_{M,\theta}r+C_{M,\theta}r^{-2}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma}

(B.50)

for all
0<r\leq r_{\rm cmp}
. Consequently, after decreasing
\delta_{M,\theta}
,

r_{\mathrm{reg}}(0,0)\geq c_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma/3}.

(B.51)

Proof.

By
Corollary

B.2
, and by the definition of the harmonic-pressure excess, there exist
(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\theta/4})
and
h\in H(Q_{\theta/4})
such that

(\theta/4)^{-2}\int_{Q_{\theta/4}}|u-V|^{3}\,dx\,dt+(\theta/4)^{-2}\int_{Q_{\theta/4}}|p-Q-h|^{3/2}\,dx\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma}.

After decreasing
\delta_{M,\theta}
, the right-hand side is at most
1
. Applying
Proposition

B.5
with

\varepsilon=C_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma}

gives (
B.50
) for
0<r\leq r_{\rm cmp}
.

Set
L=|\log\delta|
and choose
r_{L}=L^{-\sigma/3}
. After decreasing
\delta_{M,\theta}
, we may assume
r_{L}\leq r_{\rm cmp}
. Then

r_{L}^{-2}L^{-\sigma}=L^{2\sigma/3}L^{-\sigma}=L^{-\sigma/3}=r_{L}.

Therefore

\Psi(r_{L})\leq C_{M,\theta}L^{-\sigma/3}.

For
L
sufficiently large, equivalently for
\delta\leq\delta_{M,\theta}
sufficiently small, the right-hand side is less than the CKN threshold
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{CKN}}
. The quantitative CKN estimate gives

\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{\kappa r_{L}})}\leq C_{\mathrm{CKN}}r_{L}^{-1}\leq c_{\rm reg}(\kappa r_{L})^{-1},

so the definition of the regularity radius gives

r_{\mathrm{reg}}(0,0)\geq\kappa r_{L}=\kappa|\log\delta|^{-\sigma/3}.

Absorbing
\kappa
into
c_{M,\theta}
proves the radius bound.
∎

B.6
Trace and tangent-cone formulation for strict-shadow selection

This section records the trace and tangent-cone formulation behind
B.1
. The purpose is to separate the parts that are now proved from the geometric input that remains. The subcritical exponent removes the covariance-size obstruction in the normalized blow-up. The thick good-time refinement removes the trace-loss obstruction, through a short-time high-frequency drop argument. The remaining structural point is geometric: the finite-power selection problem is a tangent-cone metric regularity problem for the strict shadow trace class, and the strict pressure condition
\partial_{3}Q=0
creates a nonlinear compatibility constraint.

B.6.1
The strict shadow trace manifold

For
s\in I_{-}
, define the localized strict trace class

\mathcal{M}_{s}^{\mathrm{str}}:=\{V(s):(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})\}.

(B.52)

The finite-power selection problem is a metric regularity problem for this trace class in the localized energy topology. The trace class is constrained by the horizontal divergence-free condition, by the strict evolution equation, and by the vertical pressure compatibility condition
\partial_{3}Q=0
.

Taking the horizontal divergence of the strict equation gives, formally,

-\Delta_{h}Q[V]=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b}),\qquad a,b\in\{1,2\}.

(B.53)

Thus, modulo horizontal harmonic functions,

Q[V]=-\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b}).

The condition
\partial_{3}Q[V]=0
is therefore encoded by the nonlinear compatibility map

\mathfrak{C}(V):=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b}).

(B.54)

The fixed gauge convention above is part of the definition; without it the expression would not be a gauge-free object on the full horizontal-harmonic quotient. The strict shadow class is contained in the zero set

\mathfrak{C}(V)=0.

(B.55)

The linearization at a strict shadow
V
is

D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[W]=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}W_{b}+W_{a}V_{b}).

(B.56)

Every integrable strict tangent direction must satisfy
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[W]=0
. This is the compatibility condition corresponding to the linearized pressure constraint
\partial_{3}\Pi=0
.

We also use the quadratic bilinear form

\mathfrak{B}(A,B):=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(A_{a}B_{b}).

(B.57)

Thus

\mathfrak{C}(V)=\mathfrak{B}(V,V),\qquad D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[W]=\mathfrak{B}(V,W)+\mathfrak{B}(W,V).

Definition B.3

(Integrable tangent cone)
.

Let
(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
. The localized integrable tangent cone
T^{\rm int}_{V}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{str}}
consists of all finite-energy fields
W=(W_{h},0)
for which there exist exact strict shadows
(V^{\varepsilon},Q^{\varepsilon})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
such that, along a sequence
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
,

\frac{V^{\varepsilon}-V}{\varepsilon}\to W

(B.58)

in the local energy topology and in the localized trace topology at the relevant time slice.

Remark B.10

(Tangent cone versus formal tangent space)
.

The formal linearized strict system is

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}W_{h}-\Delta W_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes W_{h}+W_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi=0,\\
\nabla_{h}\cdot W_{h}=0,\\
\partial_{3}\Pi=0.\end{cases}

(B.59)

Solutions of (
B.59
) are formal tangent directions. To use the first-variation argument, however, one needs the blow-up direction to lie in the integrable tangent cone. Thus the correct strict-shadow input is not that every formal linearized solution is integrable, but that the special blow-up directions generated by failed selection belong to
T^{\rm int}_{V}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{str}}
.

Intermediate Target B.2

(Blow-up tangent-cone inclusion)
.

Let
W
be a normalized blow-up limit obtained from a failed subcritical selection sequence as in
Lemma

B.4
. Then

W\in\overline{T^{\rm int}_{V}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{str}}}

(B.60)

in the localized trace topology at the limiting time
s_{*}
. Equivalently,
W(s_{*})
can be approximated in
L^{2}_{\varphi}
by traces of integrable strict tangent directions.

B.6.2
Regular strata, singular strata, and quadratic compatibility

The following statement records the regular-stratum mechanism. It is an implicit-function-theorem statement for the full strict parabolic map, not merely for the scalar compatibility map: on fully regular strata, formal linearized strict directions are integrable tangent directions.

Definition B.4

(Fully regular strict shadow)
.

A strict shadow
(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
is called fully regular if the full localized strict parabolic map

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}(Y,R):=\big(

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Y_{h}-\Delta Y_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(Y_{h}\otimes Y_{h})+\nabla_{h}R,

\displaystyle\nabla_{h}\cdot Y_{h},\ \partial_{3}R,\ \mathfrak{G}_{\mathfrak{g}}(Y,R)\big)

is
C^{1}
in the localized parabolic spaces used for shadow selection, after the fixed pressure gauge
\mathfrak{g}
and the trace convention
\mathfrak{G}_{\mathfrak{g}}
have been imposed, and the derivative

D\mathfrak{F}_{(V,Q)}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{Y}

has a bounded right inverse on the relevant complemented range. Here
\mathcal{X}
denotes the localized horizontal divergence-compatible perturbation space,
\mathcal{P}
the fixed-gauge pressure space, and
\mathcal{Y}
the full residual space containing the evolution residual, divergence residual, vertical pressure residual, and gauge/trace residual. This is stronger than right-invertibility of
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}
alone.

Proposition B.6

(Regular-stratum tangent-cone inclusion)
.

Let
(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
be a fully regular strict shadow. Let
(W,\Pi)
be a finite-energy solution of the linearized strict system (
B.59
) around
(V,Q)
, in the same fixed pressure gauge and trace convention. Then

W\in T^{\rm int}_{V}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{str}}

in the local energy topology and in the localized trace topology. In particular, if a failed-selection blow-up limit has a fully regular base shadow, then the blow-up tangent-cone inclusion
B.2
holds.

Proof.

Since
(V,Q)
is an exact strict shadow,
\mathfrak{F}(V,Q)=0
. Since
(W,\Pi)
solves the full linearized strict system with the fixed gauge and trace convention, it satisfies

D\mathfrak{F}_{(V,Q)}[W,\Pi]=0.

For small
\varepsilon
, solve

\mathfrak{F}\big((V,Q)+\varepsilon(W,\Pi)+(R^{\varepsilon},S^{\varepsilon})\big)=0

with
(R^{\varepsilon},S^{\varepsilon})
in a chosen complement to the kernel of
D\mathfrak{F}_{(V,Q)}
. The bounded right inverse and the Banach implicit-function theorem give

(R^{\varepsilon},S^{\varepsilon})=O(\varepsilon^{2})

in the localized parabolic product space. Hence

(V^{\varepsilon},Q^{\varepsilon}):=(V,Q)+\varepsilon(W,\Pi)+(R^{\varepsilon},S^{\varepsilon})

is an exact strict shadow in the fixed gauge, for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, and it remains in
L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
after the usual harmless restriction to a smaller neighborhood. Moreover

\frac{V^{\varepsilon}-V}{\varepsilon}\to W

in the local energy topology and in the
L^{2}_{\varphi}
trace topology. Therefore
W\in T^{\rm int}_{V}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{str}}
.
∎

Remark B.11

(A singular-stratum obstruction)
.

At the zero shadow
V=0
, the map
\mathfrak{C}
is quadratic and hence
D\mathfrak{C}_{0}=0
. Therefore the formal tangent space
\ker D\mathfrak{C}_{0}
is too large. A linearized heat-flow perturbation
W
is integrable only if the quadratic compatibility condition

\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(W_{a}W_{b})=0

(B.61)

holds, at least at the next order. For example, in a local periodic model, take

W_{h}=\nabla_{h}^{\perp}\bigl(\sin x_{1}\sin x_{2}\,f(x_{3})\bigr).

Then, up to harmless constants,

\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(W_{a}W_{b})=f(x_{3})^{2}(\cos 2x_{1}+\cos 2x_{2}),

so the induced quadratic pressure has nonzero vertical derivative unless
ff^{\prime}\equiv 0
. Thus such a
W
may solve the linearized strict equation around
0
, but it is not automatically an integrable strict tangent direction. This shows why the strict-shadow reduction must be formulated in terms of tangent cones or regular strata, rather than the full formal tangent space.

The next proposition records a structural gain from the failed-selection blow-up itself: the normalized blow-up direction is not an arbitrary formal tangent direction. It satisfies the quadratic compatibility condition forced by the approximate strict pressure relation.

Proposition B.7

(Projected quadratic compatibility of blow-up directions)
.

Let
W
be a normalized blow-up limit obtained from a failed sharp finite-power selection sequence, and let
(V,Q)
be the limiting strict shadow. Then, for every fixed finite-stage projection
P_{N}
,

P_{N}\mathfrak{B}(W,W)\in\overline{P_{N}\operatorname{Range}(D\mathfrak{C}_{V})}

(B.62)

in the finite-dimensional projected compatibility-defect topology. Equivalently, the quotient distance of
P_{N}\mathfrak{B}(W,W)
to the projected moving-base range is zero. If, in addition, the moving-base contribution vanishes and the projected range of
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}
is trivial, then

P_{N}\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0.

(B.63)

Proof.

Write

\varepsilon_{n}:=m_{n}^{1/2},\qquad U^{n}=V^{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n}.

Taking the horizontal divergence of the prepared covariance-form equation gives, in the localized distributional sense,

-\Delta_{h}P^{n}=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(U_{a}^{n}U_{b}^{n})+\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\tau^{n}_{ab}+\operatorname{div}_{h}G^{n}_{\delta_{n}}.

(B.64)

The strict shadow satisfies

-\Delta_{h}Q^{n}=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}^{n}V_{b}^{n}),\qquad\partial_{3}Q^{n}=0.

(B.65)

Subtracting and expanding
U^{n}=V^{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n}
gives

\displaystyle-\Delta_{h}(P^{n}-Q^{n})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{n}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}^{n}W_{b}^{n}+W_{a}^{n}V_{b}^{n})+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(W_{a}^{n}W_{b}^{n})

\displaystyle+\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\tau^{n}_{ab}+\operatorname{div}_{h}G^{n}_{\delta_{n}}.

Apply
\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}
to this identity. Since
\partial_{3}Q^{n}=0
, one obtains

D\mathfrak{C}_{V^{n}}[W_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})=-\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}P^{n}+\mathcal{R}_{n},

(B.66)

where
\mathcal{R}_{n}
contains the covariance and one-component residual contributions after division by
\varepsilon_{n}
.
Dividing (
B.66
) by
\varepsilon_{n}
gives

\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}D\mathfrak{C}_{V^{n}}[W_{n}]+\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})=-\varepsilon_{n}^{-2}\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}P^{n}+\frac{\mathcal{R}_{n}}{\varepsilon_{n}}.

(B.67)

The right-hand side tends to zero in the localized compatibility-defect topology. Indeed,

\varepsilon_{n}^{-2}\|\partial_{3}P^{n}\|_{Y^{\prime}}=\frac{\|\partial_{3}P^{n}\|_{Y^{\prime}}}{m_{n}}\leq C\frac{\eta_{n}}{m_{n}}\to 0,

and the covariance and residual terms are controlled in the same way, using

\|\tau^{n}\|_{L^{3/2}}\lesssim\ell_{n}^{1/6},\qquad m_{n}\gg\ell_{n}^{\mu},\qquad 0<\mu<1/6,

together with the residual estimate
\|G^{n}_{\delta_{n}}\|_{Z^{\prime}}\lesssim\eta_{n}
.
After applying any fixed finite-stage projection
P_{N}
, the term
P_{N}\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}D\mathfrak{C}_{V^{n}}[W_{n}]
lies in the projected moving-base range. The preceding estimate says that
P_{N}\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})
has vanishing distance to that projected range. Passing to the limit, using the smooth convergence
V^{n}\to V
and the trace/local convergence of
W_{n}\to W
, yields precisely (
B.62
). The stronger unprojected statement
\mathfrak{B}(W,W)\in\operatorname{Range}(D\mathfrak{C}_{V})
would require a closed-range and moving-base stability hypothesis; it is not used here. The projected zero-range conclusion (
B.63
) follows only under the additional trivial-range and no-moving-base assumptions stated in the proposition.
∎

Intermediate Target B.3

(Singular-stratum strict curve selection)
.

Let
(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
be a possibly singular strict shadow, and let
W
be a finite-energy solution of the linearized strict system around
(V,Q)
. Suppose that, at every finite stage, the projected quadratic compatibility condition

P_{N}\mathfrak{B}(W,W)\in\overline{P_{N}\operatorname{Range}(D\mathfrak{C}_{V})}

(B.68)

holds in the localized finite-stage compatibility-defect topology, together with the moving-base stability assumptions needed to pass from finite-stage compatibility to an exact strict curve. Then
W
belongs to the integrable tangent cone
T_{V}^{\rm int}\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{str}}
.

Remark B.12

(Status of the singular-stratum problem)
.

Proposition

B.6
proves tangent-cone inclusion on fully regular strata, where the full strict parabolic map has a right inverse.
Proposition

B.7
shows that failed-selection blow-up directions satisfy the required projected quadratic compatibility condition. The remaining strict-shadow geometric input is
B.3
, an all-order correction or curve-selection statement for the zero set of the nonlinear pressure-compatibility map, including the moving-base and closed-projected-range issues. In the special case
V=0
, it asks whether

\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0

for a finite-energy heat-flow strict linearized direction is sufficient to construct exact strict shadows
V^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon W+o(\varepsilon)
.

B.6.3
Trace tightness from thick good-time minimality

We next prove that sharp good-time almost minimizers do not lose trace energy into arbitrarily high frequencies. This replaces the localized trace-tightness assumption in the earlier compactness argument. To make the frequency estimate clean,
J_{\rho}
is taken in this subsection to be a standard Littlewood low-frequency cutoff to frequencies
|\xi|\lesssim\rho^{-1}
, and
P_{\rho}:=I-J_{\rho}
. The conclusion is equivalent to the corresponding statement with a standard spatial mollifier.

Let

\eta_{n}:=\ell_{n}^{\mu}+\ell_{n}^{-N}\delta_{n}^{b},\qquad m_{n}:=d^{\ell_{n},\sharp}_{\mathrm{cc},g}(U^{n},P^{n},\tau^{n})^{2},

(B.69)

and suppose

\eta_{n}\to 0,\qquad m_{n}\to 0,\qquad\frac{m_{n}}{\eta_{n}}\to\infty.

(B.70)

Let
s_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{\ell_{n}}^{\sharp}
and
(V^{n},Q^{n})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
be
o(m_{n})
-minimizers,

\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|U^{n}(s_{n})-V^{n}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx+\int\varphi\kappa^{n}(s_{n})\,dx\leq m_{n}+o(m_{n}),

(B.71)

and set

Z_{n}:=U^{n}-V^{n},\qquad W_{n}:=m_{n}^{-1/2}Z_{n}.

(B.72)

Remark B.13

(Localization conventions in the high-frequency argument)
.

In
Proposition

B.8
, all frequency projectors are used after multiplication by a fixed cutoff supported in
B_{\rm sh}
. Commutators such as
[P_{\rho},\chi]
are estimated in the standard localized Littlewood calculus and are lower order on the time scale
\rho^{2}
. The pressure term in the normalized equation is treated after applying the localized horizontal Leray projection, equivalently by the localized Stokes pressure estimate in the horizontal variables. The vertical pressure-compatibility defect is already part of the normalized one-component residual class and vanishes at the subcritical scale. Finally, a strict shadow selected at time
s_{n}
is an admissible competitor at a later admissible comparison time
t_{n}
, because the selection distance takes the infimum over the full strict class at each time and imposes no exact trace condition.

Proposition B.8

(High-frequency trace drop)
.

Assume (
B.70
) and (
B.71
). If, for some
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and some fixed small
\rho>0
,

\int\varphi|W_{n}(s_{n})-J_{\rho}W_{n}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx\geq\varepsilon_{0}

(B.73)

along a subsequence, then for all sufficiently large
n
there exists a set

T_{n,\rho}\subset(s_{n},s_{n}+c_{0}\rho^{2})

with
|T_{n,\rho}|\geq c_{1}\rho^{2}
, such that for all
t\in T_{n,\rho}
,

\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|Z_{n}(t)|^{2}\,dx\leq\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|Z_{n}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx-c_{1}\varepsilon_{0}m_{n}+o_{\rho}(1)m_{n}+o_{n}(1)m_{n}.

(B.74)

Proof.

Write
\varphi=\chi^{2}
, with
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{\rm sh})
and
\chi\equiv 1
on the target spatial region. It is enough to prove the high-frequency estimate for

f_{n}(t):=\chi W_{n}(t).

The formulation with
\varphi|W_{n}-J_{\rho}W_{n}|^{2}
is equivalent to that with
\|P_{\rho}(\chi W_{n})\|_{2}^{2}
, because the standard Littlewood commutator estimate gives

\|[P_{\rho},\chi]W_{n}\|_{2}\leq C\rho\|W_{n}\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm sh})},

and the normalized local energy bound gives
\|W_{n}\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm sh})}\leq C_{M,\theta}
.

Subtracting the strict equation for
V^{n}
from the prepared equation for
U^{n}
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Z^{n}_{h}-\Delta Z^{n}_{h}

\displaystyle+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}^{n}\otimes Z_{h}^{n}+Z_{h}^{n}\otimes V_{h}^{n})+\nabla_{h}(P^{n}-Q^{n})

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{h}\cdot(Z_{h}^{n}\otimes Z_{h}^{n})-\nabla_{h}\cdot\tau^{n}+G^{n}_{\delta_{n}}.

Dividing by
m_{n}^{1/2}
gives

\partial_{t}W^{n}_{h}-\Delta W^{n}_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}^{n}\otimes W_{h}^{n}+W_{h}^{n}\otimes V_{h}^{n})+\nabla_{h}\Pi^{n}=R_{n},

(B.75)

where
\Pi^{n}=m_{n}^{-1/2}(P^{n}-Q^{n})
and

R_{n}=-m_{n}^{1/2}\nabla_{h}\cdot(W_{h}^{n}\otimes W_{h}^{n})-m_{n}^{-1/2}\nabla_{h}\cdot\tau^{n}+m_{n}^{-1/2}G^{n}_{\delta_{n}}.

The normalized compactness estimates give, for each fixed
\rho>0
, convergence of
R_{n}
to zero in any frequency-localized energy-dual norm appearing below. The covariance and residual terms vanish because
m_{n}/\eta_{n}\to\infty
and
0<\mu<1/6
.

Apply the cutoff
\chi
and the high-frequency projector
P_{\rho}
to (
B.75
). Testing by
P_{\rho}f_{n}
yields

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|P_{\rho}f_{n}(t)\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla P_{\rho}f_{n}(t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\|P_{\rho}f_{n}(t)\|_{2}^{2}+C\rho\|W_{n}(t)\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm sh})}^{2}+r_{n,\rho}(t).

(B.76)

Here
C
depends only on
M,\theta
and the cutoff. The first-order strict-shadow transport terms are controlled by the interior bound on
V^{n}
and
\nabla_{h}V^{n}
. The cutoff commutators give the
C\rho\|W_{n}\|_{2}^{2}
term. The localized pressure term is handled by the local horizontal Leray projection, equivalently by the standard localized Stokes pressure estimate; the vertical pressure-compatibility defect contributes only
o_{n}(1)
because
m_{n}^{-1/2}\partial_{3}P^{n}\to 0
. Thus

\int_{s_{n}}^{s_{n}+c\rho^{2}}|r_{n,\rho}(t)|\,dt=o_{n}(1)

for fixed
\rho
.

Since
P_{\rho}
projects to frequencies
\gtrsim\rho^{-1}
,

\|\nabla P_{\rho}f_{n}\|_{2}^{2}\geq c\rho^{-2}\|P_{\rho}f_{n}\|_{2}^{2}.

For
\rho
sufficiently small, (
B.76
) gives

\frac{d}{dt}\|P_{\rho}f_{n}(t)\|_{2}^{2}+c\rho^{-2}\|P_{\rho}f_{n}(t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\rho\|W_{n}(t)\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm sh})}^{2}+r_{n,\rho}(t).

Gronwall’s inequality on
[s_{n},s_{n}+h_{\rho}]
, with
h_{\rho}=\kappa_{0}\rho^{2}
and
\kappa_{0}
large, gives for every
t\in[s_{n}+h_{\rho}/2,s_{n}+h_{\rho}]
,

\|P_{\rho}f_{n}(t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|P_{\rho}f_{n}(s_{n})\|_{2}^{2}+C\rho\sup_{\tau\in(s_{n},s_{n}+h_{\rho})}\|W_{n}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm sh})}^{2}+o_{n}(1).

The low-frequency part
J_{\rho}f_{n}
changes by only
O(\rho)
over the same time scale, again by the localized equation and the bounded local energy of
W_{n}
:

\|J_{\rho}f_{n}(t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|J_{\rho}f_{n}(s_{n})\|_{2}^{2}+C\rho\sup_{\tau\in(s_{n},s_{n}+h_{\rho})}\|W_{n}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm sh})}^{2}+o_{n}(1).

Adding the low and high frequency estimates and using the almost orthogonality of the Littlewood decomposition gives

\|f_{n}(t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|f_{n}(s_{n})\|_{2}^{2}-c\|P_{\rho}f_{n}(s_{n})\|_{2}^{2}+C\rho\sup_{\tau\in(s_{n},s_{n}+h_{\rho})}\|W_{n}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm sh})}^{2}+o_{n}(1).

If (
B.73
) holds, then for
\rho
chosen small depending on
\varepsilon_{0},M,\theta
and for
n
large, this becomes

\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|W_{n}(t)|^{2}\,dx\leq\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|W_{n}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx-c\varepsilon_{0}+o_{\rho}(1)+o_{n}(1)

for every
t\in[s_{n}+h_{\rho}/2,s_{n}+h_{\rho}]
. Multiplying by
m_{n}
gives (
B.74
) on that interval, and therefore on a set
T_{n,\rho}
of measure comparable to
\rho^{2}
.
∎

Hypothesis B.2

(Sharp admissible-time intersection for trace drop)
.

For every sharp almost minimizing branch and every fixed frequency scale
\rho>0
, the high-frequency drop set
T_{n,\rho}
, the thick good-time set
\mathcal{G}^{\sharp}_{\ell_{n}}
, and the relevant
o(m_{n})
-near-minimizing admissible set have positive intersection for all sufficiently large
n
. Equivalently, the time selected by the parabolic high-frequency drop can be chosen as an admissible sharp comparison time.

Remark B.14

(Status of the time-intersection input)
.

The high-frequency trace drop preceding
B.2
is a parabolic estimate. It does not alone guarantee that a drop time is also a thick good time and an admissible near-minimizing comparison time. This intersection property is therefore retained as the sharp admissible-time trace-tightness input used in the final reduction.

Theorem B.3

(Conditional trace tightness of sharp almost minimizers)
.

Assume (
B.70
), (
B.71
), and the sharp admissible-time intersection hypothesis
B.2
. Then the normalized traces are locally tight:

\lim_{\rho\downarrow 0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\int\varphi|W_{n}(s_{n})-J_{\rho}W_{n}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx=0.

(B.77)

Proof.

Suppose not. Then there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and a sequence
\rho_{j}\downarrow 0
such that, along a subsequence,

\int\varphi|W_{n}(s_{n})-J_{\rho_{j}}W_{n}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx\geq\varepsilon_{0}.

By
Proposition

B.8
, on a set
T_{n,\rho_{j}}\subset(s_{n},s_{n}+c\rho_{j}^{2})
of measure comparable to
\rho_{j}^{2}
, the unnormalized localized energy drops by at least
c\varepsilon_{0}m_{n}
, up to lower-order errors.

Since
s_{n}\in\mathcal{G}^{\sharp}_{\ell_{n}}
, the forward variance control (
B.42
) gives

\frac{1}{\rho_{j}^{2}}\int_{s_{n}}^{s_{n}+c\rho_{j}^{2}}\int\varphi\kappa^{n}(t)\,dx\,dt\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell_{n}^{2}.

Because
m_{n}\gg\ell_{n}^{\mu}
and
0<\mu<2
, this average is
o(m_{n})
. Thus one can choose
t_{n}\in T_{n,\rho_{j}}
for which

\int\varphi\kappa^{n}(t_{n})\,dx=o(m_{n}).

By
B.2
, the time can be chosen so that, in addition to belonging to the high-frequency drop set and satisfying the variance bound above,
t_{n}
is an admissible sharp comparison time for the same near-minimizing problem.

Using the same strict shadow
V^{n}
as competitor at time
t_{n}
gives

m_{n}\leq\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|U^{n}(t_{n})-V^{n}(t_{n})|^{2}\,dx+\int\varphi\kappa^{n}(t_{n})\,dx.

By the high-frequency drop and the almost minimality at
s_{n}
,

m_{n}\leq m_{n}-c\varepsilon_{0}m_{n}+o(m_{n}),

which is impossible. Therefore (
B.77
) holds.
∎

B.6.4
Normalized blow-up compactness and trace non-loss

Lemma B.4

(Normalized blow-up compactness)
.

Fix
0<\mu<1/6
. Let
\ell_{n}\downarrow 0
,
\delta_{n}\downarrow 0
, and set

\eta_{n}:=\ell_{n}^{\mu}+\ell_{n}^{-N}\delta_{n}^{b}.

Let

(U^{n},P^{n},\tau^{n})=(U^{\ell_{n}},P^{\ell_{n}},\tau^{\ell_{n}})

be prepared covariance-form trajectories satisfying
B.1
. Suppose that

\eta_{n}\to 0,\qquad m_{n}\to 0,\qquad\frac{m_{n}}{\eta_{n}}\to\infty,

and that there exist
s_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{\ell_{n}}
and
(V^{n},Q^{n})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
such that

\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|U^{n}(s_{n})-V^{n}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx+\int\varphi\kappa^{n}(s_{n})\,dx\leq m_{n}+o(m_{n}).

(B.78)

Assume, after passing to a subsequence, that

s_{n}\to s_{*}\in I_{-},\qquad(V^{n},Q^{n})\to(V,Q)

locally smoothly in
Q_{\rm sh}
, where
(V,Q)\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm sh})
. Define

W_{n}:=\frac{U^{n}-V^{n}}{m_{n}^{1/2}}.

Then, after passing to a subsequence,
W_{n}
is bounded in the local energy class on every compact subcylinder of
B_{\rm sh}\times(s_{*},0)
, converges strongly in
L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
, and the limit
W=(W_{h},0)
solves the homogeneous linearized strict system (
B.59
) around
(V,Q)
.

Proof.

Set
Z_{n}:=U^{n}-V^{n}=m_{n}^{1/2}W_{n}
. Subtracting the strict equation for
V^{n}
from the prepared equation for
U^{n}
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Z^{n}_{h}-\Delta Z^{n}_{h}

\displaystyle+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V^{n}_{h}\otimes Z^{n}_{h}+Z^{n}_{h}\otimes V^{n}_{h})+\nabla_{h}(P^{n}-Q^{n})

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{h}\cdot(Z^{n}_{h}\otimes Z^{n}_{h})-\nabla_{h}\cdot\tau^{n}+G^{n}_{\delta_{n}}.

Dividing by
m_{n}^{1/2}
gives the normalized equation. The variance-buffered relative energy inequality, divided by
m_{n}
, yields local energy bounds because (
B.78
) gives normalized initial energy
O(1)
and because

\frac{\ell_{n}^{1/6}}{m_{n}}\to 0,\qquad\frac{\|G^{n}_{\delta_{n}}\|_{Z^{\prime}}^{2}+\|\partial_{3}P^{n}\|_{Y^{\prime}}^{2}}{m_{n}}\to 0.

The automatic gradient buffer for strict shadows controls the transport terms. Aubin–Lions–Simon compactness gives strong local
L^{2}
convergence
[
9
]
after applying the horizontal Leray projection to obtain a negative Sobolev bound for
\partial_{t}W_{n}
.

It remains to pass to the limit in the normalized equation. The quadratic term vanishes because it is multiplied by
m_{n}^{1/2}
. The covariance term vanishes since

\frac{\|\tau^{n}\|_{L^{3/2}}}{m_{n}^{1/2}}\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell_{n}^{1/6-\mu/2}\left(\frac{\ell_{n}^{\mu}}{m_{n}}\right)^{1/2}\to 0.

The one-component residual and the vertical pressure-compatibility defect vanish because

\frac{\|G^{n}_{\delta_{n}}\|_{Z^{\prime}}+\|\partial_{3}P^{n}\|_{Y^{\prime}}}{m_{n}^{1/2}}\leq C_{M,\theta}\frac{\eta_{n}}{m_{n}^{1/2}}\to 0.

Since
V^{n}\to V
locally smoothly, the linear terms pass to the limit. De Rham pressure reconstruction gives a distribution
\Pi
, and the normalized vertical compatibility relation gives
\partial_{3}\Pi=0
. Thus
W
solves (
B.59
).
∎

Lemma B.5

(Strong trace non-loss)
.

Let
W_{n}
be the normalized blow-up sequence obtained in
Lemma

B.4
. Assume the trace-tightness conclusion (
B.77
). Suppose that

\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|W_{n}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx=1+o(1).

Then, after passing to a subsequence,
W
has a weighted
L^{2}
trace at
s_{*}
and

\varphi^{1/2}W_{n}(s_{n})\to\varphi^{1/2}W(s_{*})\quad\text{strongly in }L^{2}.

In particular,

\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=1.

(B.79)

Proof.

The trace-tightness estimate makes the sequence
\varphi^{1/2}W_{n}(s_{n})
precompact in
L^{2}
. Let its strong limit be
g_{*}
. The normalization gives
\|g_{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=2
. The negative Sobolev bound for
\partial_{t}W_{n}
identifies
g_{*}
with the weighted trace
\varphi^{1/2}W(s_{*})
by testing against smooth compactly supported functions and letting
t\downarrow s_{*}
. This proves the strong trace convergence and (
B.79
).
∎

B.6.5
First variation and conditional metric regularity

Lemma B.6

(First variation at the strict shadow trace class)
.

Let
W_{n}
be a normalized blow-up sequence satisfying the strong trace non-loss conclusion of
Lemma

B.5
. Suppose
(s_{n},V^{n},Q^{n})
is an
o(m_{n})
-minimizer in the sense that, for every strict competitor
(\widetilde{V},\widetilde{Q})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|U^{n}(s_{n})-V^{n}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx+\int\varphi\kappa^{n}(s_{n})\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|U^{n}(s_{n})-\widetilde{V}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx+\int\varphi\kappa^{n}(s_{n})\,dx+o(m_{n}).

Then
W(s_{*})
is orthogonal in
L^{2}_{\varphi}
to every integrable tangent trace. More precisely, if
Z(s_{*})
is the trace of an integrable strict tangent direction, then

\int\varphi W(s_{*})\cdot Z(s_{*})\,dx=0.

(B.80)

Proof.

Let
(V^{n,\varepsilon},Q^{n,\varepsilon})
be an exact strict-shadow curve with difference quotient converging to the tangent trace
Z(s_{*})
. Use the two competitors
\varepsilon_{n}=\pm\lambda m_{n}^{1/2}
, with fixed
\lambda>0
. The variance term is independent of the competitor and cancels. Expanding the square and dividing by
m_{n}
gives

0\leq-\lambda\int\varphi W(s_{*})\cdot Z(s_{*})\,dx+C_{Z}\lambda^{2}

from the positive perturbation and

0\leq\lambda\int\varphi W(s_{*})\cdot Z(s_{*})\,dx+C_{Z}\lambda^{2}

from the negative perturbation. Letting
\lambda\downarrow 0
gives (
B.80
).
∎

Theorem B.4

(Strict subcritical metric regularity under tangent-cone inclusion)
.

Assume
B.1
, the sharp admissible-time intersection hypothesis
B.2
, and the blow-up tangent-cone inclusion
B.2
. Fix
0<\mu<1/6
. Then the subcritical covariance-calibrated finite-power selection principle,
B.1
, holds.

Proof.

It is enough to prove the sharp estimate. Suppose it fails. Then there are sequences
\ell_{n}\downarrow 0
,
\delta_{n}\downarrow 0
such that, with
\eta_{n}=\ell_{n}^{\mu}+\ell_{n}^{-N}\delta_{n}^{b}
and
m_{n}=d^{\ell_{n},\sharp}_{\mathrm{cc},g}(U^{n},P^{n},\tau^{n})^{2}
, one has
m_{n}/\eta_{n}\to\infty
. The abstract sharp selection theorem gives
m_{n}\to 0
.

Choose sharp almost minimizers
s_{n}\in\mathcal{G}^{\sharp}_{\ell_{n}}
and
(V^{n},Q^{n})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
. Since
s_{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{\ell_{n}}
,

\int\varphi\kappa^{n}(s_{n})\,dx\leq C\ell_{n}^{2}=o(m_{n}),

and therefore

\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|W_{n}(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx=1+o(1).

By
Lemma

B.4
, after passing to a subsequence,
W_{n}\to W
locally and
W
solves the homogeneous linearized strict system. By
Theorem

B.3
and
Lemma

B.5
,

\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=1.

The first-variation lemma gives orthogonality of
W(s_{*})
to every integrable tangent trace. By the blow-up tangent-cone inclusion,
W(s_{*})
belongs to the closure of those traces. Hence

\int\varphi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=0,

contradicting the nonzero trace identity. Thus the failed-selection sequence cannot exist, and the sharp finite-power estimate holds. Since
\mathcal{G}_{\ell}^{\sharp}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
up to constants,
B.1
follows.
∎

Corollary B.4

(Regular-stratum strict metric regularity)
.

Assume
B.1
and
B.2
. Suppose every failed sharp finite-power selection blow-up has a fully regular limiting strict shadow in the sense of
Definition

B.4
. Then
B.1
holds, and hence the logarithmic harmonic-pressure approximation and logarithmic CKN radius bound of
Corollaries

B.2
and
B.3
hold.

Proof.

By
Proposition

B.6
, fully fully regular limiting strict shadows satisfy the blow-up tangent-cone inclusion. The conclusion follows from
Theorem

B.4
and the implications proved in
Section

B.5
.
∎

Corollary B.5

(Reduction to singular-stratum curve selection)
.

Assume
B.1
and
B.2
and the singular-stratum curve-selection principle
B.3
. Then
B.1
holds.

Proof.

Let a failed sharp finite-power selection sequence be given. If the limiting strict shadow is regular, the tangent-cone inclusion follows from
Proposition

B.6
. If it lies on a singular stratum,
Proposition

B.7
gives the quadratic compatibility condition, and
B.3
gives the tangent-cone inclusion. Hence
B.2
holds in all cases. Apply
Theorem

B.4
.
∎

Remark B.15

(Remaining input in the strict-shadow reduction)
.

The normalized covariance stress, the localized variance cutoff error, the one-component residual, and the vertical pressure-compatibility defect vanish at the subcritical scale. The trace-tightness step is conditional on the sharp admissible-time intersection input isolated above; with that input in place, the remaining geometric issue is
B.3
: one must solve the all-order curve-selection problem for singular strata of the nonlinear pressure-compatibility constraint. This is weaker and more accurate than asking every solution of the formal linearized strict system to be integrable.

Remark B.16

(Possible relaxed-shadow alternative)
.

The strict tangent-cone problem is difficult because of the pressure compatibility condition
\partial_{3}Q=0
. A possible alternative is to work with a relaxed no-stretching class in which
V=(V_{h},0)
and
\nabla_{h}\cdot V_{h}=0
, but the comparison pressure is allowed to satisfy
\partial_{3}\pi\neq 0
. The resulting vertical residual would then pair with the small component
u_{3}
in the relative energy identity. This relaxed alternative may avoid the strict shadow manifold problem, at the cost of a different pressure reconstruction argument.

B.7
Role in the final dependency chain

The conclusions of this paper can be summarized in four layers.

First, the compactness-level strict harmonic projection can be upgraded to a good-time covariance-calibrated selection with an abstract modulus. The key correction is to restrict the selection time to a set where the unresolved variance is controlled. This avoids the flaw of minimizing over unrestricted times. The thick good-time refinement
\mathcal{G}_{\ell}^{\sharp}
is also unconditional: it has positive measure and controls the variance on short forward time intervals.

Second, the finite-power good-time selection needed for a logarithmic theorem should be formulated with a subcritical power
\ell^{\mu}
,
0<\mu<1/6
, rather than with the stronger scale
\ell^{2}
. This modification is enough for the logarithmic optimization and is compatible with the available covariance estimates. Thus
B.1
is the single finite-rate gate for the logarithmic theorem.

Third, conditional on the subcritical finite-power covariance-calibrated selection principle, the variance-buffered relative entropy estimate gives the separated bound

X^{\mathrm{har}}_{\theta/4}(u,p;M)\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{a_{*}}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N_{*}}e^{C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N_{*}}}\delta^{b_{*}},

and optimizing in
\ell
yields the logarithmic harmonic-pressure approximation theorem. The harmonic-pressure comparison step, based on the local pressure decomposition and CKN smallness criterion, then gives

\Psi(r)\leq C_{M,\theta}r+C_{M,\theta}r^{-2}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma},

and hence the finite-scale radius lower bound

r_{\mathrm{reg}}(0,0)\geq c_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma/3}.

This implication is fully established in
Section

B.5
, assuming the finite-power selection principle.

Fourth, the corrected strict-shadow reduction to the finite-power selection principle has now been decomposed more sharply. The high-frequency trace drop argument proves trace tightness for sharp good-time almost minimizers once the sharp admissible-time intersection input is imposed. Therefore trace non-loss is no longer an independent compactness assumption beyond that explicit intersection hypothesis in the sharp argument. The regular-stratum tangent-cone inclusion is also proved by an implicit-function theorem for the pressure-compatibility map. In addition, every failed-selection blow-up direction satisfies the quadratic compatibility relation

\mathfrak{B}(W,W)\in\overline{\operatorname{Range}(D\mathfrak{C}_{V})}.

Thus the only remaining strict-shadow input is the singular-stratum curve-selection problem: one must prove that quadratic-compatible formal blow-up directions can be realized by exact strict-shadow curves.

The strict pressure condition
\partial_{3}Q=0
is the essential geometric difficulty. It is equivalent, modulo horizontal harmonic functions, to the nonlinear pressure-compatibility constraint

\mathfrak{C}(V)=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b})=0.

Consequently, the strict shadow trace class may have singular strata. Formal solutions of the linearized strict system need not all be integrable strict tangent directions. The correct remaining problem is therefore not ordinary tangent-space integrability, but curve selection in the tangent-cone sense for the singular strata of this nonlinear compatibility constraint.

Thus Appendix
B
reduces the full logarithmic finite-scale theorem to the following precise geometric-analytic problem:

prove singular-stratum strict curve selection for quadratic-compatible blow-up directions.

If this input is resolved,
B.1
follows by
Corollary

B.5
, and the logarithmic one-component regularity theorem follows by the chain established in
Section

B.5
. If the singular curve-selection problem is obstructed, the relaxed-shadow alternative described in
Remark

B.16
provides a natural alternative program.

Appendix C
Singular-stratum geometry and finite-mode flat branches

C.1
Introduction

This appendix is the singular-geometry component of a finite-scale logarithmic one-component regularity
program for suitable weak solutions of the three-dimensional Navier–Stokes equations,
in the local energy framework initiated by Leray, Hopf, and Caffarelli–Kohn–Nirenberg

[
1
,
2
,
3
,
4
,
5
,
7
,
10
,
13
,
29
]
.
The final target is a logarithmic lower bound for the local regularity radius under a
scale-invariant bound and smallness of one critical component:

\Phi(1)\leq M,\qquad C_{3}(1)=\delta\ll 1\quad\Longrightarrow\quad r_{\rm reg}(0,0)\geq c_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma}.

(C.1)

In the formulation of Appendix
B
, after the harmonic-pressure comparison scale is
optimized, the radius bound appears as

r_{\rm reg}(0,0)\geq c_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma/3}.

The value of
\sigma>0
may change from line to line.

In the background of one-component regularity criteria
[
30
,
31
,
35
,
37
]
, Appendix
A
constructs a low-frequency prepared horizontal trajectory

U^{\ell}=(U_{h}^{\ell},0),\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot U_{h}^{\ell}=0,

from a suitable weak solution with small vertical component. The prepared trajectory
satisfies a covariance-form approximate strict equation, whose commutator structure is in the spirit of Onsager-type coarse-graining identities
[
11
]
,

\partial_{t}U_{h}^{\ell}-\Delta U_{h}^{\ell}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(U_{h}^{\ell}\otimes U_{h}^{\ell})+\nabla_{h}P^{\ell}=-\nabla_{h}\cdot\tau^{\ell}+G_{\delta}^{\ell},

(C.2)

where

\tau^{\ell}=S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})-S_{\ell}u_{h}\otimes S_{\ell}u_{h},\qquad\kappa^{\ell}=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\tau^{\ell}.

The unresolved variance
\kappa^{\ell}
cancels the dangerous covariance term in the
localized relative-energy identity. This produces a variance-corrected slice energy

E_{\varphi}^{\ell}(s;U^{\ell},V):=\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|U^{\ell}(s)-V(s)|^{2}\,dx+\int\varphi\kappa^{\ell}(s)\,dx.

(C.3)

Appendix
B
shows that the logarithmic theorem is reduced to a subcritical finite-power
selection principle: for some
0<\mu<1/6
, one should select a good time
s_{\ell}

and a strict shadow
(V^{\ell},Q^{\ell})
such that

E_{\varphi}^{\ell}(s_{\ell};U^{\ell},V^{\ell})\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}.

(C.4)

The stronger target scale
\ell^{2}
is stronger than needed. Any positive subcritical
power is enough for logarithmic optimization, and the restriction
\mu<1/6
makes
the normalized covariance and cutoff errors vanish in the blow-up proof.

Appendix
B
proves the implication, in the same finite-scale quantitative spirit as recent concentration and weak–strong approaches
[
36
,
38
]
,

\eqref{pIII:eq:finite-power-selection-intro}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad X^{\rm har}_{\theta/4}(u,p;M)\leq C_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad r_{\rm reg}(0,0)\geq c_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma/3}.

(C.5)

Thus the remaining structural point is the proof of (
C.4
). A
failed-selection sequence leads to normalized differences

U^{n}=V^{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},

where
m_{n}
is the squared sharp distance to the strict shadow trace class. After
passing to the limit,
W_{n}\to W
, and
W
solves the linearized strict system around
the limiting strict shadow
V
. The first-variation argument then gives orthogonality
of
W(s_{*})
to all integrable strict tangent traces. To obtain a contradiction from
the nonzero trace identity, one needs

W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}.

(C.6)

This tangent-cone inclusion is the target of Appendix
C
.

Role of this appendix

The strict shadow class is constrained by

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}V_{h}-\Delta V_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}Q=0,\\
&\nabla_{h}\!\cdot V_{h}=0,\\
&\partial_{3}Q=0,\\
&V=(V_{h},0).\end{aligned}\right.

(C.7)

Taking the horizontal divergence gives

-\Delta_{h}Q=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b}),\qquad a,b\in\{1,2\}.

Thus, modulo horizontal harmonic pressure functions, the strict vertical pressure
condition is encoded by the nonlinear compatibility map

\mathfrak{C}(V):=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b})=0.

(C.8)

The zero set of
\mathfrak{C}
may have singular strata. At regular points, tangent lifting
is an implicit-function theorem. At the zero shadow,
D\mathfrak{C}_{0}=0
, and a different
mechanism is required.

The main addition of this paper is the zero-shadow jet construction. The quadratic condition

\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0
gives only the first nontrivial compatibility. The exact curve
selection problem produces an all-order hierarchy

\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(R_{i},R_{j})=0,\qquad k\geq 2,\qquad R_{1}=W.

(C.9)

We prove that for blow-up directions arising from failed selection, no finite-order
obstruction in (
C.9
) can be the first genuine obstruction. At a
finite order
K
, either sharpness fails and one already obtains a positive finite-power
selection estimate, or sharpness holds and the
K
-th obstruction is inherited as a
vanishing coefficient in the pressure-compatibility expansion. If this continues to all
orders, an analytic majorant argument sums the compatible jets to an exact strict shadow
curve through the zero shadow.

For nonzero singular strata, the tame finite-codimensional case is handled by a
moving-base Lyapunov–Schmidt scheme. The non-tame branch is treated by a different
finite-mode mechanism. Instead of trying to prove a global finite stratification of

\mathfrak{C}^{-1}(0)
, we project the compatibility defect onto finite-dimensional
compatibility spaces. This avoids relying on a global analytic or subanalytic stratification theorem, although finite-dimensional analogues motivate the terminology
[
40
,
43
,
44
]
. If a finite-mode obstruction is visible, the pressure-compatibility
expansion gives a finite-power selection estimate. If no finite-mode obstruction is
visible, the branch is finite-mode flat. Combining finite-mode flatness with a Sobolev
tail estimate and moving-base jet extraction gives metric tangent-cone closure, under the
analytic trace-flatness and majorant inputs stated below.

Technical organization

Section

C.2
defines the compatibility map, strict trace class, and integrable
tangent cone.
Section

C.3
recalls the output of Appendix
B
.
Section

C.4

studies the zero shadow and explains the obstruction hierarchy.
Section

C.5

proves third-order, fourth-order, and finite-order obstruction inheritance from sharpened
failed-selection blow-up.
Section

C.6
states the iterated finite-order jet
extraction lemma.
Section

C.7
sums compatible jets and closes the zero-shadow
branch.
Section

C.8
records the tame moving-base Lyapunov–Schmidt
reduction.
Section

C.9
develops the finite-mode dichotomy for
non-tame branches.
Section

C.10
proves the high-frequency tail
estimate and states the finite-order moving-base realizer input.
Section

C.11

and
Section

C.12
exactify finite-mode flat branches.

Section

C.13
explains how these alternatives feed back into the Appendix
B

logarithmic theorem.

C.2
The strict pressure-compatibility constraint

This section records the geometric object left by Appendix
B
. The harmonic-pressure quotient
and the singular-integral operators used below are understood in the standard local pressure
and Calderon–Zygmund framework
[
Stein1970
,
29
,
34
]
. We work on fixed interior
cylinders and suppress harmless localization choices. All pressure statements are
understood modulo horizontal harmonic functions.

C.2.1
Compatibility map and bilinear form

Let
Y=(Y_{h},0)
, where
Y_{h}=(Y_{1},Y_{2})
and
\nabla_{h}\!\cdot Y_{h}=0
. Define

\mathfrak{C}(Y):=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(Y_{a}Y_{b}),\qquad a,b\in\{1,2\}.

(C.10)

We also define the associated symmetric bilinear form

\mathfrak{B}(A,B):=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(A_{a}B_{b}).

(C.11)

Then

\mathfrak{C}(Y)=\mathfrak{B}(Y,Y).

(C.12)

The strict pressure condition
\partial_{3}Q=0
is equivalent, modulo horizontal
harmonic ambiguity, to

\mathfrak{C}(V)=0.

(C.13)

At a strict shadow
V
, the differential of
\mathfrak{C}
is

D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[W]=\mathfrak{B}(V,W)+\mathfrak{B}(W,V).

(C.14)

Every integrable strict tangent direction at
V
must satisfy

D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[W]=0,

(C.15)

which is the compatibility form of the linearized strict pressure condition

\partial_{3}\Pi=0
.

C.2.2
Strict trace class and integrable tangent cone

For a time slice
s
, define the localized strict trace class

\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s}:=\{V(s):(V,Q)\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm str})\}.

(C.16)

Definition C.1

(Integrable tangent cone)
.

Let
(V,Q)\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm str})
. A trace
Z(s)
belongs to the localized
integrable tangent cone
T^{\rm int}_{V(s)}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s}
if there exist exact strict shadows

(V^{\varepsilon},Q^{\varepsilon})\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm str})
and a sequence
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
such that

\frac{V^{\varepsilon}(s)-V(s)}{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow Z(s)\quad\text{strongly in }L^{2}_{\varphi}.

(C.17)

If the convergence also holds in the local energy topology on a time interval, then

Z
is called an integrable strict tangent field.

The first-variation argument from Appendix
B
needs the normalized blow-up direction

W(s_{*})
to belong to this cone. A formal solution of the linearized strict system is
not enough.

C.2.3
Formal tangent space versus tangent cone

The formal linearized strict system around a strict shadow
(V,Q)
is

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}W_{h}-\Delta W_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes W_{h}+W_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi=0,\\
&\nabla_{h}\!\cdot W_{h}=0,\\
&\partial_{3}\Pi=0,\\
&W=(W_{h},0).\end{aligned}\right.

(C.18)

The implication

W\text{ solves }\eqref{pIII:eq:linearized-strict}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}

is false without further structure. At a regular point of
\mathfrak{C}^{-1}(0)
, it follows
from an implicit-function theorem. At singular points, one must solve a curve-selection
problem.

C.3
Tangent-cone input inherited from the selection reduction

We recall the precise output of Appendix
B
in the language of the present paper.

Theorem C.1

(Selection reduction)
.

Assume that the following two statements hold for every failed sharp finite-power
selection sequence:

(a)

the high-frequency trace drop, together with the sharp admissible-time intersection input, gives trace tightness and hence strong trace non-loss;

(b)

the normalized blow-up direction
W(s_{*})
belongs to

T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}
.

Then the subcritical finite-power selection estimate (
C.4
)
holds. Consequently, the logarithmic harmonic-pressure approximation and the logarithmic
regularity-radius lower bound (
C.5
) hold.

The high-frequency trace drop is an analytic parabolic estimate. The present paper
focuses on the second statement. Appendix
B
also shows that the blow-up directions are not
arbitrary formal linearized directions: they satisfy a quadratic compatibility condition.

Proposition C.1

(Projected quadratic compatibility inherited from failed selection)
.

Let
W
be a normalized blow-up limit generated by a failed sharp finite-power
selection sequence. Then, for every fixed finite-stage projection
P_{N}
,

P_{N}\mathfrak{B}(W,W)\in\overline{P_{N}\Range(D\mathfrak{C}_{V})}

(C.19)

in the finite-dimensional projected compatibility-defect topology. In particular, if the relevant projected range is trivial and the moving-base contribution vanishes, then

P_{N}\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0.

(C.20)

Formal derivation.

Write

U^{n}=V^{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2}.

The horizontal divergence of the prepared equation gives, at the level of the horizontal
pressure,

-\Delta_{h}P^{n}=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(U^{n}_{a}U^{n}_{b})+\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\tau^{n}_{ab}+\operatorname{div}_{h}G^{n}_{\delta_{n}},

while the exact strict shadow satisfies

-\Delta_{h}Q^{n}=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V^{n}_{a}V^{n}_{b}),\qquad\partial_{3}Q^{n}=0.

Subtracting, expanding
U^{n}=V^{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n}
, and applying

\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}
, one obtains

D\mathfrak{C}_{V^{n}}[W_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})=\text{terms controlled by }\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}\partial_{3}P^{n},\ \varepsilon_{n}^{-1}\tau^{n},\ \varepsilon_{n}^{-1}G^{n}_{\delta_{n}}.

Dividing by
\varepsilon_{n}
, using
\varepsilon_{n}^{2}=m_{n}
, the estimates

\|\partial_{3}P^{n}\|_{Y^{\prime}}+\|G^{n}_{\delta_{n}}\|_{Z^{\prime}}\lesssim\eta_{n},\qquad\eta_{n}/m_{n}\to 0,

and the subcritical covariance smallness gives

\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}D\mathfrak{C}_{V^{n}}[W_{n}]+\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})\to 0.

Passing to the limit after applying any fixed finite-stage projection gives (
C.19
). Only under the additional trivial projected-range and vanishing moving-base assumptions does (
C.20
) follow.
∎

Remark C.1
.

The conclusion of
Proposition

C.1
is necessary for curve
selection. It is not, by itself, a full proof of curve selection. The rest of this paper
explains the additional regularity, transversality, or all-order jet structure needed to
turn this compatibility into exact strict-shadow curves.

C.4
The zero shadow

The most important singular point is the zero strict shadow. At
V=0
, one has

D\mathfrak{C}_{0}=0.

Thus the usual implicit-function theorem at the base point gives no information. However,
the constraint is homogeneous quadratic, which makes the correct rescaled equation and jet
hierarchy transparent.

C.4.1
Quadratic cone at the zero shadow

At
V=0
, the compatibility constraint is

\mathfrak{C}(Y)=\mathfrak{B}(Y,Y)=0.

(C.21)

If
W
satisfies
\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0
, then

\mathfrak{C}(\varepsilon W)=\varepsilon^{2}\mathfrak{C}(W)=0.

(C.22)

Thus, from the pressure-compatibility point of view alone,
\varepsilon W
lies exactly on the
quadratic cone. The remaining structural point is the nonlinear strict evolution.

Set

V^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon Y^{\varepsilon},\qquad Q^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\Pi^{\varepsilon}.

(C.23)

Then the strict equation is equivalent to

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}Y_{h}^{\varepsilon}-\Delta Y_{h}^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon\nabla_{h}\cdot(Y_{h}^{\varepsilon}\otimes Y_{h}^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{h}\Pi^{\varepsilon}=0,\\
&\nabla_{h}\!\cdot Y_{h}^{\varepsilon}=0,\\
&\partial_{3}\Pi^{\varepsilon}=0,\\
&\mathfrak{C}(Y^{\varepsilon})=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(C.24)

At
\varepsilon=0
, this reduces to the linearized strict system around zero together with the
quadratic cone condition.

C.4.2
Second-order lift and first obstruction

Let
W=(W_{h},0)
solve the zero-shadow linearized strict system

\partial_{t}W_{h}-\Delta W_{h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{1}=0,\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot W_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi_{1}=0.

(C.25)

Assume the quadratic compatibility condition

\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0.

(C.26)

Define the canonical second-order correction
(R_{2},\Pi_{2})
by

\partial_{t}R_{2,h}-\Delta R_{2,h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{2}=-\nabla_{h}\cdot(W_{h}\otimes W_{h}),\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot R_{2,h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi_{2}=0,

(C.27)

with zero lower-time trace in the localized Stokes construction. The pressure condition

\partial_{3}\Pi_{2}=0
is precisely (
C.26
): after taking the horizontal
divergence in (
C.27
), one obtains

-\Delta_{h}\Pi_{2}=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(W_{a}W_{b}),

and applying
\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}
gives
\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0
.

Thus

V^{\varepsilon}_{(2)}=\varepsilon W+\varepsilon^{2}R_{2},\qquad Q^{\varepsilon}_{(2)}=\varepsilon\Pi_{1}+\varepsilon^{2}\Pi_{2}

is a second-order strict lift. Its compatibility defect begins at the next order:

\mathfrak{C}(V^{\varepsilon}_{(2)})=2\varepsilon^{3}\mathfrak{B}(W,R_{2})+\varepsilon^{4}\mathfrak{B}(R_{2},R_{2}).

(C.28)

The first genuine new obstruction is therefore

\mathfrak{O}_{3}(W):=2\mathfrak{B}(W,R_{2}).

(C.29)

C.4.3
All-order strict jet hierarchy

Set
R_{1}=W
. We seek a formal strict curve

V^{\varepsilon}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{k}R_{k},\qquad Q^{\varepsilon}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{k}\Pi_{k}.

(C.30)

The coefficient equations are

\partial_{t}R_{k,h}-\Delta R_{k,h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{k}=-\sum_{i+j=k}\nabla_{h}\cdot(R_{i,h}\otimes R_{j,h}),\qquad k\geq 2,

(C.31)

with

\nabla_{h}\!\cdot R_{k,h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi_{k}=0.

(C.32)

The strict pressure compatibility at order
k
is

\mathfrak{O}_{k}:=\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(R_{i},R_{j})=0,\qquad k\geq 2.

(C.33)

The first orders are

\mathfrak{O}_{2}=\mathfrak{B}(W,W),\qquad\mathfrak{O}_{3}=2\mathfrak{B}(W,R_{2}),

\mathfrak{O}_{4}=2\mathfrak{B}(W,R_{3})+\mathfrak{B}(R_{2},R_{2}).

Thus zero-shadow curve selection is an all-order compatibility and summability problem.

C.4.4
Directional regularity as a sufficient condition

Definition C.2

(Directional regularity at zero)
.

Let
W
solve (
C.25
) and satisfy
\mathfrak{C}(W)=0
. We say that

W
is directionally regular if the operator

(R,\pi)\mapsto\left(\partial_{t}R_{h}-\Delta R_{h}+\nabla_{h}\pi,\nabla_{h}\!\cdot R_{h},\partial_{3}\pi,D\mathfrak{C}_{W}[R],R(t_{0})\right)

(C.34)

has a bounded right inverse between the localized parabolic spaces under consideration,
for a lower time
t_{0}<s_{*}
.

Proposition C.2

(Singular base, regular direction curve selection)
.

Let
V=0
, and let
W
solve (
C.25
). Assume

\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0
, and assume that
W
is directionally regular in the sense of

Definition

C.2
. Then

W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{0}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}.

Proof.

Define

\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon}(Y,\Pi):=\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{t}Y_{h}-\Delta Y_{h}+\varepsilon\nabla_{h}\cdot(Y_{h}\otimes Y_{h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi\\
\nabla_{h}\!\cdot Y_{h}\\
\partial_{3}\Pi\\
\mathfrak{C}(Y)\\
Y(t_{0})-W(t_{0})\end{pmatrix}.

Then
\mathcal{G}_{0}(W,\Pi_{1})=0
, and the derivative of
\mathcal{G}_{0}
at
(W,\Pi_{1})

is precisely (
C.34
). The right-inverse assumption and the
Banach implicit-function theorem give a branch
(Y^{\varepsilon},\Pi^{\varepsilon})=(W,\Pi_{1})+O(\varepsilon)

solving
\mathcal{G}_{\varepsilon}(Y^{\varepsilon},\Pi^{\varepsilon})=0
. Setting

V^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon Y^{\varepsilon}
and
Q^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\Pi^{\varepsilon}
gives exact strict shadows with

V^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon\to W
. Hence
W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{0}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}
.
∎

Remark C.2
.

The proposition gives one clean sufficient condition. The rest of the zero-shadow analysis
below follows a different construction tailored to failed-selection blow-ups: instead of assuming
directional regularity of
W
, it extracts all compatible jets from the failed-selection
sequence itself.

C.5
Obstruction inheritance from sharpened zero-shadow blow-up

We now show how the failed-selection origin of
W
eliminates finite-order obstructions.
The point is not that
\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0
implies all higher identities for arbitrary
W
.
That statement is false in general. The point is that a genuine failed-selection sequence
which remains sharper than every positive finite-power alternative must encode the higher
identities in its own pressure-compatibility expansion.

C.5.1
Zero-shadow normalized sequence and sharpness

In the zero-shadow branch, after recentering at the zero strict shadow, write

U_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}:=m_{n}^{1/2}\downarrow 0.

(C.35)

Let

\rho_{n}:=\ell_{n}^{1/6}+\ell_{n}^{-N}\delta_{n}^{b}

(C.36)

collect the raw covariance, one-component, and vertical pressure-compatibility defects.
The normalized approximate equation has the form

\partial_{t}W_{n,h}-\Delta W_{n,h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{n}=-\varepsilon_{n}\nabla_{h}\cdot(W_{n,h}\otimes W_{n,h})+\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}{\rm Raw}_{n},

(C.37)

with

\|{\rm Raw}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}\lesssim\rho_{n}.

(C.38)

The pressure-compatibility identity reads

\mathfrak{C}(U_{n})=\mathcal{E}_{n},\qquad\|\mathcal{E}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{Y}}\lesssim\rho_{n}.

(C.39)

Since
U_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}W_{n}
and
\mathfrak{C}(Y)=\mathfrak{B}(Y,Y)
, this is

\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})=\mathcal{E}_{n}.

(C.40)

For an integer
K\geq 2
, we say that the sequence is
K
-sharp if

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K}).

(C.41)

If
K
-sharpness fails, then along a subsequence

\varepsilon_{n}^{K}\lesssim\rho_{n}.

Since
m_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
, this gives

m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K}\lesssim\ell_{n}^{1/(3K)}+\ell_{n}^{-2N/K}\delta_{n}^{2b/K},

(C.42)

which is already a positive finite-power selection estimate. Thus a true failed-selection
sequence must be arbitrarily sharp at every finite order.

C.5.2
Third-order obstruction

Proposition C.3

(Third-order zero-shadow obstruction inherited from sharp blow-up)
.

Assume that the zero-shadow sequence is third-order sharp:

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{3}).

Assume

W_{n}\to W

in the local energy topology, where
W
solves the zero-shadow linearized strict system.
Let
R_{2}
be the local energy limit of

H_{n}:=\frac{W_{n}-W}{\varepsilon_{n}}.

(C.43)

Then
R_{2}
solves (
C.27
), and

\mathfrak{B}(W,R_{2})=0.

(C.44)

Proof.

Subtract the limiting linear equation for
W
from (
C.37
) and divide
by
\varepsilon_{n}
. We obtain

\partial_{t}H_{n,h}-\Delta H_{n,h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{2,n}=-\nabla_{h}\cdot(W_{n,h}\otimes W_{n,h})+\varepsilon_{n}^{-2}{\rm Raw}_{n}.

Since
\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{3})
, the last term tends to zero. Compactness gives

H_{n}\to R_{2}
, and passing to the limit gives (
C.27
). The pressure
condition
\partial_{3}\Pi_{2}=0
follows from the already inherited quadratic compatibility

\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0
.

It remains to prove (
C.44
). By (
C.40
) and third-order
sharpness,

\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})=o(\varepsilon_{n}).

Using
W_{n}=W+\varepsilon_{n}H_{n}
, we expand

\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})=\mathfrak{B}(W,W)+2\varepsilon_{n}\mathfrak{B}(W,H_{n})+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\mathfrak{B}(H_{n},H_{n}).

The first term is zero. Dividing by
\varepsilon_{n}
and letting
n\to\infty
gives

2\mathfrak{B}(W,R_{2})=0.

∎

C.5.3
Fourth-order obstruction

Proposition C.4

(Fourth-order zero-shadow obstruction inherited from sharp blow-up)
.

Assume that the zero-shadow sequence is fourth-order sharp:

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{4}).

Assume the third-order identity

\mathfrak{B}(W,W)=0,\qquad\mathfrak{B}(W,R_{2})=0,

and suppose that

K_{n}:=\frac{W_{n}-W-\varepsilon_{n}R_{2}}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}}

(C.45)

converges in the local energy topology to
R_{3}
. Then
R_{3}
solves

\partial_{t}R_{3,h}-\Delta R_{3,h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{3}=-\nabla_{h}\cdot(W_{h}\otimes R_{2,h}+R_{2,h}\otimes W_{h}),

(C.46)

with

\nabla_{h}\!\cdot R_{3,h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi_{3}=0,

and the fourth-order obstruction vanishes:

2\mathfrak{B}(W,R_{3})+\mathfrak{B}(R_{2},R_{2})=0.

(C.47)

Proof.

Subtract the equations for
W
and
\varepsilon_{n}R_{2}
from (
C.37
), and divide
by
\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
. This gives

\partial_{t}K_{n,h}-\Delta K_{n,h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{3,n}=-\nabla_{h}\cdot\left(\frac{W_{n}\otimes W_{n}-W\otimes W}{\varepsilon_{n}}\right)+\varepsilon_{n}^{-3}{\rm Raw}_{n}.

Fourth-order sharpness gives
\varepsilon_{n}^{-3}{\rm Raw}_{n}\to 0
. Since

W_{n}=W+\varepsilon_{n}R_{2}+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}K_{n},

we have

\frac{W_{n}\otimes W_{n}-W\otimes W}{\varepsilon_{n}}\to W\otimes R_{2}+R_{2}\otimes W.

Passing to the limit gives (
C.46
). The strict pressure condition

\partial_{3}\Pi_{3}=0
is equivalent to
2\mathfrak{B}(W,R_{2})=0
, which is already known.

For the obstruction identity, fourth-order sharpness and (
C.40
) give

\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{2}).

Expanding

W_{n}=W+\varepsilon_{n}R_{2}+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}K_{n}

yields

\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})=\mathfrak{B}(W,W)+2\varepsilon_{n}\mathfrak{B}(W,R_{2})+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\bigl(2\mathfrak{B}(W,K_{n})+\mathfrak{B}(R_{2},R_{2})\bigr)+O(\varepsilon_{n}^{3}).

The lower-order obstructions vanish. Dividing by
\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
and letting
n\to\infty

gives (
C.47
).
∎

C.5.4
Finite-order obstruction inheritance

Proposition C.5

(Finite-order zero-shadow obstruction inheritance)
.

Fix
K\geq 2
. Assume that the zero-shadow sequence is
K
-sharp and that jets

R_{1}=W,R_{2},\ldots,R_{K-1}
have been extracted so that, for each
1\leq q\leq K-1
,

W_{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\varepsilon_{n}^{j-1}R_{j}+o(\varepsilon_{n}^{q-1})

(C.48)
