in the topology needed to test
\mathfrak{B}
. Then the compatibility identities

\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(R_{i},R_{j})=0,\qquad 2\leq k\leq K,

(C.49)

hold.

Proof.

By
K
-sharpness and (
C.40
),

\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K-2}).

For each
2\leq k\leq K
, substitute the expansion (
C.48
) into

\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})
. The coefficient of
\varepsilon_{n}^{k-2}
is exactly

\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(R_{i},R_{j}).

Since the full expression is
o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K-2})
, all coefficients through order
K-2

vanish, which proves (
C.49
).
∎

Corollary C.1

(Finite-order zero-shadow dichotomy)
.

Let
K\geq 2
. For a zero-shadow sharp failed-selection sequence, one of the following
alternatives holds.

First alternative:

K
-sharpness fails. Then, along a subsequence,

m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K}\lesssim\ell_{n}^{1/(3K)}+\ell_{n}^{-2N/K}\delta_{n}^{2b/K},

and a positive finite-power selection estimate already follows.

Second alternative:
the sequence is
K
-sharp. Then the
K
-th compatibility
obstruction is inherited by the blow-up jets:

\sum_{i+j=K}\mathfrak{B}(R_{i},R_{j})=0.

Consequently, no finite-order pressure-compatibility obstruction can be the first
obstruction to zero-shadow curve selection.

C.6
Finite-order jet extraction at the zero shadow

We now record the finite-order compactness statement needed for the zero-shadow jet construction.
Its purpose is to make precise the iterative replacement for the first-order normalized
compactness used in Appendix
B
.

Let

Q_{\rm in}\Subset Q_{\rm mid}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}

be fixed interior cylinders. We write

\mathcal{E}(Q_{\rm mid}):=L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(Q_{\rm mid})\cap L^{2}_{t}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}(Q_{\rm mid})

for the local energy class, and
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(Q_{\rm mid})
denotes the corresponding
localized energy-dual residual space. The compatibility-defect space is denoted by

\mathcal{Y}(Q_{\rm mid})
.

Lemma C.1

(Iterated zero-shadow jet extraction)
.

Fix
K\geq 2
. Assume the zero-shadow sequence is
K
-sharp. Assume also the
finite-order trace-flatness estimates

\sup_{n}\left\|\varepsilon_{n}^{1-q}\left(W_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{q-1}\varepsilon_{n}^{j-1}R_{j}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{E}(Q_{\rm mid})}\leq C_{q},\qquad 1\leq q\leq K-1,

(C.50)

where the empty sum is interpreted as zero for
q=1
. Then, after passing to a
subsequence, there exist fields
R_{1},\ldots,R_{K-1}
and pressures

\Pi_{1},\ldots,\Pi_{K-1}
such that
R_{1}=W
and, for every
1\leq q\leq K-1
,

\varepsilon_{n}^{1-q}\left(W_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{q-1}\varepsilon_{n}^{j-1}R_{j}\right)\longrightarrow R_{q}

(C.51)

strongly in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}(Q_{\rm in})
, weakly in the local energy class, and in the
localized trace topology.

Moreover, the jets satisfy

\partial_{t}R_{1,h}-\Delta R_{1,h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{1}=0,\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot R_{1,h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi_{1}=0,

(C.52)

and, for
2\leq q\leq K-1
,

\partial_{t}R_{q,h}-\Delta R_{q,h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{q}=-\sum_{i+j=q}\nabla_{h}\cdot(R_{i,h}\otimes R_{j,h}),

(C.53)

\nabla_{h}\!\cdot R_{q,h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi_{q}=0.

(C.54)

Finally, the pressure-compatibility obstructions vanish up to order
K
:

\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(R_{i},R_{j})=0,\qquad 2\leq k\leq K.

(C.55)

Proof.

We argue by induction. For
q=1
, (
C.50
) gives a uniform local
energy bound for
W_{n}
. Equation (
C.37
) and the first-order sharpness
consequence

\|\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}{\rm Raw}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}\lesssim\rho_{n}/\varepsilon_{n}=o(1)

give a uniform negative Sobolev bound for
\partial_{t}W_{n}
. The Aubin–Lions–Simon
compactness lemma gives, after passing to a subsequence,

W_{n}\to R_{1}=:W

strongly in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}(Q_{\rm in})
and weakly in the local energy class. Passing to
the limit in (
C.37
) gives (
C.52
).

Assume that
R_{1},\ldots,R_{q-1}
have been extracted for some
2\leq q\leq K-1
, and set

P^{(q-1)}_{n}:=\sum_{j=1}^{q-1}\varepsilon_{n}^{j-1}R_{j},\qquad Y^{(q)}_{n}:=\varepsilon_{n}^{1-q}(W_{n}-P^{(q-1)}_{n}).

By (
C.50
),
Y^{(q)}_{n}
is bounded in
\mathcal{E}(Q_{\rm mid})
.
Subtract from (
C.37
) the equations for the partial jet sum

P^{(q-1)}_{n}
. Using the induction hypothesis and dividing by
\varepsilon_{n}^{q-1}
, we get

\partial_{t}Y^{(q)}_{n,h}-\Delta Y^{(q)}_{n,h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi^{(q)}_{n}=-\sum_{i+j=q}\nabla_{h}\cdot(R_{i,h}\otimes R_{j,h})+\mathcal{R}^{(q)}_{n},

(C.56)

where

\mathcal{R}^{(q)}_{n}\to 0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}(Q_{\rm in}).

Indeed, every unaccounted quadratic term contains a positive power of
\varepsilon_{n}
, while the
raw residual contributes
\varepsilon_{n}^{-q}{\rm Raw}_{n}
, which tends to zero because

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K})
and
q\leq K-1
.

The energy bound for
Y^{(q)}_{n}
, the equation (
C.56
), and the negative
Sobolev bound for
\partial_{t}Y^{(q)}_{n}
give compactness. Thus, after passing to a
subsequence,

Y^{(q)}_{n}\to R_{q}

strongly in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}(Q_{\rm in})
, weakly in the local energy class, and in the
trace topology. Passing to the limit gives (
C.53
), and the divergence-free
condition passes to the limit.

The strict pressure condition follows from compatibility. Taking the horizontal divergence
of (
C.53
) gives

-\Delta_{h}\Pi_{q}=\sum_{i+j=q}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(R_{i,a}R_{j,b}).

Thus
\partial_{3}\Pi_{q}=0
is equivalent, modulo horizontal harmonic functions, to

\sum_{i+j=q}\mathfrak{B}(R_{i},R_{j})=0.

The identities (
C.55
) follow from

Proposition

C.5
. This closes the induction.
∎

Remark C.3

(Source of finite-order trace-flatness)
.

Estimate (
C.50
) is the finite-order analogue of the trace-tightness
statement proved in Appendix
B
for the first normalized sequence. After subtracting the already
extracted jets, the remainder
Y^{(q)}_{n}
satisfies a localized parabolic equation whose
principal part is again the heat operator with horizontal pressure. The forcing terms consist
of lower-order extracted jets, a harmless positive power of
\varepsilon_{n}
multiplying the current
remainder, and the raw residual divided by
\varepsilon_{n}^{q}
. Under
K
-sharpness this last
term tends to zero for
q\leq K-1
. Therefore the short-time high-frequency drop argument
of Appendix
B
applies to
Y^{(q)}_{n}
after the same localization and density-point choices.
If trace-flatness failed at the first order
q
, the corresponding energy-drop comparison
would produce a strict competitor with a finite-power improvement. Hence any genuine
failed-selection sequence that survives all finite-power alternatives must satisfy
(
C.50
) for every fixed finite order.

C.7
Conditional zero-shadow exactification in strong parabolic spaces

The preceding two sections identify and eliminate the finite-order geometric obstructions at the zero
shadow. We now record the analytic estimates which turn the resulting compatible jets into exact
strict-shadow curves. The point is to make the zero-shadow closure independent of informal appeals
to higher-order trace compactness or unspecified summability.

Throughout this section we fix nested cylinders

Q_{\rm in}\Subset Q_{\rm mid}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}\Subset Q_{\rm str}

and a lower time
t_{0}<s_{*}
inside the time projection of
Q_{\rm sh}
. Constants may depend on
this cylinder chain. We choose once and for all a Sobolev index

s>\frac{5}{2}.

On a cylinder
Q=I\times B
, set

X_{s}(Q):=L^{\infty}(I;H^{s}(B))\cap L^{2}(I;H^{s+1}(B))

with the natural norm. The associated pressure space is

P_{s}(Q):=L^{2}(I;H^{s}(B)/\mathcal{H}_{h}(B)),

where
\mathcal{H}_{h}(B)
denotes the horizontal harmonic ambiguity. The choice
s>5/2
makes

H^{s}
a Banach algebra and allows no-loss product estimates.

Lemma C.2

(Localized strict Stokes majorant)
.

Let
A=(A_{h},0)
and
B=(B_{h},0)
belong to
X_{s}(Q_{\rm mid})
, with

\nabla_{h}\cdot A_{h}=\nabla_{h}\cdot B_{h}=0.

Assume that the horizontal pressure
\pi
reconstructed from

-\Delta_{h}\pi=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(A_{a}B_{b})

satisfies
\partial_{3}\pi=0
modulo horizontal harmonic functions. Let
(R,\pi)
solve the
localized strict Stokes problem

\partial_{t}R_{h}-\Delta R_{h}+\nabla_{h}\pi=-\nabla_{h}\cdot(A_{h}\otimes B_{h}),

\nabla_{h}\cdot R_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\pi=0,

with zero lower-time trace at
t_{0}
, after the usual interior localization. Then

\|R\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm in})}+\|\pi\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm in})}\leq C_{s}\|A\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mid})}\|B\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mid})}.

Proof.

The localized parabolic Stokes estimate gives

\|R\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm in})}+\|\pi\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm in})}\leq C_{s}\|\nabla_{h}\cdot(A_{h}\otimes B_{h})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}(Q_{\rm mid})}.

Since
s>5/2
,
H^{s}
is an algebra and

\|\nabla_{h}\cdot(A_{h}\otimes B_{h})\|_{H^{s-1}}\leq C_{s}\|A_{h}\otimes B_{h}\|_{H^{s}}\leq C_{s}\|A\|_{H^{s}}\|B\|_{H^{s}}.

Taking the
L^{2}_{t}
-norm and using
X_{s}\subset L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{x}
gives the stated
bound. The pressure estimate follows from the horizontal elliptic equation in the quotient by
horizontal harmonic functions. The assumed compatibility is exactly the condition that the pressure
belongs to the strict class.
∎

Lemma C.3

(Interior smoothing of zero-shadow blow-up directions)
.

Let
W=(W_{h},0)
be a local-energy solution of the zero-shadow linearized strict system

\partial_{t}W_{h}-\Delta W_{h}+\nabla_{h}\Pi=0,\qquad\nabla_{h}\cdot W_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi=0

in
Q_{\rm sh}
. Then, for every
Q_{\rm in}\Subset Q_{\rm mid}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}
whose lower
time is strictly above the lower time of
Q_{\rm sh}
,

W\in X_{s}(Q_{\rm in}),\qquad\Pi\in P_{s}(Q_{\rm in}),

and

\|W\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm in})}+\|\Pi\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm in})}\leq C_{s,Q_{\rm in},Q_{\rm mid}}\|W\|_{\mathcal{E}(Q_{\rm mid})}.

Proof.

After multiplication by a cutoff supported in
Q_{\rm mid}
, the equation becomes a forced heat
system whose forcing consists only of cutoff commutators involving
W
and
\nabla W
. These
terms are controlled by the local energy norm on
Q_{\rm mid}
. Interior
L^{2}
-based parabolic
regularity gives
H^{s}
-regularity for every finite
s
on
Q_{\rm in}
. The pressure estimate is the
horizontal elliptic estimate modulo horizontal harmonic functions. The condition
\partial_{3}\Pi=0

is preserved distributionally and hence in the smoothed interior representative.
∎

We now make the trace-flatness input in Section
C.6
explicit. Suppose that

U_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}W_{n}
,
\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2}
, is a zero-shadow failed-selection sequence and

\rho_{n}:=\ell_{n}^{1/6}+\ell_{n}^{-N}\delta_{n}^{b}.

For
K\geq 2
, recall that the sequence is
K
-sharp if

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K}).

Assume that jets
R_{1},\ldots,R_{q-1}
have already been extracted and set

Y_{n}^{(q)}:=\varepsilon_{n}^{1-q}\left(W_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{q-1}\varepsilon_{n}^{j-1}R_{j}\right).

Proposition C.6

(Finite-order trace-flatness alternative)
.

Fix
K\geq 2
and
1\leq q\leq K-1
. Assume that the sequence is
K
-sharp and that no positive
finite-power selection estimate has already been obtained at any order
\leq q+1
. Then

\sup_{n}\|Y_{n}^{(q)}\|_{\mathcal{E}(Q_{\rm mid})}\leq C_{q},

and the traces are locally tight:

\lim_{\rho\downarrow 0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\int\phi\big|Y_{n}^{(q)}(s_{n})-J_{\rho}Y_{n}^{(q)}(s_{n})\big|^{2}\,dx=0.

Consequently, after passing to a subsequence,

Y_{n}^{(q)}\to R_{q}

strongly in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}(Q_{\rm in})
, weakly in the local energy class, and strongly in the
localized trace topology.

Proof.

Subtract the equations satisfied by the partial jet

P_{n}^{(q-1)}:=\sum_{j=1}^{q-1}\varepsilon_{n}^{j-1}R_{j}

from the normalized equation for
W_{n}
, and divide by
\varepsilon_{n}^{q-1}
. The remainder

Y_{n}^{(q)}
satisfies

\partial_{t}Y_{n,h}^{(q)}-\Delta Y_{n,h}^{(q)}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{n}^{(q)}=-\sum_{i+j=q}\nabla_{h}\cdot(R_{i,h}\otimes R_{j,h})+\mathcal{R}_{n}^{(q)},

where
\mathcal{R}_{n}^{(q)}\to 0
in the localized energy-dual norm on
Q_{\rm in}
. Indeed, every
unaccounted quadratic term carries a positive power of
\varepsilon_{n}
, while the raw residual is

\varepsilon_{n}^{-q}{\rm Raw}_{n}
, which tends to zero because
q\leq K-1
and

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K})
.

The same localized high-frequency trace-drop argument used in Appendix
B
applies to this equation.
If the high-frequency trace of
Y_{n}^{(q)}(s_{n})
were not tight, a short-time parabolic drop would
produce a strict competitor improving the original squared distance by a definite multiple of

\varepsilon_{n}^{2q}
. All lower-order jet terms have already been matched, and the raw terms are
negligible under
K
-sharpness unless
\rho_{n}\gtrsim\varepsilon_{n}^{q+1}
. In that exceptional case

m_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/(q+1)},

which is already a positive finite-power selection estimate, contrary to the alternative under
consideration. Thus trace loss cannot occur. The uniform local energy bound follows from the
same localized inequality. With tight traces and a negative Sobolev bound for
\partial_{t}Y_{n}^{(q)}
,
the Aubin–Lions–Simon compactness lemma
[
9
]
yields the asserted convergence.
∎

Corollary C.2

(Iterated zero-shadow jet extraction, strict form)
.

Fix
K\geq 2
. Suppose that the zero-shadow sequence is
K
-sharp and that no positive finite-power
selection estimate has appeared at orders
\leq K
. Then there exist jets

R_{1}=W,R_{2},\ldots,R_{K-1}

and pressures
\Pi_{1},\ldots,\Pi_{K-1}
such that, for
1\leq q\leq K-1
,

\varepsilon_{n}^{1-q}\left(W_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{q-1}\varepsilon_{n}^{j-1}R_{j}\right)\to R_{q}

strongly in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}
, weakly in the local energy class, and in the localized trace topology.
Moreover the strict jet equations hold through order
K-1
, and

\sum_{i+j=k}B(R_{i},R_{j})=0,\qquad 2\leq k\leq K.

Proof.

Apply Proposition
C.6
successively. Passing to the
limit in the remainder equations gives the parabolic jet hierarchy. The compatibility identities are
obtained by expanding
B(W_{n},W_{n})
in

\varepsilon_{n}^{2}B(W_{n},W_{n})=\mathcal{E}_{n},\qquad\|\mathcal{E}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{Y}}\lesssim\rho_{n}.

Since
K
-sharpness gives
B(W_{n},W_{n})=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K-2})
, each coefficient through order

K-2
must vanish. The coefficient of
\varepsilon_{n}^{k-2}
is exactly

\sum_{i+j=k}B(R_{i},R_{j})
.
∎

Lemma C.4

(Admissibility of small zero-shadow curves)
.

Suppose

V^{\varepsilon}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{k}R_{k},\qquad Q^{\varepsilon}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{k}\Pi_{k}

converges in
X_{s}(Q_{\rm str})\times P_{s}(Q_{\rm str})
for
|\varepsilon|<\varepsilon_{0}
, and solves the exact strict system. Then, after possibly decreasing
\varepsilon_{0}
,

(V^{\varepsilon},Q^{\varepsilon})\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm str})

for every
|\varepsilon|<\varepsilon_{0}
.

Proof.

Since
s>5/2
, convergence in
X_{s}
implies the local energy,
L^{3}
, and pressure bounds.
Moreover

\|V^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla V^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|V^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}}\leq C|\varepsilon|\sum_{k\geq 1}|\varepsilon|^{k-1}\|R_{k}\|_{X_{s}},

and the right-hand side tends to zero as
\varepsilon\to 0
. The pressure series gives the
corresponding
L^{3/2}
-bound modulo horizontal harmonic functions. Hence, for
|\varepsilon|

sufficiently small,
\Phi_{V^{\varepsilon}}(Q_{\rm str})\leq K_{0}(M,\theta)
, which is the admissibility
bound defining
\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}
.
∎

Theorem C.2

(Technically closed zero-shadow branch)
.

Assume the zero-shadow failed-selection branch survives every positive finite-power alternative. Then

W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{0}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}.

Consequently, the zero-shadow branch cannot produce a genuine failed subcritical finite-power
selection sequence.

Proof.

The sequence is
K
-sharp for every finite
K
. By
Corollary
C.2
, it produces compatible strict jets to
every finite order. A diagonal argument gives a full compatible jet sequence. By
Lemma
C.3
, the first jet belongs to
X_{s}
on every interior
cylinder with lower time below
s_{*}
. Lemma
C.2
gives the
Catalan majorant for the recursive construction, hence convergence of

V^{\varepsilon}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{k}R_{k},\qquad Q^{\varepsilon}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{k}\Pi_{k}

for
|\varepsilon|
small. Termwise summation gives the exact strict equation, and
Lemma
C.4
gives admissibility. Finally,

\frac{V^{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon}=W+\varepsilon R_{2}+\varepsilon^{2}R_{3}+\cdots\to W

in the local energy and trace topologies. Hence
W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{0}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}
. The first-variation
orthogonality from Appendix
B
then contradicts strong trace non-loss exactly as in
Corollary 5.4. Therefore the zero-shadow branch cannot survive.
∎

C.8
Nonzero singular strata: moving-base Lyapunov–Schmidt reduction

We now treat the main nonzero singular configuration. Let
(V,Q)\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm str})
be a
nonzero strict shadow and set

L_{V}:=DC_{V}.

The tame branch below is an infinite-dimensional Lyapunov–Schmidt reduction, in the standard sense used in bifurcation theory
[
45
]
.
The nonzero singular case differs from the zero shadow in two ways. First,
L_{V}
is not zero;
second, along a failed-selection sequence one only knows
V_{n}\to V
, with no rate relative to

\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2}
. For this reason the correct formulation is a moving-base one: we
construct exact strict curves through the finite-level shadows
V_{n}
, rather than a curve through the
limit
V
alone.

C.8.1
Tame finite-codimensional singular strata

We work in the strong localized space
X_{s}
from Section
C.7
, and in a compatibility
space
\mathcal{Y}_{s}
adapted to the map
C
. A nonzero singular family is called tame finite-codimensional
near
V
if, for all strict shadows
\widetilde{V}
sufficiently close to
V
in
X_{s}
, the operator

L_{\widetilde{V}}:=DC_{\widetilde{V}}:X_{s}\to\mathcal{Y}_{s}

admits splittings

X_{s}=\Ker L_{\widetilde{V}}\oplus N_{\widetilde{V}},\qquad\mathcal{Y}_{s}=\Range(L_{\widetilde{V}})\oplus K_{\widetilde{V}},

where
K_{\widetilde{V}}
is finite-dimensional, and

L_{\widetilde{V}}:N_{\widetilde{V}}\to\Range(L_{\widetilde{V}})

has a uniformly bounded right inverse

G_{\widetilde{V}}:\Range(L_{\widetilde{V}})\to N_{\widetilde{V}}.

We denote by
\Pi_{\widetilde{V}}^{R}
and
\Pi_{\widetilde{V}}^{K}
the corresponding range and cokernel
projections. Uniformity means that the projections, right inverses, and product estimates are bounded
with constants independent of
n
for
\widetilde{V}=V_{n}
.

The all-order compatibility hierarchy around a fixed nonzero base is

L_{V}[R_{1}]=0,

L_{V}[R_{k}]+\sum_{i+j=k}B(R_{i},R_{j})=0,\qquad k\geq 2,\qquad R_{1}=W.

At order
k
, the cokernel obstruction is

\Pi_{V}^{K}\left(\sum_{i+j=k}B(R_{i},R_{j})\right)=0.

When this identity holds, the normal correction may be chosen by

R_{k}^{\perp}=-G_{V}\Pi_{V}^{R}\left(\sum_{i+j=k}B(R_{i},R_{j})\right),

and the kernel component is fixed by a gauge, for instance
R_{k}^{\parallel}=0
. This solves the
compatibility equation at order
k
. The strict parabolic equation at the same order is then

\displaystyle\partial_{t}R_{k,h}-\Delta R_{k,h}

\displaystyle+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes R_{k,h}+R_{k,h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{k}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i+j=k}\nabla_{h}\cdot(R_{i,h}\otimes R_{j,h}),

with

\nabla_{h}\cdot R_{k,h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi_{k}=0.

The strict pressure condition is equivalent to the compatibility equation above.

C.8.2
Moving-base tangent correction

Let a failed sharp selection sequence have finite-level strict shadows
(V_{n},Q_{n})
and normalized
differences

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2}.

The moving-base formulation corrects
W_{n}
into an exact formal tangent at
V_{n}
.

Lemma C.5

(Moving-base tangent correction)
.

Assume the tame finite-codimensional splitting above for
V_{n}
. Suppose

\|DC_{V_{n}}[W_{n}]\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{s}}\to 0.

Then there exist corrected directions

Z_{n}:=W_{n}-G_{V_{n}}\Pi_{V_{n}}^{R}DC_{V_{n}}[W_{n}]

such that

DC_{V_{n}}[Z_{n}]=\Pi_{V_{n}}^{K}DC_{V_{n}}[W_{n}],

and, if in addition

\Pi_{V_{n}}^{K}DC_{V_{n}}[W_{n}]=0,

then

DC_{V_{n}}[Z_{n}]=0,\qquad Z_{n}-W_{n}\to 0

in the topology used for the first variation.

Proof.

The definition gives

L_{V_{n}}[Z_{n}]=L_{V_{n}}[W_{n}]-L_{V_{n}}G_{V_{n}}\Pi_{V_{n}}^{R}L_{V_{n}}[W_{n}]=\Pi_{V_{n}}^{K}L_{V_{n}}[W_{n}].

If the cokernel component vanishes, then
Z_{n}\in\Ker L_{V_{n}}
. Uniform boundedness of

G_{V_{n}}
gives

\|Z_{n}-W_{n}\|_{X_{s}}\leq C\|DC_{V_{n}}[W_{n}]\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{s}}\to 0.

∎

Remark C.4
.

The hypothesis
\Pi_{V_{n}}^{K}DC_{V_{n}}[W_{n}]=0
is automatic if
W_{n}
is first projected onto the
finite-level trace stratum. In the failed-selection setting, the uncancelled cokernel components are
handled by the same sharpness dichotomy used for higher obstructions: if they are not negligible,
then the distance improves by a positive power; if the sequence survives, their coefficients vanish.

C.8.3
Reduced obstruction inheritance

The pressure-compatibility identity is

C(U_{n})-C(V_{n})=\varepsilon_{n}DC_{V_{n}}[W_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}B(W_{n},W_{n})=\mathcal{E}_{n},

with
\|\mathcal{E}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{Y}}\lesssim\rho_{n}
. After division by
\varepsilon_{n}
, one has

DC_{V_{n}}[W_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}B(W_{n},W_{n})=\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{n}.

This yields the first-order tangent condition and, at the next order, the quadratic compatibility
condition of Appendix
B
. The same expansion gives all reduced cokernel identities along any surviving
sharp sequence.

Proposition C.7

(Reduced obstruction inheritance at a tame nonzero base)
.

Fix
K\geq 2
. Assume the sequence is
K
-sharp,

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K}),

and suppose that moving-base jets

Z_{1,n},Z_{2,n},\ldots,Z_{K-1,n}

have been extracted at the finite-level bases
V_{n}
, with
Z_{1,n}=Z_{n}
, so that

W_{n}=Z_{1,n}+\varepsilon_{n}Z_{2,n}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{n}^{K-2}Z_{K-1,n}+o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K-2})

in the topology needed to test
B
. Then the coefficient identities hold through the extracted orders:

\Pi_{V_{n}}^{K}\left(DC_{V_{n}}[Z_{k,n}]+\sum_{i+j=k}B(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n})\right)\to 0,\qquad 2\leq k\leq K-1.

Moreover, because
DC_{V_{n}}[Z_{K,n}]
would lie in the range whenever a
K
-th correction is chosen,
the reduced obstruction at the next order is already determined by the extracted lower jets:

\Pi_{V_{n}}^{K}\left(\sum_{i+j=K}B(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n})\right)\to 0.

Thus, in the gauge where the range equation is solved at each lower order, all reduced cokernel
obstructions through order
K
vanish.
If
K
-sharpness fails, then

m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K},

which is already a positive finite-power selection estimate.

Proof.

If
K
-sharpness fails, then
\varepsilon_{n}^{K}\lesssim\rho_{n}
, hence

m_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K}
. This gives the first alternative.

Assume
K
-sharpness. Substitute the moving-base expansion into

C(V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n})-C(V_{n})=\mathcal{E}_{n}.

Since
C
is quadratic, the coefficient of
\varepsilon_{n}^{k}
for
2\leq k\leq K-1
is

DC_{V_{n}}[Z_{k,n}]+\sum_{i+j=k}B(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n}).

At order
K
, the possible term
DC_{V_{n}}[Z_{K,n}]
belongs to

\Range(DC_{V_{n}})
, so it disappears after applying the cokernel projection. The full defect is

o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K})
. Projecting onto the finite-dimensional cokernel and comparing coefficients
through order
K
gives the stated reduced identities. The range component is then removed by the
tame right inverse.
∎

C.8.4
Tame moving-base curve selection

Theorem C.3

(Tame nonzero singular stratum reduction)
.

Let a failed sharp finite-power selection sequence have limiting strict shadow
V\neq 0
. Assume
that the nearby finite-level shadows
V_{n}
lie in a tame finite-codimensional singular stratum in the
sense above. Assume also the moving-base finite-order trace-flatness iteration and the uniform
parabolic majorant estimates for the linearized strict operators around
V_{n}
. Then this branch
cannot produce a genuine failed-selection sequence.

Proof.

If finite-order sharpness fails at some order, the estimate

m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K}
gives a positive finite-power selection estimate, contradicting genuine
failure. Thus a surviving sequence is sharp to every finite order. By the moving-base tangent
correction and the finite-order trace-flatness iteration, one extracts moving-base jets

Z_{1,n},Z_{2,n},Z_{3,n},\ldots

through every finite order. Proposition
C.7
gives the
vanishing of all reduced cokernel obstructions. The tame right inverse solves the range equations,
and the uniform parabolic majorant estimates sum the jets to exact strict-shadow curves

V_{n}^{\varepsilon}=V_{n}+\varepsilon Z_{1,n}+\varepsilon^{2}Z_{2,n}+\cdots

for
|\varepsilon|
small. Moreover

Z_{1,n}-W_{n}\to 0.

Hence the traces of integrable tangent directions through
V_{n}
converge to the same limiting trace

W(s_{*})
obtained in the blow-up.

The first-variation argument at the sharp minimizers gives orthogonality of
W(s_{*})
to all such
integrable tangent traces. Since
W(s_{*})
itself is reached as the limit of these moving-base
integrable tangents, one obtains

\int\phi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=0.

Strong trace non-loss gives instead

\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=1.

This contradiction excludes a genuine failed sequence on a tame nonzero singular stratum.
∎

C.8.5
Remaining finite-stage alternative after tame nonzero analysis

The preceding theorem closes all nonzero singular strata for which the compatibility derivative has
uniform closed complemented range and the reduced cokernel equations are controlled by either
transversality or failed-selection sharpness. The remaining possible obstruction is therefore much
narrower: a nonzero strict shadow
V
for which
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}
has no tame complemented range, or for
which the reduced hierarchy remains infinitely singular. In the rest of the paper we do not attempt
a global finite stratification theorem for
\mathfrak{C}^{-1}(0)
. This is deliberately weaker than finite-dimensional analytic stratification theory
[
40
,
43
]
. Instead, we use a finite-mode dichotomy:
finite-dimensional visible obstructions force a positive finite-power selection estimate, while the
absence of all such obstructions defines a finite-mode flat branch. The latter is then treated by
high-frequency tail estimates, moving-base jet extraction, and compact exactification.

C.9
Finite-mode obstructions and the flat non-tame branch

We now sharpen the remaining nonzero non-tame case. The point is that one does not need a
global stratification theorem for the full compatibility variety
\mathfrak{C}^{-1}(0)
. Instead, every
finite-dimensional observation of a nonzero obstruction already forces a positive finite-power
selection estimate. Thus a genuinely surviving failed-selection branch must be invisible to all
finite-mode obstructions.

Let

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},\qquad V_{n}\to V\neq 0,

be a nonzero failed-selection branch. Write

L_{n}:=D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}.

The pressure-compatibility defect identity has the form

\mathfrak{C}(U_{n})-\mathfrak{C}(V_{n})=E_{n},\qquad\|E_{n}\|_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq C\rho_{n},

(C.57)

where

\rho_{n}=\ell_{n}^{1/6}+\ell_{n}^{-N_{0}}\delta_{n}^{b}

collects the covariance, one-component, and localized pressure-compatibility errors. Since

\mathfrak{C}
is quadratic,

\mathfrak{C}(V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n})-\mathfrak{C}(V_{n})=\varepsilon_{n}L_{n}W_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n}).

(C.58)

Let
P_{N}:\mathcal{Y}\to Y_{N}
be a finite-rank projection onto a finite-dimensional compatibility
space
Y_{N}
. We assume only that
P_{N}
is bounded:

\|P_{N}F\|_{Y_{N}}\leq C_{N}\|F\|_{\mathcal{Y}}.

No closed-range assumption is imposed on
L_{n}
in the full space. For
F\in\mathcal{Y}
, define the
finite-mode quotient defect

\mathbf{d}_{N,n}(F):=\inf_{R\in X_{s}}\|P_{N}(F-L_{n}R)\|_{Y_{N}}.

(C.59)

Equivalently,

\mathbf{d}_{N,n}(F)=\dist_{Y_{N}}\bigl(P_{N}F,P_{N}\Range(L_{n})\bigr).

This quantity is well-defined even when
\Range(L_{n})
is not closed in
\mathcal{Y}
, because the
projected range lies in the finite-dimensional space
Y_{N}
.

Lemma C.6

(Finite-mode quadratic obstruction dichotomy)
.

For every fixed finite-mode projection
P_{N}
,

\mathbf{d}_{N,n}\bigl(\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})\bigr)\leq C_{N}\frac{\rho_{n}}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}}.

(C.60)

Consequently, if for some
N
there exists
c_{N}>0
such that, along a subsequence,

\mathbf{d}_{N,n}\bigl(\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})\bigr)\geq c_{N},

then

m_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\leq C_{N}\rho_{n}.

In particular, a positive finite-power selection estimate already follows.

Proof.

By (
C.57
) and (
C.58
),

\varepsilon_{n}L_{n}W_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})=E_{n}.

Dividing by
\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
, we obtain

\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})+L_{n}\left(\frac{W_{n}}{\varepsilon_{n}}\right)=\frac{E_{n}}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}}.

Taking
R=-W_{n}/\varepsilon_{n}
in the definition of
\mathbf{d}_{N,n}
, we get

\mathbf{d}_{N,n}\bigl(\mathfrak{B}(W_{n},W_{n})\bigr)\leq\left\|P_{N}\frac{E_{n}}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}}\right\|_{Y_{N}}\leq C_{N}\frac{\rho_{n}}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}}.

If the left-hand side is bounded from below by
c_{N}>0
, then

c_{N}\leq C_{N}\frac{\rho_{n}}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}},

and hence

m_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\leq C_{N}\rho_{n}.

This is already a positive finite-power selection estimate.
∎

Definition C.3

(Nonzero
K
-sharpness)
.

For an integer
K\geq 2
, we say that the nonzero failed-selection branch is
K
-sharp if

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K}).

If
K
-sharpness fails, then along a subsequence
\varepsilon_{n}^{K}\lesssim\rho_{n}
, and therefore

m_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K}.

Thus failure of
K
-sharpness is already a positive finite-power selection estimate.

Assume that, for some
K\geq 2
, one has extracted moving-base jets

Z_{1,n},Z_{2,n},\ldots,Z_{K-1,n}

so that

W_{n}=Z_{1,n}+\varepsilon_{n}Z_{2,n}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{n}^{K-2}Z_{K-1,n}+r_{K,n},\qquad r_{K,n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K-2}),

(C.61)

in the topology needed to test
\mathfrak{B}
. For
2\leq k\leq K-1
, define

\mathcal{O}_{k,n}:=L_{n}Z_{k,n}+\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n}).

(C.62)

The
K
-th reduced obstruction is

\mathcal{O}^{\rm red}_{K,n}:=\sum_{i+j=K}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n}).

(C.63)

The term
L_{n}Z_{K,n}
, if a
K
-th correction is chosen, lies in
\Range(L_{n})
. Thus, after
passing to the quotient by the finite-mode range, the true obstruction at order
K
is precisely

\mathcal{O}^{\rm red}_{K,n}
.

Proposition C.8

(Finite-mode higher-order obstruction dichotomy)
.

Fix
K\geq 2
and
N\geq 1
. Assume that the branch is
K
-sharp and admits the moving-base
expansion (
C.61
). Assume also that the lower-order finite-mode
coefficients have already been removed modulo
P_{N}\Range(L_{n})
, in the following sense: for each

2\leq k\leq K-1
,

\mathbf{d}_{N,n}\left(\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n})\right)=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K-k}).

(C.64)

Then

\mathbf{d}_{N,n}\bigl(\mathcal{O}^{\rm red}_{K,n}\bigr)\leq C_{K,N}\frac{\rho_{n}}{\varepsilon_{n}^{K}}+o(1).

(C.65)

Consequently, if for some
K,N
there exists
c_{K,N}>0
such that

\mathbf{d}_{N,n}\bigl(\mathcal{O}^{\rm red}_{K,n}\bigr)\geq c_{K,N}

along a subsequence, then

m_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K}.

Thus a positive finite-power selection estimate already follows.

Proof.

Substitute (
C.61
) into
(
C.58
). Since
\mathfrak{C}
is quadratic and
\mathfrak{B}
is continuous
in the chosen topology, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}(U_{n})-\mathfrak{C}(V_{n})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{n}L_{n}Z_{1,n}+\sum_{k=2}^{K-1}\varepsilon_{n}^{k}\left[L_{n}Z_{k,n}+\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n})\right]

\displaystyle+\varepsilon_{n}^{K}\sum_{i+j=K}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n})+o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K}).

That is,

E_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}L_{n}Z_{1,n}+\sum_{k=2}^{K-1}\varepsilon_{n}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{k,n}+\varepsilon_{n}^{K}\mathcal{O}^{\rm red}_{K,n}+o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K}).

(C.66)

The first term
\varepsilon_{n}L_{n}Z_{1,n}
belongs to
\Range(L_{n})
. For each lower order

2\leq k\leq K-1
, assumption (
C.64
) says that the nonlinear part of

\mathcal{O}_{k,n}
differs from an element of
\Range(L_{n})
by

o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K-k})
after applying
P_{N}
. Since
L_{n}Z_{k,n}
itself belongs to the range,
the projected lower-order contribution is within
o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K})
of
P_{N}\Range(L_{n})
.
Taking the finite-mode distance of (
C.66
) to
P_{N}\Range(L_{n})
, we get

\varepsilon_{n}^{K}\mathbf{d}_{N,n}\bigl(\mathcal{O}^{\rm red}_{K,n}\bigr)\leq\|P_{N}E_{n}\|_{Y_{N}}+o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K}).

Using
\|E_{n}\|_{\mathcal{Y}}\leq C\rho_{n}
, we obtain (
C.65
). If the left-hand side
is bounded below by
c_{K,N}>0
, then
\varepsilon_{n}^{K}\lesssim\rho_{n}
, and therefore

m_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K}
.
∎

Corollary C.3

(Finite-mode flatness of a genuinely surviving branch)
.

Let

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad V_{n}\to V\neq 0,

be a nonzero failed-selection branch which survives every positive finite-power alternative.
Then, for every
K\geq 2
and every finite-mode projection
P_{N}:\mathcal{Y}\to Y_{N}
, no
K
-th
projected reduced obstruction can have a positive lower bound. Equivalently, every extracted
moving-base jet hierarchy satisfies

\mathbf{d}_{N,n}\bigl(\mathcal{O}^{\rm red}_{K,n}\bigr)\longrightarrow 0

for each fixed
K,N
. Thus a genuinely surviving nonzero non-tame branch is finite-mode flat.

Proof.

If
K
-sharpness fails for some
K
, then
m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K}
, which is already a positive
finite-power selection estimate. Hence a genuinely surviving branch is
K
-sharp for every

K
. Fix
K,N
. If the projected
K
-th reduced obstruction had a positive lower bound,

Proposition

C.8
would again give

m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K}
, contradicting genuine failure. Therefore the projected reduced
obstruction must vanish for every fixed
K,N
.
∎

Definition C.4

(Finite-mode flat nonzero branch)
.

A nonzero failed-selection branch

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad V_{n}\to V\neq 0,

is called finite-mode flat if, for every integer
K\geq 2
, every finite-rank projection

P_{N}:\mathcal{Y}\to Y_{N}
, and every extracted moving-base jet hierarchy up to order
K-1
, the

K
-th projected reduced obstruction satisfies

\mathbf{d}_{N,n}\bigl(\mathcal{O}^{\rm red}_{K,n}\bigr)\to 0.

By
Corollary

C.3
, every genuinely surviving nonzero
failed-selection branch must be finite-mode flat.

C.10
High-frequency tails and finite-order moving-base realizers

We now record the analytic inputs which turn finite-mode flatness into full weak compatibility. The
first input is a high-frequency Sobolev tail estimate. This appendix is independent of any tame inverse
for
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}
. The second input is the finite-order moving-base realizer statement, which is the
nonzero analogue of the zero-shadow trace-flatness iteration.

C.10.1
High-frequency compatibility tail estimate

Let

Q_{\rm min}\Subset Q_{\rm mmid}\Subset Q_{\rm msh}

be fixed interior cylinders, and choose
s>5/2
. We use the strong parabolic space
X_{s}
defined
in
Section

C.7
. For
0<a<a_{0}
, set

\mathcal{Y}_{s-2-a}(Q_{\rm min}):=L^{2}_{t}H^{s-2-a}_{x}(Q_{\rm min}).

Let
\{\psi_{j}\}_{j\geq 1}
be an orthonormal basis of eigenfunctions for a fixed positive elliptic
operator on the spatial domain of
Q_{\rm min}
, with eigenvalues
\lambda_{j}\to\infty
. Denote by

P_{N}
the spectral projection onto
\mathrm{span}\{\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{N}\}
, acting time-slice by
time-slice. Then
P_{N}
is finite-rank and

\|(I-P_{N})f\|_{H^{r-a}}\leq C\lambda_{N}^{-a/2}\|f\|_{H^{r}}.

Lemma C.7

(Sobolev tail estimate for the compatibility operator)
.

Let
s>5/2
. There exist
a_{0}>0
and constants
C_{s}
, depending only on the interior cylinder
chain and on
s
, such that for every
0<a\leq a_{0}
,

\|(I-P_{N})\mathfrak{B}(A,B)\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{s-2-a}(Q_{\rm min})}\leq C_{s}\lambda_{N}^{-a/2}\|A\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})}\|B\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})}.

In particular, with
\omega_{N}=C_{s}\lambda_{N}^{-a/2}
,

\|(I-P_{N})\mathfrak{B}(A,B)\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{s-2-a}(Q_{\rm min})}\leq\omega_{N}\|A\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})}\|B\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})},\qquad\omega_{N}\to 0.

Proof.

Let
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{\rm mmid})
be identically one on
Q_{\rm min}
. It is enough to
estimate
\zeta\mathfrak{B}(A,B)
. After the horizontal harmonic ambiguity has been fixed, the localized
operator

T=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}

has order two in the Sobolev scale used here. Hence

\|\zeta\mathfrak{B}(A,B)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}\leq C_{s}\|\zeta A_{a}B_{b}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}.

Since
s>5/2
,
H^{s}
is a Banach algebra. Therefore

\|A_{a}B_{b}\|_{H^{s}}\leq C_{s}\|A\|_{H^{s}}\|B\|_{H^{s}}.

Taking the
L^{2}_{t}
-norm and using
X_{s}\subset L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}\cap L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{x}
, we obtain

\|\zeta\mathfrak{B}(A,B)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}\leq C_{s}\|A\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})}\|B\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})}.

The spectral tail estimate, a standard Sobolev spectral-tail consequence, gives

\|(I-P_{N})f\|_{H^{s-2-a}}\leq C\lambda_{N}^{-a/2}\|f\|_{H^{s-2}}.

Applying this to
f=\zeta\mathfrak{B}(A,B)
and integrating in time proves the claim.
∎

Corollary C.4

(Tail estimate for the moving-base linearized compatibility)
.

Let
s>5/2
,
0<a\leq a_{0}
, and
V\in X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})
. Then

\|(I-P_{N})D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[Z]\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{s-2-a}(Q_{\rm min})}\leq C_{s}\lambda_{N}^{-a/2}\|V\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})}\|Z\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})}.

More generally, if
V_{n}
is bounded in
X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})
, then

\|(I-P_{N})D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z]\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{s-2-a}(Q_{\rm min})}\leq C_{s}\lambda_{N}^{-a/2}\|Z\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})},

with a constant uniform in
n
.

Proof.

Since
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[Z]=\mathfrak{B}(V,Z)+\mathfrak{B}(Z,V)
, the result follows immediately from

Lemma

C.7
.
∎

Proposition C.9

(From finite-mode flatness to full weak compatibility)
.

Let
s>5/2
,
0<a\leq a_{0}
, and let
V_{n}\to V
be bounded in

X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})
. Suppose that, for a fixed order
k
, one has moving-base coefficients

Z_{1,n},\ldots,Z_{k,n}
, uniformly bounded in
X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})
, and define

\mathcal{O}_{k,n}:=D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z_{k,n}]+\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n}).

Assume that for every finite-mode projection
P_{N}
,

\|P_{N}\mathcal{O}_{k,n}\|_{Y_{N}}\to 0\qquad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Then

\lim_{N\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\|\mathcal{O}_{k,n}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{s-2-a}(Q_{\rm min})}=0.

Proof.

Split
\mathcal{O}_{k,n}=P_{N}\mathcal{O}_{k,n}+(I-P_{N})\mathcal{O}_{k,n}
. The projected part tends
to zero for each fixed
N
by assumption. For the tail, use

Lemmas

C.7
and
C.4
:

\displaystyle\|(I-P_{N})\mathcal{O}_{k,n}\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{s-2-a}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s}\lambda_{N}^{-a/2}\|V_{n}\|_{X_{s}}\|Z_{k,n}\|_{X_{s}}

\displaystyle+C_{s}\lambda_{N}^{-a/2}\sum_{i+j=k}\|Z_{i,n}\|_{X_{s}}\|Z_{j,n}\|_{X_{s}}.

The
X_{s}
-bounds are uniform in
n
, so the
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
of the tail is bounded by

C_{k}\lambda_{N}^{-a/2}
. Letting
N\to\infty
gives the claim.
∎

C.10.2
Finite-order moving-base realizers

We next record the finite-order moving-base analogue of the zero-shadow jet extraction. This
statement is weaker than analytic summability: it constructs jets only up to a fixed finite order

K
. This is the correct level at which finite-mode flatness is first used.

Proposition C.10

(Finite-order moving-base jet extraction)
.

Fix
K\geq 2
. Assume that the nonzero branch is
K
-sharp and survives all positive finite-power
alternatives of order
\leq K
. Assume the moving-base finite-order trace-flatness iteration for the
normalized remainders. Then, after passing to a subsequence, there exist moving-base jets

(Z_{1,n},\Pi_{1,n}),\ldots,(Z_{K-1,n},\Pi_{K-1,n})

such that
Z_{1,n}-W_{n}\to 0
in the local energy and trace topologies, and

W_{n}=Z_{1,n}+\varepsilon_{n}Z_{2,n}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{n}^{K-2}Z_{K-1,n}+o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K-2})

in the topology needed to test
\mathfrak{B}
. Moreover, for each
1\leq q\leq K-1
,
Z_{q,n}

satisfies the
q
-th moving-base linearized parabolic jet equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Z_{q,n,h}-\Delta Z_{q,n,h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{n,h}\otimes Z_{q,n,h}+Z_{q,n,h}\otimes V_{n,h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{q,n}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=q\\
i,j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\nabla_{h}\cdot(Z_{i,n,h}\otimes Z_{j,n,h})+r^{(q)}_{n},

where
r^{(q)}_{n}\to 0
in the localized energy-dual residual space, and

\nabla_{h}\cdot Z_{q,n,h}=0
. Finally, for every finite-mode projection
P_{N}
,

P_{N}\left(D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z_{q,n}]+\sum_{i+j=q}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n})\right)\to 0

for each fixed
q\leq K-1
, along every genuinely surviving finite-mode flat branch.

Proof.

The proof is an induction parallel to the zero-shadow extraction, but with the linearized operator
frozen at the moving base
V_{n}
. For
q=1
, the normalized difference
W_{n}
satisfies the
equation obtained by subtracting the exact strict equation for
V_{n}
from the approximate strict
equation for
U_{n}
, and dividing by
\varepsilon_{n}
. The result has the form

\displaystyle\partial_{t}W_{n,h}-\Delta W_{n,h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{n,h}\otimes W_{n,h}+W_{n,h}\otimes V_{n,h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{1,n}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon_{n}\nabla_{h}\cdot(W_{n,h}\otimes W_{n,h})+\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}{\rm Raw}_{n}.

Since the branch is
K
-sharp, the raw term is negligible at every order
q\leq K-1
. The
moving-base trace-flatness input gives trace tightness and local energy compactness unless a
positive finite-power selection estimate has already been obtained. Under the present assumption,
that alternative is excluded.

Assume that jets up to order
q-1
have been constructed and define

Y^{(q)}_{n}:=\varepsilon_{n}^{1-q}\left(W_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{q-1}\varepsilon_{n}^{j-1}Z_{j,n}\right).

Subtract the moving-base jet equations for the already constructed partial sum and divide by

\varepsilon_{n}^{q-1}
. The remainder satisfies the displayed equation at order
q
, with a
residual
R^{(q)}_{n}\to 0
. The raw residual is divided by at most
\varepsilon_{n}^{q}
, which is
harmless because
q\leq K-1
and
\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K})
. The trace-flatness input again
gives compactness for
Y^{(q)}_{n}
, unless a finite-power improvement occurs. This closes the
induction.

The finite-mode compatibility identities follow by expanding

\mathfrak{C}(V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n})-\mathfrak{C}(V_{n})=E_{n},\qquad\|E_{n}\|_{\mathcal{Y}}\lesssim\rho_{n},

and comparing coefficients after applying
P_{N}
. If any projected coefficient failed to vanish,
the finite-mode obstruction dichotomy would give
m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/q}
, a positive
finite-power selection estimate. Since the branch is genuinely surviving, all such projected
coefficients vanish.
∎

C.11
Compact exactification of finite-mode flat branches

We now explain how finite-mode flatness yields tangent-cone closure without any tame right inverse.
The idea is to construct approximate strict shadows whose residuals vanish, and then take a compact
limit at each fixed small amplitude.

Throughout this section,

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad V_{n}\to V\neq 0,\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},

is a finite-mode flat nonzero branch. For a pair
(Y,R)
, define the strict residual

\mathfrak{R}(Y,R):=\left(\partial_{t}Y_{h}-\Delta Y_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(Y_{h}\otimes Y_{h})+\nabla_{h}R,\nabla_{h}\cdot Y_{h},\partial_{3}R\right).

Exact strict shadows are precisely those pairs for which

\mathfrak{R}(Y,R)=0
and
\mathfrak{C}(Y)=0
, modulo horizontal harmonic pressure ambiguity.

Lemma C.8

(Compact exactification at fixed amplitude)
.

Fix
0<\eta<1
. Suppose that there exists a sequence

(\widetilde{V}_{j}^{\eta},\widetilde{Q}_{j}^{\eta})
on
Q_{\rm mstr}
satisfying the following properties:

(i)

Uniform strong bound:

\sup_{j}\left(\|\widetilde{V}_{j}^{\eta}\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm msh})}+\|\widetilde{Q}_{j}^{\eta}\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm msh})}\right)<\infty.

(ii)

Vanishing strict residual:

\|\mathfrak{R}(\widetilde{V}_{j}^{\eta},\widetilde{Q}_{j}^{\eta})\|_{Z^{\prime}(Q_{\rm min})}+\|\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{V}_{j}^{\eta})\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{s-2-a}(Q_{\rm min})}\to 0.

(iii)

First-order trace normalization:

\widetilde{V}_{j}^{\eta}(s_{*})=V(s_{*})+\eta W(s_{*})+O(\eta^{2})+o_{j}(\eta)

strongly in
L^{2}_{\varphi}
, where the
O(\eta^{2})
bound is independent of
j
.

Then, after passing to a subsequence,

(\widetilde{V}_{j}^{\eta},\widetilde{Q}_{j}^{\eta})\longrightarrow(V^{\eta},Q^{\eta})

locally strongly in the energy and trace topologies, and
(V^{\eta},Q^{\eta})
is an exact strict
shadow on
Q_{\rm min}
. Moreover,

V^{\eta}(s_{*})=V(s_{*})+\eta W(s_{*})+O(\eta^{2})

in
L^{2}_{\varphi}
.

Proof.

The uniform
X_{s}\times P_{s}
bound gives compactness in lower local parabolic norms by the
Aubin–Lions–Simon compactness lemma. Thus, after passing to a subsequence,

\widetilde{V}_{j}^{\eta}\to V^{\eta}
strongly in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}
, in the localized trace topology, and
weakly in the strong parabolic space. The pressure sequence converges weakly in the quotient
pressure space. Because
s>5/2
, products are stable under the strong local convergence. Passing
to the limit in the vanishing residual gives the strict equation, the horizontal divergence constraint,
and
\partial_{3}Q^{\eta}=0
. The convergence of
\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{V}_{j}^{\eta})
to zero gives

\mathfrak{C}(V^{\eta})=0
, consistent with the strict pressure condition. The trace statement follows
from strong trace convergence.
∎

Intermediate Target C.1

(Bounded finite-mode flat realizers)
.

For every
K\geq 1
, after passing to a subsequence, the finite-mode flat branch admits moving-base
jets

(Z_{1,n},\Pi_{1,n}),\ldots,(Z_{K,n},\Pi_{K,n})

with
Z_{1,n}-W_{n}\to 0
in the local energy and trace topologies, satisfying the moving-base jet
equations through order
K
. Moreover, there exist constants
A,M_{*}
, independent of
K
and

n
, such that

\|Z_{k,n}\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm msh})}+\|\Pi_{k,n}\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm msh})}\leq M_{*}A^{k},\qquad 1\leq k\leq K.

For every finite-mode projection
P_{N}:\mathcal{Y}\to Y_{N}
, the projected compatibility coefficients
satisfy

\left\|P_{N}\left(D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z_{k,n}]+\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n})\right)\right\|_{Y_{N}}\to 0

for each fixed
k\leq K
.

Lemma C.9

(Approximate strict shadows from finite-mode flat jets)
.

Assume
C.1
and the high-frequency compatibility tail
estimate of
Lemma

C.7
. Let

\widetilde{V}_{n,K}^{\eta}=V_{n}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}Z_{k,n},\qquad\widetilde{Q}_{n,K}^{\eta}=Q_{n}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}\Pi_{k,n}.

Then for every fixed
0<\eta<(4A)^{-1}
, every
K
, and every
N
,

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{R}(\widetilde{V}_{n,K}^{\eta},\widetilde{Q}_{n,K}^{\eta})\|_{Z^{\prime}}+\|\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{V}_{n,K}^{\eta})\|_{\mathcal{Y}_{s-2-a}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M_{*},A)(A\eta)^{K+1}+C\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}\alpha_{k,N,n}+C\omega_{N},

where

\alpha_{k,N,n}:=\left\|P_{N}\left(D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z_{k,n}]+\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n})\right)\right\|_{Y_{N}}\to 0

for each fixed
k,N
. Moreover,

\widetilde{V}_{n,K}^{\eta}(s_{*})=V(s_{*})+\eta W(s_{*})+O(\eta^{2})+o_{n}(\eta)

in
L^{2}_{\varphi}
, uniformly for
K\geq 2
.

Proof.

Since
V_{n}
is an exact strict shadow,
\mathfrak{R}(V_{n},Q_{n})=0
and
\mathfrak{C}(V_{n})=0
. Expanding
in powers of
\eta
, the coefficients through order
K
vanish by the moving-base jet equations,
while the remaining quadratic terms are bounded by
C(M_{*},A)(A\eta)^{K+1}
. For the
compatibility map, the quadratic identity
\mathfrak{C}(Y)=\mathfrak{B}(Y,Y)
gives the coefficient expansion

\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{V}_{n,K}^{\eta})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}\left(D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z_{k,n}]+\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n},Z_{j,n})\right)+O((A\eta)^{K+1}).

Splitting each coefficient into low and high modes yields the stated residual estimate.

For the trace, write

\widetilde{V}_{n,K}^{\eta}(s_{*})-V(s_{*})=V_{n}(s_{*})-V(s_{*})+\eta Z_{1,n}(s_{*})+\sum_{k=2}^{K}\eta^{k}Z_{k,n}(s_{*}).

The first two terms give
\eta W(s_{*})+o_{n}(\eta)
, and the geometric bound gives the

O(\eta^{2})
estimate for the remaining sum.
∎

Theorem C.4

(Flat-branch exactification by compactness)
.

Assume that a nonzero failed-selection branch is finite-mode flat and satisfies

C.1
. Assume also the high-frequency tail estimate of

Lemma

C.7
. Then

W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}.

Consequently, the finite-mode flat branch cannot produce a genuine failed-selection sequence.

Proof.

Fix
0<\eta<(4A)^{-1}
. Choose sequences
K_{j}\to\infty
,
N_{j}\to\infty
, and

n_{j}\to\infty
so that
(A\eta)^{K_{j}+1}\to 0
,
\omega_{N_{j}}\to 0
, and, for every

1\leq k\leq K_{j}
,
\alpha_{k,N_{j},n_{j}}\to 0
. Define

\widetilde{V}_{j}^{\eta}:=V_{n_{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{K_{j}}\eta^{k}Z_{k,n_{j}},\qquad\widetilde{Q}_{j}^{\eta}:=Q_{n_{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{K_{j}}\eta^{k}\Pi_{k,n_{j}}.

By
Lemma

C.9
, the strict residual and compatibility residual tend
to zero. The majorant bound gives a uniform
X_{s}\times P_{s}
bound. Therefore

Lemma

C.8
gives an exact strict shadow
(V^{\eta},Q^{\eta})

such that

V^{\eta}(s_{*})=V(s_{*})+\eta W(s_{*})+O(\eta^{2}).

Let
\eta_{\ell}\downarrow 0
. Applying the construction with
\eta=\eta_{\ell}
, and taking

\eta_{\ell}
small enough to preserve the admissibility bound, gives exact strict shadows in

\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}
with

\frac{V^{\eta_{\ell}}(s_{*})-V(s_{*})}{\eta_{\ell}}\to W(s_{*})

strongly in
L^{2}_{\varphi}
. Hence
W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}
. The first-variation
orthogonality from Appendix
B
then contradicts strong trace non-loss, excluding the branch.
∎

C.12
All-order moving-base exactification of the flat non-tame branch

The compact exactification theorem above is the safest formulation. We now record a more direct
all-order version: finite-mode flatness and high-frequency tail control give compatible jets at the
limiting nonzero shadow, and a strict parabolic majorant sums those jets to an exact curve.

Let
(V,Q)\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm mstr})
,
V\neq 0
, be the limiting nonzero strict shadow, and let

W
be the normalized blow-up direction produced by a genuinely surviving finite-mode flat
failed-selection branch. Define

\mathscr{L}_{V}(R,\Pi):=\partial_{t}R_{h}-\Delta R_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes R_{h}+R_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi.

Proposition C.11

(All-order moving-base jets at the limiting shadow)
.

Assume that the nonzero failed-selection branch is finite-mode flat and survives every positive
finite-power alternative. Assume also the moving-base finite-order trace-flatness iteration and the
high-frequency compatibility tail estimate. Then, after a diagonal extraction, there exist jets

(R_{k},\Pi_{k})_{k\geq 1},\qquad R_{1}=W,

such that
R_{k}\in X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})
,
\Pi_{k}\in P_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})
, and the following hierarchy
holds. For
k=1
,

\mathscr{L}_{V}(R_{1},\Pi_{1})=0,\qquad\nabla_{h}\cdot R_{1,h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi_{1}=0,\qquad D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[R_{1}]=0.

For every
k\geq 2
,

\mathscr{L}_{V}(R_{k},\Pi_{k})=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=k\\
i,j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\nabla_{h}\cdot(R_{i,h}\otimes R_{j,h}),

with

\nabla_{h}\cdot R_{k,h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi_{k}=0,

and

D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[R_{k}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=k\\
i,j\geq 1\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{B}(R_{i},R_{j})=0.

Proof.

Fix
K\geq 2
. By the moving-base finite-order trace-flatness iteration, one obtains finite-level
jets around
V_{n}
through order
K
. Since the branch survives every positive finite-power
alternative, it is
K
-sharp, so all raw residuals are negligible up to order
K
. Parabolic
compactness on interior cylinders gives, for each fixed order
k
,

Z_{k,n}\to R_{k},\qquad\Pi_{k,n}\rightharpoonup\Pi_{k}

in the required local topologies. Passing to the limit gives the linearized parabolic jet equations.
For compatibility, finite-mode flatness gives the vanishing of every projected coefficient, and

Proposition

C.9
upgrades this to full weak compatibility in

\mathcal{Y}_{s-2-a}
. Passing to the limit gives the displayed compatibility identity. A diagonal
extraction in
K
gives the full infinite jet sequence.
∎

Lemma C.10

(Linearized strict parabolic majorant around
V
)
.

Let
s>5/2
. There exists a constant
C_{V}
, depending only on the interior cylinder chain and
on
\|V\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm msh})}
, such that the following holds. Suppose
(R,\Pi)
solves

\mathscr{L}_{V}(R,\Pi)=F,\qquad\nabla_{h}\cdot R_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi=0,

with zero lower-time trace in the localized construction. Then

\|R\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm min})}+\|\Pi\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm min})}\leq C_{V}\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}(Q_{\rm mmid})}.

Consequently, if

F=-\sum_{i+j=k}\nabla_{h}\cdot(R_{i,h}\otimes R_{j,h}),

then

\|R_{k}\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm min})}+\|\Pi_{k}\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm min})}\leq C_{V}\sum_{i+j=k}\|R_{i}\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})}\|R_{j}\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm mmid})}.

Proof.

This is the localized parabolic estimate for a linear system with coefficients controlled by the
strict shadow
V
; see, for example, the standard parabolic estimates in

[
41
,
42
]
. The drift term is bounded by

\|\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes R_{h}+R_{h}\otimes V_{h})\|_{H^{s-1}}\leq C_{s}\|V\|_{H^{s}}\|R\|_{H^{s}}.

Standard interior estimates for linear parabolic equations with bounded strong coefficients yield the
first inequality, with constant depending on
\|V\|_{X_{s}}
and the cylinder chain. For the
quadratic forcing,
s>5/2
makes
H^{s}
a Banach algebra, so

\|\nabla_{h}\cdot(R_{i,h}\otimes R_{j,h})\|_{H^{s-1}}\leq C_{s}\|R_{i}\|_{H^{s}}\|R_{j}\|_{H^{s}}.

Taking
L^{2}_{t}
-norms gives the majorant estimate.
∎

Lemma C.11

(Catalan majorant for the nonzero moving-base jets)
.

Let

a_{k}:=\|R_{k}\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm min})}+\|\Pi_{k}\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm min})}.

Then there exist constants
A_{0},A_{1}>0
such that

a_{k}\leq A_{0}A_{1}^{k},\qquad k\geq 1.

In particular, the series
\sum_{k\geq 1}\eta^{k}R_{k}
and
\sum_{k\geq 1}\eta^{k}\Pi_{k}
converge in

X_{s}(Q_{\rm min})\times P_{s}(Q_{\rm min})
whenever
|\eta|<A_{1}^{-1}
.

Proof.

The first coefficient
R_{1}=W
is smooth in the interior by parabolic smoothing of the linearized
strict system, so
a_{1}<\infty
. For
k\geq 2
,

Lemma

C.10
gives

a_{k}\leq C_{V}\sum_{i+j=k}a_{i}a_{j}.

Let
b_{1}=a_{1}
and define recursively
b_{k}=C_{V}\sum_{i+j=k}b_{i}b_{j}
. Then
a_{k}\leq b_{k}
. The
generating function
B(z)=\sum_{k\geq 1}b_{k}z^{k}
satisfies

B(z)=a_{1}z+C_{V}B(z)^{2}.

Thus
B
has positive radius of convergence, at least
(4C_{V}a_{1})^{-1}
. Hence

b_{k}\leq A_{0}A_{1}^{k}
for suitable constants, and the same bound holds for
a_{k}
.
∎

Theorem C.5

(Flat non-tame branch reduction)
.

Let

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad V_{n}\to V\neq 0,

be a nonzero failed-selection branch which survives every positive finite-power alternative. Assume
the moving-base finite-order trace-flatness iteration and the high-frequency compatibility tail
estimate. If the branch is finite-mode flat and the all-order jet sequence satisfies the strong-space
majorant above, then

W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}.

Consequently, the finite-mode flat non-tame branch cannot produce a genuine failed-selection
sequence.

Proof.

By
Proposition

C.11
, the finite-mode flat branch produces an all-order
compatible moving-base jet sequence
(R_{k},\Pi_{k})_{k\geq 1}
, with
R_{1}=W
. By

Lemma

C.11
, the series

V^{\eta}:=V+\sum_{k\geq 1}\eta^{k}R_{k},\qquad Q^{\eta}:=Q+\sum_{k\geq 1}\eta^{k}\Pi_{k}

converges in
X_{s}(Q_{\rm min})\times P_{s}(Q_{\rm min})
for
|\eta|
small. Since the series
converges absolutely and
s>5/2
, products may be summed term by term. The coefficient of

\eta
in the strict equation is
\mathscr{L}_{V}(R_{1},\Pi_{1})=0
. For
k\geq 2
, the coefficient of

\eta^{k}
is

\mathscr{L}_{V}(R_{k},\Pi_{k})+\sum_{i+j=k}\nabla_{h}\cdot(R_{i,h}\otimes R_{j,h}),

which vanishes by the jet equation. Thus
(V^{\eta},Q^{\eta})
solves the strict equation. The
compatibility identity gives
\mathfrak{C}(V^{\eta})=0
, equivalently
\partial_{3}Q^{\eta}=0
modulo
horizontal harmonic pressures. Therefore
(V^{\eta},Q^{\eta})
is an exact strict shadow.

For
|\eta|
sufficiently small, the admissibility bound defining
\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}
is preserved after the
standard harmless enlargement of the local shadow constant. Moreover,

\frac{V^{\eta}(s_{*})-V(s_{*})}{\eta}=R_{1}(s_{*})+\sum_{k\geq 2}\eta^{k-1}R_{k}(s_{*})\to W(s_{*})

strongly in
L^{2}_{\varphi}
. Hence
W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}
. The first-variation
orthogonality from Appendix
B
then gives
\int\varphi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=0
, while strong trace non-loss
gives
\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=1
. This contradiction excludes the branch.
∎

C.13
Consequences for the final dependency chain

The goal of this appendix is not to repeat the implication from finite-power selection to the logarithmic
theorem. That implication is established in Appendix
B
. The purpose here is to identify the singular
geometric alternatives under which the strict-shadow reduction proves finite-power selection.

Theorem C.6

(Strict-shadow reduction under the stated analytic inputs)
.

Assume the analytic trace-drop and trace non-loss conclusions, including the sharp admissible-time intersection input of Appendix
B
, and assume the moving-base finite-order trace-flatness conclusions used in the preceding sections. Assume also the strong-space parabolic
majorant hypotheses needed to sum compatible jets. Then every failed sharp finite-power selection
sequence is excluded. Consequently, the subcritical finite-power selection principle holds:

E^{\ell}_{\varphi}(s_{\ell};U^{\ell},V^{\ell})\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b}

for some
0<\mu<1/6
. Hence the logarithmic harmonic-pressure approximation and the
logarithmic regularity-radius lower bound follow:

\mathcal{X}^{\rm har}_{\theta/4}(u,p;M)\leq C_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma},

and

r_{\rm reg}(0,0)\geq c_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma/3}.

Proof.

Let a failed sharp finite-power selection sequence be given. Passing to the normalized blow-up
yields a limiting strict shadow
V
and a normalized direction
W
.

If
V
lies on a fully regular stratum for the full strict parabolic map, the implicit-function theorem gives

W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}
, and the first-variation contradiction follows.
If
V=0
,
Theorem

C.2
gives the same tangent-cone inclusion.
If
V\neq 0
and the singular stratum is tame finite-codimensional, then

Theorem

C.3
excludes the branch.

It remains to consider the nonzero non-tame case. If some finite-order, finite-mode obstruction is
visible, the finite-mode obstruction dichotomy gives

m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K}

for some finite
K
, which is already a positive finite-power selection estimate and contradicts
genuine failure. Therefore every finite-mode obstruction is invisible, and the branch is finite-mode
flat. By
Theorem

C.5
,

W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}.

The first-variation orthogonality again gives
\int\varphi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=0
, whereas strong
trace non-loss gives
\frac{1}{2}\int\varphi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=1
. This contradiction excludes the
final branch.

Thus no failed sharp finite-power selection sequence exists. The finite-power selection estimate
follows. The implication from finite-power selection to the logarithmic harmonic-pressure
approximation and then to the logarithmic regularity-radius lower bound is the Appendix
B
reduction.
∎

C.14
Role in the final dependency chain and remaining inputs

The state of the program after this appendix is as follows.

C.14.1
Established chain

Appendices
A
and
B
establish, modulo the finite-power selection input,

\displaystyle\text{subcritical finite-power selection}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\rm har}_{\theta/4}(u,p;M)\lesssim|\log\delta|^{-\sigma},

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow r_{\rm reg}(0,0)\gtrsim|\log\delta|^{-\sigma/3}.

The normalized covariance stress, localized variance cutoff error, one-component residual, and
vertical pressure-compatibility defect vanish at subcritical scales. The high-frequency trace-loss
part of the blow-up argument has been separated from the strict-shadow geometry and is treated by a
parabolic frequency-drop mechanism, conditional on the sharp admissible-time intersection input isolated in Appendix
B
.

C.14.2
What this appendix adds

This paper adds the singular-stratum analysis. It identifies the nonlinear constraint
\mathfrak{C}(V)=0

as the source of the strict shadow geometry. It closes the zero-shadow branch through finite-order
sharpness, obstruction inheritance, iterated jet extraction, and analytic summation. It closes tame
finite-codimensional nonzero strata by moving-base Lyapunov–Schmidt reduction. For the remaining
non-tame branch, it introduces a finite-mode dichotomy: visible finite-mode obstructions already give
finite-power selection, while invisible branches are finite-mode flat. Under the stated moving-base
trace-flatness and strong-space majorant inputs, finite-mode flat branches exactify to strict curves
and hence also satisfy the tangent-cone inclusion required by Appendix
B
.

C.14.3
Remaining analytic input

The remaining structural point is no longer a geometric classification of the whole singular set

\mathfrak{C}^{-1}(0)
. The strict-shadow geometry has been reduced to explicit analytic inputs: the
moving-base finite-order trace-flatness iteration, the strong-space bounds for finite-mode flat
realizers, and the parabolic majorant estimates needed to pass from finite-order jets to an exact
strict curve. The high-frequency compatibility tail estimate is proved above. A relaxed-shadow
replacement, in which the condition
\partial_{3}Q=0
is softened and the resulting vertical residual
is paired with the small component, remains a possible alternative formulation.

C.15
Conclusion

This appendix isolates the geometric obstruction in the strict two-and-a-half-dimensional reduction to
logarithmic finite-scale one-component regularity. The obstruction is the singular geometry of the
pressure-compatibility constraint

\mathfrak{C}(V)=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b})=0.

Quadratic compatibility of blow-up directions is necessary and is inherited from failed selection,
but it is not automatically sufficient for curve selection.

The zero-shadow branch is closed by an all-order jet construction. Each finite-order obstruction either
yields a positive finite-power selection estimate already, or vanishes by coefficient extraction from
the sharpened failed-selection sequence. If the sequence survives all finite-power alternatives, it
produces compatible jets to every order, and a localized strict Stokes majorant sums these jets to an
exact strict shadow curve.

The nonzero singular theory is organized into tame and non-tame branches. Tame finite-codimensional
strata are handled by moving-base Lyapunov–Schmidt reduction. The non-tame branch is handled by a
finite-mode dichotomy: a finite-dimensional visible obstruction gives finite-power selection, while a
branch with no visible finite-mode obstruction is finite-mode flat. Low-frequency flatness combined
with high-frequency Sobolev tail control gives full weak compatibility of the moving-base jet
coefficients. Under the stated moving-base trace-flatness and parabolic majorant inputs, these jets
exactify to strict curves and yield the required tangent-cone inclusion.

Consequently, the technical appendices fit into the final dependency chain as follows. Appendix
A
proves the unconditional low-frequency pieces of the analytic preparation and
variance-buffered stability engine. Appendix
B
proves the logarithmic consequence of subcritical
finite-power strict shadow selection and reduces that selection to tangent-cone closure. Appendix
C

organizes the singular-stratum geometry that supplies this closure whenever the stated moving-base
trace-flatness and majorant inputs are available. With those analytic inputs in hand, the Appendix
B

selection principle closes and the logarithmic one-component regularity bound follows.

Appendix D
Frequency-split finite-window construction

D.1
Introduction

This appendix records the frequency-split finite-stage exactification component of a logarithmic finite-scale one-component
regularity program for suitable weak solutions of the three-dimensional
incompressible Navier–Stokes equations. The final target is a logarithmic lower
bound for the local regularity radius under a scale-invariant bound and smallness
of the vertical component,

\Phi(1)\leq M,\qquad C_{3}(1)=\delta\ll 1\quad\Longrightarrow\quad r_{\rm reg}(0,0)\geq c_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma/3}.

(D.1)

The preceding appendices separate the analytic and geometric parts of this implication.
Appendix
A
constructs a low-frequency horizontal preparation and a variance-buffered
relative-energy estimate. The key object is the covariance stress

\tau^{\ell}=S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})-S_{\ell}u_{h}\otimes S_{\ell}u_{h},\qquad\kappa^{\ell}=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\tau^{\ell},

whose unresolved variance cancels the dangerous high-frequency stress term in the
relative-energy identity. Appendix
B
proves that the logarithmic theorem follows
from a subcritical strict shadow selection estimate of the form

\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|U^{\ell}(s_{\ell})-V^{\ell}(s_{\ell})|^{2}\,dx+\int\phi\kappa^{\ell}(s_{\ell})\,dx\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b},\qquad 0<\mu<\frac{1}{6}.

(D.2)

It further reduces this selection estimate to a tangent-cone inclusion for
normalized blow-up directions of failed sharp selection sequences. Appendix
C

analyzes the singular geometry of the strict shadow class. The strict pressure
condition is encoded, modulo horizontal harmonic pressure ambiguity, by the
quadratic compatibility map

\mathfrak{C}(V):=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b})=0,\qquad a,b\in\{1,2\}.

(D.3)

At fully regular strata, the tangent-cone inclusion follows from an implicit-function
argument. At the zero shadow, Appendix
C
develops an all-order jet construction. At
nonzero tame singular strata, it uses a moving-base Lyapunov–Schmidt scheme. The
remaining branch is the nonzero finite-mode flat non-tame branch.

The most direct formulation of the last branch asks for bounded moving-base
realizers. If

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},\qquad V_{n}\to V\neq 0,

is a finite-mode flat failed-selection branch, one might try to prove that for
every order
K
there are moving-base jets

(Z_{1,n},\Pi_{1,n}),\ldots,(Z_{K,n},\Pi_{K,n})

with
Z_{1,n}-W_{n}\to 0
, satisfying the moving-base jet equations, and obeying a
uniform geometric strong-space bound

\left\|Z_{k,n}\right\|_{X_{s}}+\left\|\Pi_{k,n}\right\|_{P_{s}}\leq M_{*}A^{k},\qquad 1\leq k\leq K,

(D.4)

with
M_{*}
and
A
independent of
K,n
. This paper explains why
(
D.4
) is not the right final target. The normalized blow-up
trace is obtained in the localized energy topology, and Appendix
B
gives strong
non-loss only in
L^{2}_{\phi}
at the selected slice. One should not force the trace

W(s_{*})
to possess uniform
H^{s}
-regularity. More structurally, the linearized
compatibility operator
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}
is microlocally degenerate on horizontal
divergence-free high-frequency modes; a global elliptic observability estimate for
approximate kernels is false.

The replacement is frequency-split exactification. The low-frequency part of the
blow-up trace is exactified in strong spaces, with constants allowed to depend on
the frequency window. The high-frequency trace part is harmless because the
high-frequency trace-drop argument of Appendix
B
gives trace tightness at sharp
good-time minimizers. The high-frequency compatibility part is harmless because
Appendix
C
proves a Sobolev tail estimate for the compatibility operator. The
amplitude
\eta
of the strict-shadow curve is then chosen small relative to the
low-frequency majorant constant. This gives exact strict shadows with difference
quotients converging to
W(s_{*})
in
L^{2}_{\phi}
, which is exactly the tangent-cone
inclusion required by Appendix
B
.

D.1.1
Main contribution of this appendix

The main contribution is the following principle:

\boxed{\begin{gathered}\text{finite-mode flatness handles low-frequency obstructions,}\\
\text{parabolic trace drop removes high-frequency trace loss,}\\
\text{Sobolev tail estimates remove high-frequency compatibility defects.}\end{gathered}}

Thus the last non-tame branch is reduced to fixed finite windows without proving the all-frequency bound
(
D.4
). Under an additional uniform analytic-germ and nondegenerate-minor hypothesis, this appendix gives an optional finite-power exactification route. Without that hypothesis, the finite-window objects constructed here are passed to the trace-cost mechanism of Appendix
E
and the vertical-duality closure of Appendix
F
. This realization replaces the abstract sectorial trace-density formulation and does not require an all-frequency strong bound for the approximating sequence. It is paired with a finite-mode range-gauge correction for the compatibility coefficients, while the final theorem uses selected-time trace cost rather than a global finite-power strong inverse.

D.1.2
Technical organization

Section

D.2
records the inherited objects from Appendices
B
and
C
.
Section

D.3

proves the microlocal degeneracy of
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}
on horizontal divergence-free modes,
which explains why all-frequency bounded realizers are too strong.
Section

D.4

introduces frequency windows and the localized strict first-order realization.

Section

D.5
constructs finite-window moving-base jets and proves their
Catalan-type bounds.
Section

D.6
replaces the full-range quotient by
the homogeneous strict quotient and proves the finite-mode obstruction dichotomy.

Section

D.7
uses the Sobolev tail estimate in an amplitude-weighted form.

Section

D.8
constructs diagonal approximate strict shadows, proves the
branch-native residual estimate, states the conditional finite-power analytic-minor bound for the
finite-stage inverse, and derives conditional diagonal compatibility from the genuinely
surviving sharpness
\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{R})
for every finite
R
.

Section

D.9
derives the tangent-cone inclusion, and
Section

D.10

reconnects the resulting selection principle to the logarithmic radius bound.

D.2
Setup inherited from the preceding parts

We work on fixed nested interior cylinders

Q_{\theta/4}\Subset Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}\Subset Q_{\rm str}\Subset Q_{\rm prep}\Subset Q_{3/4}.

(D.5)

Let
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{\rm sh})
,
0\leq\phi\leq 1
, be identically one on

Q_{\rm tar}
. All constants may depend on
M,\theta
, and on the cylinder
chain. Horizontal indices
a,b
always range over
\{1,2\}
.

D.2.1
Strong parabolic spaces

Fix a Sobolev index

s>\frac{5}{2}.

(D.6)

For a cylinder
Q=I\times B
, set

X_{s}(Q):=L^{\infty}(I;H^{s}(B))\cap L^{2}(I;H^{s+1}(B)),

(D.7)

and let
P_{s}(Q)
denote the associated pressure space

P_{s}(Q):=L^{2}(I;H^{s}(B)/\mathcal{H}_{h}(B)),

(D.8)

where
\mathcal{H}_{h}(B)
denotes the horizontal harmonic ambiguity. The
compatibility-defect space is denoted by
Y_{s-2-a}
, with
0<a<a_{0}
, and the
strict residual space by
Z_{s}^{\prime}
. The precise localization of these spaces is fixed
once and for all. The only properties used below are the parabolic estimates, compact
embeddings on smaller cylinders, and the algebra property of
H^{s}
.

D.2.2
Compatibility map

For a horizontal vector field
Y=(Y_{h},0)
with
\nabla_{h}\!\cdot Y_{h}=0
, define

\mathfrak{C}(Y):=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(Y_{a}Y_{b}),

(D.9)

and the associated bilinear form

\mathfrak{B}(A,B):=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(A_{a}B_{b}).

(D.10)

Then
\mathfrak{C}(Y)=\mathfrak{B}(Y,Y)
. At a strict shadow
V
, the differential is

D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[Z]=\mathfrak{B}(V,Z)+\mathfrak{B}(Z,V).

(D.11)

The strict pressure condition
\partial_{3}Q=0
, together with

-\Delta_{h}Q=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b})
, is equivalent to
\mathfrak{C}(V)=0
modulo
horizontal harmonic pressure functions.

D.2.3
Failed-selection branches

A nonzero finite-mode flat failed-selection branch is written as

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},\qquad V_{n}\to V\neq 0,

(D.12)

where
(V_{n},Q_{n})\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm str})
are exact strict shadows and
m_{n}
is
the squared sharp covariance-calibrated distance. The raw defect scale is

\rho_{n}:=\ell_{n}^{1/6}+\ell_{n}^{-N}\delta_{n}^{b}.

(D.13)

The branch is called genuinely surviving if it survives every positive finite-power
alternative. Equivalently, for every finite
K
, one has

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K}).

(D.14)

Indeed, if (
D.14
) failed at order
K
, then

\varepsilon_{n}^{K}\lesssim\rho_{n}
, and therefore

m_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/K},

(D.15)

which is already a positive finite-power selection estimate.

The normalized blow-up sequence has strong trace non-loss:

\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=1,

(D.16)

where
W_{n}\to W
in the local topology and
s_{n}\to s_{*}
. Appendix
B
also gives the
first-variation orthogonality:
W(s_{*})
is orthogonal in
L^{2}_{\phi}
to every
integrable strict tangent trace. Thus to exclude a failed branch, it is enough to
show

W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}.

(D.17)

D.2.4
Trace tightness

Let
J_{\Lambda}
be a standard localized Littlewood low-frequency cutoff to
frequencies
|\xi|\lesssim\Lambda
. The high-frequency trace-drop argument of
Appendix
B
gives

\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left\|(I-J_{\Lambda})W_{n}(s_{n})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}=0.

(D.18)

Consequently,

J_{\Lambda}W_{n}(s_{n})\to W(s_{*})\quad\text{strongly in }L^{2}_{\phi},

(D.19)

with the usual order of limits
n\to\infty
, then
\Lambda\to\infty
.

D.3
Microlocal degeneracy of the compatibility operator

This section explains why the all-frequency bounded-realizer principle is too strong.
The linearized compatibility operator is not elliptic on the physically relevant
horizontal divergence-free high-frequency cone.

Proposition D.1

(Principal-symbol degeneracy)
.

Let
V
be frozen at a point and consider a high-frequency horizontal perturbation

Z_{h}(x)=a_{h}e^{i\xi\cdot x},\qquad\xi_{h}\cdot a_{h}=0.

The principal symbol of
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}
vanishes on such modes:

\sigma_{\rm prin}(D\mathfrak{C}_{V})(\xi)a_{h}=0.

(D.20)

Proof.

Ignoring lower-order commutators generated by spatial variation of
V
, the scalar
part of
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}[Z]
is

\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}Z_{b}+Z_{a}V_{b}).

For the mode
Z_{h}=a_{h}e^{i\xi\cdot x}
, the principal scalar symbol is

-\xi_{a}\xi_{b}(V_{a}a_{b}+a_{a}V_{b})=-2(\xi_{h}\cdot V_{h})(\xi_{h}\cdot a_{h}).

The horizontal solenoidal condition gives
\xi_{h}\cdot a_{h}=0
. Hence this principal
scalar symbol vanishes. The remaining factor

\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}
cannot recover a nonzero principal symbol from zero.
Thus the principal symbol of
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}
vanishes on the horizontal divergence-free
cone.
∎

Remark D.1

(No all-frequency elliptic observability)
.

Proposition

D.1
rules out an estimate of the form

\left\|Z\right\|_{X_{s}}\lesssim\left\|\mathcal{L}_{V}Z\right\|_{Z_{s}^{\prime}}+\left\|D\mathfrak{C}_{V}Z\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}+\left\|Z(s)\right\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}

with constants independent of frequency. High-frequency horizontal divergence-free
modes may have large strong norms while producing small compatibility defects in the
negative topology. The correct strategy is therefore to work in fixed frequency
windows and let the curve amplitude absorb the frequency-window constants.

D.4
Frequency windows and strict first-order traces

The role of a frequency window is to replace the unavailable all-frequency right
inverse of
D\mathfrak{C}_{V}
by a finite-dimensional, localized strict tangent
approximation. The window size is eventually sent to infinity after the
exact-shadow amplitude has been chosen small enough. This section expands the
implementation point that was isolated in the auxiliary formulation as localized strict
Galerkin density.

D.4.1
Frequency projectors and cutoff commutators

Let
J_{\Lambda}
be a standard localized Littlewood projector on
Q_{\rm sh}
,
used after multiplication by fixed cutoffs supported in
Q_{\rm str}
. Its
high-frequency complement is
J_{\Lambda}^{\perp}=I-J_{\Lambda}
. For every
r\leq s
,

\left\|J_{\Lambda}f\right\|_{H^{s}}\leq C\Lambda^{s-r}\left\|f\right\|_{H^{r}}.

(D.21)

The following elementary commutator estimate is used whenever a Littlewood window is
moved across a localization cutoff.

Lemma D.1

(Localized Littlewood commutator)
.

Let
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{\rm str})
be fixed and equal to one on

Q_{\rm sh}
. For every integer
N\geq 1
and every Sobolev index
r
,

\left\|[J_{\Lambda},\chi]f\right\|_{H^{r}(Q_{\rm sh})}\leq C_{\chi,N,r}\Lambda^{-N}\left\|f\right\|_{H^{r-N}(Q_{\rm str})}.

(D.22)

The same estimate holds in the corresponding localized parabolic norms.

Proof.

Write
J_{\Lambda}
in a local chart as a Fourier multiplier with smooth compactly
supported symbol and kernel
K_{\Lambda}(x,y)=\Lambda^{3}K(\Lambda(x-y))
, up to the
fixed partition of unity. Then

[J_{\Lambda},\chi]f(x)=\int K_{\Lambda}(x,y)(\chi(y)-\chi(x))f(y)\,dy.

Taylor expansion of
\chi(y)-\chi(x)
, followed by integration by parts in the
oscillatory representation of the kernel, gains an arbitrary power of

\Lambda^{-1}
. Summing over the finite partition of unity gives
(
D.22
). The parabolic version follows after integration in time.
∎

D.4.2
The linearized strict trace and interior smoothing

Fix a strict shadow
(V,Q)\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm str})
and a time
s_{*}
in the
interior time interval. We define the localized linearized strict trace space by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\rm lin}_{V,s_{*}}:=\bigl\{Z(s_{*}):

\displaystyle(Z,\Pi)\text{ is a local-energy solution on }Q_{\rm sh}\text{ of}

(D.23)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Z_{h}-\Delta Z_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes Z_{h}+Z_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi=0,

\displaystyle\nabla_{h}\!\cdot Z_{h}=0,\qquad Z=(Z_{h},0),\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi=0\bigr\}.

The trace is understood in the weighted topology
L^{2}_{\phi}
. The pressure is
taken modulo the horizontal harmonic ambiguity fixed in
P_{s}
. In this subsection
we shrink
Q_{\rm sh}
once, if necessary, while keeping

Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}\Subset Q_{\rm str}
. This harmless shrinkage ensures
that all small time shifts
t\mapsto t+s_{*}-s_{n}
remain inside
Q_{\rm str}
for
all sufficiently large
n
.

Lemma D.2

(Blow-up trace is a linearized strict trace)
.

Let
(U_{n},P_{n},\tau_{n})
be a failed sharp finite-power selection sequence inherited
from Appendix
B
, and write

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},\qquad(V_{n},Q_{n})\to(V,Q)

locally smoothly on
Q_{\rm sh}
. If
s_{n}\to s_{*}
, and
W
is the normalized
blow-up limit supplied by Appendix
B
, then there is a pressure
\Pi
, unique modulo the
fixed horizontal harmonic gauge, such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}W_{h}-\Delta W_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes W_{h}+W_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi=0,

\displaystyle\nabla_{h}\!\cdot W_{h}=0,\qquad W=(W_{h},0),\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi=0.

in
Q_{\rm sh}
. Moreover

W(s_{*})\in\mathcal{T}^{\rm lin}_{V,s_{*}},

(D.24)

and the selected traces satisfy the strong non-loss convergence

\phi^{1/2}W_{n}(s_{n})\to\phi^{1/2}W(s_{*})\quad\text{strongly in }L^{2},\qquad\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=1.

(D.25)

Proof.

The normalized compactness theorem of Appendix
B
