gives a subsequence such that

W_{n}\to W
strongly in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}
and weakly in the local energy class. The
covariance term, the quadratic normalized term, the one-component residual, and the
vertical pressure-compatibility defect vanish in the normalized limit. Passing to the
limit in the normalized difference equation therefore gives the displayed homogeneous
linearized strict system for
W
and
\Pi
. The horizontal divergence-free condition
and the zero third component pass to the limit from the prepared fields. The vertical
pressure condition
\partial_{3}\Pi=0
is exactly the normalized limit of the strict
pressure-compatibility defect.

Appendix
B
also gives the strong trace non-loss statement at the selected good times.
The trace is identified by the uniform negative-Sobolev bound on
\partial_{t}W_{n}
:
testing against a smooth compactly supported function and letting
t\to s_{*}
identifies
the weak trace of the limit with the strong
L^{2}_{\phi}
-limit of
W_{n}(s_{n})
. Hence

W(s_{*})
is the weighted trace of a finite-energy solution of the homogeneous
linearized strict system, which proves (
D.24
) and
(
D.25
).
∎

Lemma D.3

(Interior strict smoothing of the blow-up limit)
.

Let
(V,Q)\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm str})
be fixed, and let
(W,\Pi)
be the homogeneous
linearized strict solution from
Lemma

D.2
. Then, for every
interior cylinder
Q_{\rm core}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}
whose time projection contains

s_{*}
, and for every
s>5/2
,

\left\|W\right\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm core})}+\left\|\Pi\right\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm core})}<\infty.

(D.26)

In particular,
W(s_{*})
may be used as a smooth strict trace on the smaller cylinder.

Proof.

The strict limiting-class smoothing package inherited from the preceding parts gives
smooth interior bounds for
V
and for the strict pressure
Q
. On
Q_{\rm sh}
,
the field
W
solves the linear parabolic system

\partial_{t}W_{h}-\Delta W_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes W_{h}+W_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi=0,\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot W_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi=0.

Choose
Q_{\rm core}\Subset Q_{\rm mid}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}
. After multiplying by a
cutoff which is identically one on
Q_{\rm core}
, the equation becomes a localized
linear Stokes system with smooth coefficients and commutator forcing controlled by the
local energy norm of
W
. Standard interior
L^{2}
-based parabolic regularity gives

W\in L^{2}_{t}H^{m+1}_{x}\cap H^{1}_{t}H^{m-1}_{x}
on
Q_{\rm core}
for every finite
integer
m
, after bootstrapping.

The pressure is recovered from the horizontal divergence equation

-\Delta_{h}\Pi=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}W_{b}+W_{a}V_{b})

modulo the fixed horizontal harmonic gauge. Since
\partial_{3}\Pi=0
holds
distributionally, the elliptic estimates in the quotient pressure space give

\Pi\in P_{s}(Q_{\rm core})
. Taking
m
large enough yields
(
D.26
).
∎

D.4.3
Moving-base first-order realization

The preceding lemma removes the need for an abstract sectorial realization of the
linearized trace space. For the first-order window used in the frequency-split
argument, it is enough to transport the actual smooth blow-up limit
W
from the
limiting base to the moving bases.

Lemma D.4

(Moving-base first-order strict realization)
.

Assume
(V_{n},Q_{n})\to(V,Q)
locally smoothly on
Q_{\rm sh}
,
s_{n}\to s_{*}
, and

W_{n}(s_{n})\to W(s_{*})\qquad\text{strongly in }L^{2}_{\phi}.

Let
(W,\Pi)
be the linearized strict solution from
Lemma

D.2
.
For each fixed
\Lambda\geq 1
, define

Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}(x,t):=W(x,t+s_{*}-s_{n}),\qquad\Pi_{1,n}^{\Lambda}(x,t):=\Pi(x,t+s_{*}-s_{n}).

The construction is independent of
\Lambda
; the parameter is kept only to match
the later frequency-split notation. Then

Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}(s_{n})=W(s_{*}),\qquad Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}(s_{n})\to W(s_{*})\quad\text{in }L^{2}_{\phi}.

(D.27)

For every fixed
\Lambda
,

\left\|Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm sh})}+\left\|\Pi_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm sh})}\leq A_{\Lambda}

(D.28)

for all large
n
, with
A_{\Lambda}
in fact independent of
\Lambda
at this
first-order level. Moreover, with

\displaystyle r_{1,n}^{\Lambda}

\displaystyle:={\mathcal{L}}_{V_{n}}Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{1,n}^{\Lambda},

(D.29)

\displaystyle e_{1,n}^{\Lambda}

\displaystyle:=\partial_{3}\Pi_{1,n}^{\Lambda},

(D.30)

one has

\left\|r_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{Z_{s}^{\prime}}+\left\|e_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\to 0\qquad(n\to\infty).

(D.31)

Consequently,

\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\bigl(\left\|r_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{Z_{s}^{\prime}}+\left\|e_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\bigr)=0.

(D.32)

Finally,

\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left\|Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}(s_{n})-W_{n}(s_{n})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}=0.

(D.33)

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=V(x,t+s_{*}-s_{n}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=W(x,t+s_{*}-s_{n}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{n}(x,t)

\displaystyle:=\Pi(x,t+s_{*}-s_{n}).

By the harmless shrinkage of
Q_{\rm sh}
, these shifted fields are defined on

Q_{\rm sh}
for all sufficiently large
n
. Since
(W,\Pi)
solves the
homogeneous linearized strict system around
(V,Q)
, the shifted pair

(\widetilde{W}_{n},\widetilde{\Pi}_{n})
solves the same system around

\widetilde{V}_{n}
:

{\mathcal{L}}_{\widetilde{V}_{n}}\widetilde{W}_{n}+\nabla_{h}\widetilde{\Pi}_{n}=0,\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot\widetilde{W}_{n,h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\widetilde{\Pi}_{n}=0.

Thus
e_{1,n}^{\Lambda}=0
. The residual with respect to the moving base
V_{n}
is

\displaystyle r_{1,n}^{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{h}\cdot\Big((V_{n,h}-\widetilde{V}_{n,h})\otimes\widetilde{W}_{n,h}\Big)

\displaystyle+\nabla_{h}\cdot\Big(\widetilde{W}_{n,h}\otimes(V_{n,h}-\widetilde{V}_{n,h})\Big).

The local smooth convergence
V_{n}\to V
, together with
s_{n}\to s_{*}
, gives

V_{n}-\widetilde{V}_{n}\to 0

in every fixed smooth norm on
Q_{\rm sh}
. By

Lemma

D.3
,
\widetilde{W}_{n}
is uniformly bounded in

X_{s}(Q_{\rm sh})
. The product estimates defining
Z_{s}^{\prime}
therefore imply

\left\|r_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{Z_{s}^{\prime}}\to 0.

This proves (
D.31
), and
(
D.32
) follows immediately.

The bound (
D.28
) follows from the same interior smoothing
bound for
W
and
\Pi
. Finally,

Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}(s_{n})=W(s_{*}),

so the trace convergence from
Lemma

D.2
gives

\left\|Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}(s_{n})-W_{n}(s_{n})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}=\left\|W(s_{*})-W_{n}(s_{n})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}\to 0.

This proves the stronger form of (
D.33
).
∎

Proposition D.2

(Localized strict first-order realization)
.

Let
(V_{n},Q_{n})\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm str})
converge locally smoothly to
(V,Q)
, and
let
W_{n}\to W
be a normalized blow-up sequence whose limit solves the homogeneous
linearized strict system. Then, for every fixed
\Lambda\geq 1
, there exist smooth
fields
(Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda},\Pi_{1,n}^{\Lambda})
on
Q_{\rm sh}
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Z_{1,n,h}^{\Lambda}-\Delta Z_{1,n,h}^{\Lambda}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{n,h}\otimes Z_{1,n,h}^{\Lambda}+Z_{1,n,h}^{\Lambda}\otimes V_{n,h})+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{1,n}^{\Lambda}=r_{1,n}^{\Lambda},

(D.34)

\displaystyle\nabla_{h}\!\cdot Z_{1,n,h}^{\Lambda}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}\Pi_{1,n}^{\Lambda}=e_{1,n}^{\Lambda},

(D.35)

with

\left\|r_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{Z_{s}^{\prime}}+\left\|e_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\to 0\qquad(n\to\infty),

(D.36)

and

\left\|Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm sh})}+\left\|\Pi_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm sh})}\leq A_{\Lambda}.

(D.37)

Moreover,

\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left\|Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}(s_{n})-W_{n}(s_{n})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}=0.

(D.38)

Indeed, in this first-order realization the fields may be chosen independently of

\Lambda
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

D.2
, the blow-up trace
W(s_{*})
is the trace of a
homogeneous linearized strict solution
(W,\Pi)
. By

Lemma

D.3
, this solution is smooth in the interior strong
spaces used here. Applying
Lemma

D.4
gives the shifted
moving-base fields
(Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda},\Pi_{1,n}^{\Lambda})
, the residual convergence
(
D.36
), the bound (
D.37
), and the trace
convergence (
D.38
).
∎

Remark D.2

(Scope of Proposition
D.2
)
.

The proposition is deliberately formulated as a first-order realization rather than
as a full sectorial density theorem for the whole strict trace space. This removes
the gap involving an abstract localized sectorial operator on

\overline{\mathcal{T}^{\rm lin}_{V,s_{*}}}^{\,L^{2}_{\phi}}
. For the blow-up branch under consideration,
the actual limit
W
already solves the homogeneous linearized strict system and is
smooth on interior cylinders; transporting this solution to the moving bases gives the
required first-order window. The higher-order compatibility and gauge-realization
issues are separate and are handled later in the finite-mode and range-gauge steps.

D.5
Finite-window moving-base jets

Fix
\Lambda\geq 1
. Starting from
(Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda},\Pi_{1,n}^{\Lambda})
, we build
a finite-window moving-base jet hierarchy. The constants may depend on
\Lambda
,
but not on
n
once
n
is large.

D.5.1
Jet equations

Let

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}Z:=\partial_{t}Z_{h}-\Delta Z_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{n,h}\otimes Z_{h}+Z_{h}\otimes V_{n,h}).

(D.39)

For
k\geq 2
, define
(Z_{k,n}^{\Lambda},\Pi_{k,n}^{\Lambda})
by

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}Z_{k,n}^{\Lambda}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{k,n}^{\Lambda}=-\sum_{i+j=k}\nabla_{h}\cdot(Z_{i,n}^{\Lambda}\otimes Z_{j,n}^{\Lambda}),

(D.40)

with

\nabla_{h}\!\cdot Z_{k,n,h}^{\Lambda}=0.

(D.41)

The lower-time trace is fixed by a localized zero-trace gauge. The vertical strict
pressure condition is not imposed at this stage; its defect is measured by the
compatibility coefficient

\mathfrak{C}_{k,n}^{\Lambda}:=D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z_{k,n}^{\Lambda}]+\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n}^{\Lambda},Z_{j,n}^{\Lambda}),\qquad k\geq 2.

(D.42)

For
k=1
, set

\mathfrak{C}_{1,n}^{\Lambda}:=D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z_{1,n}^{\Lambda}].

(D.43)

The first-order residuals in
Proposition

D.2
imply

\mathfrak{C}_{1,n}^{\Lambda}\to 0
in the weak compatibility topology as
n\to\infty
for
fixed
\Lambda
.

D.5.2
Catalan majorant in a fixed window

Lemma D.5

(Fixed-window higher jets and Catalan bound)
.

For each fixed
\Lambda
there exists
A_{\Lambda}\geq 1
such that, for every

k\geq 1
,

\left\|Z_{k,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})}+\left\|\Pi_{k,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})}\leq A_{\Lambda}^{k}

(D.44)

for all sufficiently large
n
. Moreover, there exists
C_{\Lambda}\geq 1
such that

\left\|\mathfrak{C}_{k,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}(Q_{\rm tar})}\leq C_{\Lambda}^{k}.

(D.45)

Proof.

The case
k=1
is (
D.37
), after increasing
A_{\Lambda}
. For

k\geq 2
, the localized linearized Stokes estimate around
V_{n}
gives

\left\|Z\right\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})}+\left\|\Pi\right\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})}\leq C_{*}\left\|F\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}(Q_{\rm sh})}

(D.46)

for solutions of

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}Z+\nabla_{h}\Pi=F
with zero lower-time trace in the fixed gauge. The
constant is uniform in
n
, because
(V_{n},Q_{n})
are uniformly bounded strict
shadows and converge smoothly on interior cylinders. Since
s>5/2
,
H^{s}
is a
Banach algebra, and

\left\|\nabla_{h}\cdot(A_{h}\otimes B_{h})\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\leq C_{s}\left\|A\right\|_{X_{s}}\left\|B\right\|_{X_{s}}.

(D.47)

If

a_{k,n}^{\Lambda}:=\left\|Z_{k,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})}+\left\|\Pi_{k,n}^{\Lambda}\right\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})},

then (
D.46
)–(
D.47
) give

a_{k,n}^{\Lambda}\leq C_{0}\sum_{i+j=k}a_{i,n}^{\Lambda}a_{j,n}^{\Lambda},\qquad k\geq 2,

(D.48)

with
C_{0}
independent of
k,n
. Let
b_{1}\geq\sup_{n}a_{1,n}^{\Lambda}
and

b_{k}=C_{0}\sum_{i+j=k}b_{i}b_{j}
. The generating function

B(z)=\sum_{k\geq 1}b_{k}z^{k}
satisfies
B=b_{1}z+C_{0}B^{2}
, hence

b_{k}\leq b_{1}(4C_{0}b_{1})^{k-1}
. Increasing
A_{\Lambda}
gives

a_{k,n}^{\Lambda}\leq A_{\Lambda}^{k}
.

Finally,

D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z]=\mathfrak{B}(V_{n},Z)+\mathfrak{B}(Z,V_{n}),

and the same Sobolev product estimates give

\left\|D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z_{k,n}^{\Lambda}]\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\leq CA_{\Lambda}^{k},

while

\left\|\mathfrak{B}(Z_{i,n}^{\Lambda},Z_{j,n}^{\Lambda})\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\leq CA_{\Lambda}^{i}A_{\Lambda}^{j}.

The polynomial factor from the sum over
i+j=k
is absorbed into a larger
exponential base
C_{\Lambda}
, proving (
D.45
).
∎

D.6
Finite-mode flatness and Galerkin consistency

Let
P_{N}:Y_{s-2-a}\to Y_{N}
be a finite-rank compatibility projection. For a defect

F
, define the finite-mode quotient distance at the moving base
V_{n}
by

d_{N,n}(F):=\dist_{Y_{N}}\bigl(P_{N}F,\,P_{N}\Range(D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}})\bigr).

(D.49)

In a finite-dimensional target space this distance is well-defined even when

\Range(D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}})
is not closed in the full compatibility space.

D.6.1
Finite-order trace-flatness

The next lemma is the finite-order version of the trace-drop mechanism. It is used
only on genuinely surviving branches; if the alternative finite-power estimate occurs,
the branch has already been excluded.

Lemma D.6

(Finite-order trace-flatness alternative)
.

Let

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},\qquad V_{n}\to V\neq 0,

be a finite-mode flat branch which is
K
-sharp:

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K}),\qquad\rho_{n}=\ell_{n}^{1/6}+\ell_{n}^{-N}\delta_{n}^{b}.

(D.50)

Suppose that moving-base jets
\mathfrak{Z}_{1,n},\ldots,\mathfrak{Z}_{q-1,n}
have already
been constructed for some
1\leq q\leq K-1
, with
\mathfrak{Z}_{1,n}=W_{n}
when

q=1
. Define the
q
-th normalized remainder by

Y_{q,n}:=\varepsilon_{n}^{1-q}\left(W_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{q-1}\varepsilon_{n}^{j-1}\mathfrak{Z}_{j,n}\right).

(D.51)

Then either

m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{\alpha_{q}}

(D.52)

for some finite
\alpha_{q}>0
, or
Y_{q,n}
is locally energy bounded and
trace-tight:

\sup_{n}\left\|Y_{q,n}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(Q_{\rm mid})\cap L^{2}_{t}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}(Q_{\rm mid})}<\infty,

(D.53)

\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left\|(I-J_{\Lambda})Y_{q,n}(s_{n})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}=0.

(D.54)

In the second alternative, after passing to a subsequence,

Y_{q,n}\to\mathfrak{Z}_{q}
strongly in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}
, weakly in the local energy
class, and strongly in the localized trace topology. The limit satisfies the

q
-th moving-base jet equation.

Proof.

Subtract the equations satisfied by the lower-order jets from the normalized equation
for
W_{n}
, and divide by
\varepsilon_{n}^{q-1}
. The remainder satisfies

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}Y_{q,n}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{q,n}=-\sum_{i+j=q}\nabla_{h}\cdot(\mathfrak{Z}_{i,n,h}\otimes\mathfrak{Z}_{j,n,h})+R_{q,n},

(D.55)

where
R_{q,n}\to 0
in the localized energy-dual norm. Indeed, every unaccounted
nonlinear term carries an additional power of
\varepsilon_{n}
, while the raw residual is
bounded by
\varepsilon_{n}^{-q}\rho_{n}
, which tends to zero for
q\leq K-1
by
(
D.50
). Localized energy estimates for the forced linear parabolic
equation give

\sup_{t}\left\|Y_{q,n}(t)\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm mid})}^{2}+\int\left\|\nabla Y_{q,n}\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm mid})}^{2}\,dt\leq C\left\|Y_{q,n}(s_{n})\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{\rm sh})}^{2}+C+o(1).

Thus compactness can fail only through trace growth or trace escape.

If a fixed finite-mode part of
Y_{q,n}(s_{n})
becomes visible, then the normalized
projected direction produces a nonzero finite-dimensional obstruction in the

q
-th compatibility coefficient. The finite-mode obstruction dichotomy from Part
III then yields (
D.52
). Since the present branch is
used only in the genuinely surviving case, this alternative is excluded there.

It remains to rule out high-frequency trace escape. If (
D.54
)
fails, the high-frequency trace-drop argument of Appendix
B
applies to
(
D.55
). The heat term gives a coercive decay on frequencies

\gtrsim\Lambda
, while the drift, pressure, cutoff, and residual terms are lower
order under the same sharpness assumptions. Hence a later admissible time

t_{n}\in(s_{n},s_{n}+c\Lambda^{-2})
improves the selected energy by

c\varepsilon_{n}^{2q}+o(\varepsilon_{n}^{2q})
. The minimizers are chosen sharp to

o(\varepsilon_{n}^{2K})
, and
q\leq K-1
, so this improvement is incompatible with
sharpness unless a finite-power relation of the form (
D.52
)
already holds. Therefore trace escape is impossible on a genuinely surviving branch.

The energy bound, the equation for
Y_{q,n}
, and the trace tightness give an
Aubin–Lions–Simon compactness argument. Passing to the limit in
(
D.55
) yields the
q
-th jet equation, completing the induction
step.
∎

D.6.2
Homogeneous finite-mode quotient and triangular gauge correction

The quotient distance used in the finite-mode obstruction dichotomy must be adapted
slightly for the moving-base jet construction. A full-range distance measured distance to
the projected range of
D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}
over arbitrary perturbations. At the

k
-th stage of the jet hierarchy, however, the admissible freedom is smaller: a
correction may be added without changing the
k
-th parabolic jet equation only if
it solves the homogeneous linearized moving-base equation. We therefore work with
this homogeneous range from the start.

For a moving base
V_{n}
, we use two homogeneous gauge spaces. The energy gauge
space is

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,E}_{n}:=\{(H,\Xi):

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}H+\nabla_{h}\Xi=0,\quad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot H_{h}=0,\quad H=(H_{h},0),

(D.56)

\displaystyle H\in L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}\cap L^{2}_{t}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}\text{ on }Q_{\rm tar}\}.

where the equation is understood distributionally and the pressure is taken in the
horizontal harmonic quotient. The strong homogeneous gauge space is

\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,s}_{n}:=\left\{(H,\Xi)\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,E}_{n}:H\in X_{s}(Q_{\rm tar}),\quad\Xi\in P_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})\right\}.

(D.57)

Unless explicitly stated otherwise, in the finite-mode quotient below we write

\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom}_{n}
for the strong class
\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,s}_{n}
. Thus, for a
finite-rank projection
P_{N}:Y_{s-2-a}\to Y_{N}
, define

d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}(F):=\dist_{Y_{N}}\left(P_{N}F,\,P_{N}D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,s}_{n}]\right).

(D.58)

The range in (
D.58
) is precisely the range generated by admissible
strong homogeneous
k
-th order gauge corrections. The use of the strong class is
important: after a gauge is selected, its norm is allowed to enter the
amplitude-weighted diagonal choice in Section
D.8
.

Lemma D.7

(Strong density of homogeneous gauges in finite-mode projections)
.

Fix a finite-rank projection
P_{N}:Y_{s-2-a}\to Y_{N}
. Let

(H,\Xi)\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,E}_{n}
be an energy homogeneous gauge on a cylinder

Q_{\rm mid}
with
Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{\rm mid}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}
. Then there
exist strong homogeneous gauges

(H^{m},\Xi^{m})\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,s}_{n}
on
Q_{\rm tar}
such that

\left\|P_{N}D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[H^{m}-H]\right\|_{Y_{N}}\longrightarrow 0.

(D.59)

Consequently the projected homogeneous range generated by energy gauges has the same
closure in
Y_{N}
as the projected homogeneous range generated by strong gauges.

Proof.

Choose an intermediate cylinder

Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{0}\Subset Q_{\rm mid}
, and choose a time

t_{0}
below the time projection of
Q_{\rm tar}
but inside that of
Q_{0}
. The
standard weak continuity of local-energy solutions gives an
L^{2}
trace

H(t_{0})
, after changing
t_{0}
on a null set if necessary. Approximate this trace
in
L^{2}
by smooth horizontally divergence-free traces
h^{m}
compactly supported in
the spatial section of
Q_{0}
. Let
(H^{m},\Xi^{m})
be the solution of the homogeneous
linearized moving-base equation with trace
h^{m}
at
t_{0}
, in the same horizontal
pressure gauge.

The coefficients
V_{n}
are smooth on the fixed interior cylinder. Energy stability
for the linearized parabolic system gives

H^{m}\to H\quad\text{in }L^{2}_{\rm loc}(Q_{0})\quad\text{and weakly in the local energy class on }Q_{\rm tar}.

For positive time separation from
t_{0}
, parabolic smoothing gives

H^{m}\in X_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})
and
\Xi^{m}\in P_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})
. Since
P_{N}
has finite
rank, it is enough to test against finitely many smooth compatibility test functions.
Moving the singular integral in
D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}
onto these fixed smooth tests, the
pairing depends continuously on the local
L^{2}
-convergence of
H^{m}
and on the
smooth convergence of the coefficients. This proves (
D.59
).
∎

Lemma D.8

(Finite-dimensional homogeneous replacement for small inhomogeneous errors)
.

Let
P_{N}:Y_{s-2-a}\to Y_{N}
be finite rank. Suppose that
D_{n}
is uniformly bounded
in the local energy class on
Q_{\rm mid}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}
and satisfies

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}D_{n}+\nabla_{h}\Theta_{n}=F_{n},\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot D_{n,h}=0,

(D.60)

where
F_{n}\to 0
in the localized energy-dual residual norm on
Q_{\rm mid}
. Then,
after shrinking to
Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{\rm mid}
, there exist strong homogeneous
gauges
(H_{n},\Xi_{n})\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,s}_{n}
such that

\left\|P_{N}D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[D_{n}-H_{n}]\right\|_{Y_{N}}\longrightarrow 0.

(D.61)

Proof.

Choose a lower time
t_{0}
in the buffer between
Q_{\rm tar}
and
Q_{\rm mid}

and let
(H_{n}^{E},\Xi_{n}^{E})\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,E}_{n}
be the homogeneous linearized
solution with trace
H_{n}^{E}(t_{0})=D_{n}(t_{0})
. Then

R_{n}:=D_{n}-H_{n}^{E}
satisfies

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}R_{n}+\nabla_{h}(\Theta_{n}-\Xi_{n}^{E})=F_{n},\qquad R_{n}(t_{0})=0,\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot R_{n,h}=0.

The localized energy estimate for the linearized parabolic system gives

\|R_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(Q_{\rm tar})}+\|\nabla R_{n}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\rm tar})}\leq C\|F_{n}\|_{Z_{s}^{\prime}(Q_{\rm mid})}\to 0.

Testing
D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[R_{n}]
against the finite-dimensional space
Y_{N}^{*}
, moving
the singular-integral operator onto the smooth tests, and using the preceding local
energy convergence gives

\|P_{N}D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[R_{n}]\|_{Y_{N}}\to 0.

Finally apply Lemma
D.7
to approximate the energy
homogeneous gauge
H_{n}^{E}
, for each
n
, by a strong homogeneous gauge
H_{n}
so
that the additional projected error is at most
o_{n}(1)
. This gives
(
D.61
).
∎

Lemma D.9

(Homogeneous Galerkin consistency)
.

Fix a finite order
k
, a strict trace window
\Lambda
, and a finite-rank
projection
P_{N}
. Let

(Z^{\Lambda}_{1,n},\Pi^{\Lambda}_{1,n}),\ldots,(Z^{\Lambda}_{k,n},\Pi^{\Lambda}_{k,n})

be the finite-window hierarchy constructed by the triangular scheme up to order

k
. Let

(\mathfrak{Z}_{1,n},\mathfrak{\Pi}_{1,n}),\ldots,(\mathfrak{Z}_{k,n},\mathfrak{\Pi}_{k,n})

be the extracted branch hierarchy from the failed-selection sequence. Suppose that
the two hierarchies agree through lower orders in the two-limit local topology:

\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left\|Z^{\Lambda}_{q,n}-\mathfrak{Z}_{q,n}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\rm loc}(Q_{\rm sh})}=0,\qquad 1\leq q\leq k-1.

(D.62)

Let

\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}=D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z^{\Lambda}_{k,n}]+\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(Z^{\Lambda}_{i,n},Z^{\Lambda}_{j,n})

(D.63)

and

\mathfrak{O}_{k,n}=D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[\mathfrak{Z}_{k,n}]+\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(\mathfrak{Z}_{i,n},\mathfrak{Z}_{j,n}).

(D.64)

Then

\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}\bigl(\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}-\mathfrak{O}_{k,n}\bigr)=0.

(D.65)

Proof.

The
k
-th finite-window jet and the
k
-th extracted branch jet solve the same
linearized parabolic equation, up to errors which vanish in the two-limit sense.
Indeed,

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}Z^{\Lambda}_{k,n}+\nabla_{h}\Pi^{\Lambda}_{k,n}=-\sum_{i+j=k}\nabla_{h}\cdot(Z^{\Lambda}_{i,n}\otimes Z^{\Lambda}_{j,n}),

(D.66)

while the extracted branch jet satisfies

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}\mathfrak{Z}_{k,n}+\nabla_{h}\mathfrak{\Pi}_{k,n}=-\sum_{i+j=k}\nabla_{h}\cdot(\mathfrak{Z}_{i,n}\otimes\mathfrak{Z}_{j,n})+o_{n}(1)

(D.67)

in the localized energy-dual space. By (
D.62
), the right-hand
sides in (
D.66
) and (
D.67
)
differ by a term tending to zero in the same dual topology. Thus

D^{\Lambda}_{k,n}:=Z^{\Lambda}_{k,n}-\mathfrak{Z}_{k,n}

satisfies

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}D^{\Lambda}_{k,n}+\nabla_{h}(\Pi^{\Lambda}_{k,n}-\mathfrak{\Pi}_{k,n})=o_{n,\Lambda}(1),\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot D^{\Lambda}_{k,n,h}=0.

(D.68)

Apply Lemma
D.8
to the inhomogeneous
solution
D^{\Lambda}_{k,n}
in (
D.68
). We obtain a strong
homogeneous gauge

H^{\Lambda}_{k,n}\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,s}_{n}

(D.69)

such that

P_{N}D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}\bigl[D^{\Lambda}_{k,n}-H^{\Lambda}_{k,n}\bigr]\to 0.

(D.70)

This is exactly the finite-dimensional replacement needed here: the inhomogeneous
parabolic error is first removed by solving the homogeneous problem with the same
lower-time trace, and the resulting energy gauge is then approximated by strong
homogeneous gauges in the projected compatibility topology.

We now compare the compatibility coefficients. Their difference is

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}-\mathfrak{O}_{k,n}

\displaystyle=D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z^{\Lambda}_{k,n}-\mathfrak{Z}_{k,n}]\

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i+j=k}\Bigl(\mathfrak{B}(Z^{\Lambda}_{i,n},Z^{\Lambda}_{j,n})-\mathfrak{B}(\mathfrak{Z}_{i,n},\mathfrak{Z}_{j,n})\Bigr).

The quadratic difference contains only lower-order jets. Testing against the
finite-dimensional space
Y_{N}^{*}
, moving the singular integral defining
\mathfrak{B}

onto the smooth test functions, and using the local
L^{2}
-convergence of the
lower-order jets from (
D.62
), we obtain

P_{N}\sum_{i+j=k}\Bigl(\mathfrak{B}(Z^{\Lambda}_{i,n},Z^{\Lambda}_{j,n})-\mathfrak{B}(\mathfrak{Z}_{i,n},\mathfrak{Z}_{j,n})\Bigr)\to 0.

(D.71)

Combining (
D.70
)–(
D.71
), we get

P_{N}(\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}-\mathfrak{O}_{k,n})=P_{N}D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[H^{\Lambda}_{k,n}]+o(1).

This is exactly the assertion that

d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}(\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}-\mathfrak{O}_{k,n})\to 0

in the stated two-limit sense. The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma D.10

(Vanishing of the branch obstruction)
.

Assume that the branch is
K
-sharp,

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K}).

For every
k\leq K
, the extracted branch coefficient satisfies

P_{N}\mathfrak{O}_{k,n}\to 0

(D.72)

for every fixed finite-rank projection
P_{N}
. Consequently,

d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}(\mathfrak{O}_{k,n})\to 0.

(D.73)

Proof.

The prepared branch satisfies the compatibility identity

\mathfrak{C}(U_{n})-\mathfrak{C}(V_{n})=E_{n},\qquad\left\|E_{n}\right\|_{Y}\lesssim\rho_{n}.

(D.74)

Expanding the normalized branch hierarchy

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}\mathfrak{Z}_{1,n}+\varepsilon_{n}^{2}\mathfrak{Z}_{2,n}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{n}^{K}\mathfrak{Z}_{K,n}+o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K})

inside the quadratic map
\mathfrak{C}
, the coefficient of
\varepsilon_{n}^{k}
is precisely

\mathfrak{O}_{k,n}
. Since
\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{K})
, comparing coefficients through order

K
after testing against any finite-dimensional compatibility projection gives
(
D.72
). The quotient statement
(
D.73
) follows because the distance to any
subspace is bounded by the norm of the projected vector.
∎

Proposition D.3

(Homogeneous finite-mode obstruction dichotomy)
.

Fix an order
k
, a strict trace window
\Lambda
, and a finite-rank projection

P_{N}
. If a genuinely surviving branch satisfies the Galerkin consistency
hypotheses through order
k
, then

\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}(\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n})=0.

(D.75)

Equivalently, if for some
c_{0}>0

d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}(\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n})\geq c_{0}

(D.76)

along a subsequence and along a fixed window, then the branch cannot be genuinely
surviving; instead one obtains a positive finite-power estimate

m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/k^{\prime}}

(D.77)

for some finite
k^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By the triangle inequality for quotient distances,

d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}(\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n})\leq d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}(\mathfrak{O}_{k,n})+d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}(\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}-\mathfrak{O}_{k,n}).

The first term tends to zero by Lemma
D.10
. The
second term tends to zero by Lemma
D.9
. This
proves (
D.75
). If (
D.76
)
held, then either the coefficient comparison in
Lemma
D.10
fails at order
k
, in which case

\varepsilon_{n}^{k}\lesssim\rho_{n}
, or the Galerkin consistency fails at some lower order,
which by the finite-order trace-flatness alternative gives a positive finite-power
selection estimate. In both cases (
D.77
) follows.
∎

Lemma D.11

(Homogeneous range-gauge realization)
.

Assume the conclusion of
Proposition
D.3
. Then for every fixed

\Lambda,k,N
and every subsequence along which

d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}(\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n})\to 0,

there exist homogeneous gauge corrections

(H^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N},\Xi^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N})\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom}_{n}

such that

\left\|P_{N}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}+D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[H^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}]\right)\right\|_{Y_{N}}\to 0.

(D.78)

No uniform bound on the selector norm is required. For each fixed

n,\Lambda,k,N
we set

A_{\Lambda,k,N,n}:=\left\|H^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}\right\|_{X_{s}}+\left\|\Xi^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}\right\|_{P_{s}}<\infty.

(D.79)

Proof.

This is immediate from the definition of
d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}
. Since

d^{\rm hom}_{N,n}(\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n})\to 0
, we may choose

H^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom}_{n}
so that

P_{N}\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}+P_{N}D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[H^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}]\to 0

in
Y_{N}
. This is exactly (
D.78
). The quantity

A_{\Lambda,k,N,n}
is finite because the distance in
(
D.58
) is taken with respect to the strong homogeneous gauge class

\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom,s}_{n}
. No uniformity in
n
is needed in the
frequency-split diagonal argument.
∎

Proposition D.4

(Triangular homogeneous gauge scheme)
.

Fix
\Lambda
,
K
,
N
, and
n
. Starting from the first finite-window jet

Z^{\Lambda}_{1,n}
, one can construct gauge-corrected jets

\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{1,n},\ldots,\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{K,n}

as follows. Set
\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{1,n}=Z^{\Lambda}_{1,n}
. Suppose

\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{1,n},\ldots,\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{k-1,n}
have been constructed.
First solve the
k
-th parabolic jet equation with the corrected lower-order jets on
the right-hand side:

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}Y^{\Lambda}_{k,n}+\nabla_{h}\Upsilon^{\Lambda}_{k,n}=-\sum_{i+j=k}\nabla_{h}\cdot(\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{i,n}\otimes\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{j,n}).

(D.80)

Let

\mathfrak{C}^{\Lambda,0}_{k,n}:=D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Y^{\Lambda}_{k,n}]+\sum_{i+j=k}\mathfrak{B}(\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{i,n},\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{j,n}).

(D.81)

Choose a homogeneous gauge correction

H^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}\in\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom}_{n}
using
Lemma
D.11
, and set

\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}:=Y^{\Lambda}_{k,n}+H^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}.

(D.82)

Then
\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}
satisfies the
k
-th parabolic jet equation with the
corrected lower-order jets, and its projected
k
-th compatibility coefficient is
small in
Y_{N}
. All higher-order jets are constructed using these corrected
lower-order jets.

Proof.

The proof is by induction. The case
k=1
is the finite-window Galerkin trace
construction. At order
k
, the particular solution

(Y^{\Lambda}_{k,n},\Upsilon^{\Lambda}_{k,n})
solves
(
D.80
). The homogeneous correction satisfies

\mathcal{L}_{V_{n}}H^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}+\nabla_{h}\Xi^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}=0.

Therefore adding
H^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}
to
Y^{\Lambda}_{k,n}
does not change the

k
-th parabolic jet equation. The quadratic term at order
k
contains only
lower-order jets, which have already been fixed, so the only change in the
k
-th
compatibility coefficient is

D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[H^{\Lambda}_{k,n,N}]
. This gives the desired projected cancellation.
The induction then continues to order
k+1
, where the right-hand side is built
from the corrected jets. Thus no already-corrected lower-order jet is changed after
the higher-order equations are constructed.
∎

D.7
High-frequency compatibility tails

Finite-mode homogeneous quotients remove low-mode obstructions. The high-mode part is
controlled by the Sobolev tail estimate for the compatibility operator. Because the
homogeneous gauge constants may depend on the selected index
n
, the conclusion is
used in an amplitude-weighted form in the final diagonal construction.

Lemma D.12

(Compatibility tail estimate)
.

Let
s>5/2
. There exist
a_{0}>0
, constants
C_{s}
, and numbers

\omega_{N}\downarrow 0
such that, for every
0<a<a_{0}
,

\left\|(I-P_{N})\mathfrak{B}(A,B)\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}(Q_{\rm tar})}\leq\omega_{N}\left\|A\right\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm sh})}\left\|B\right\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm sh})}.

(D.83)

Consequently, if
V_{n}
is bounded in
X_{s}
, then

\left\|(I-P_{N})D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z]\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\leq\omega_{N}C_{M}\left\|Z\right\|_{X_{s}}.

(D.84)

Proof.

This is the localized Sobolev tail estimate proved in Appendix
C
. The operator

\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}
has fixed order in the localized
Sobolev scale, and the product is controlled because
H^{s}
is a Banach algebra for

s>5/2
. Spectral tail estimates then give
\omega_{N}\to 0
. The linearized
estimate follows from

D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[Z]=\mathfrak{B}(V_{n},Z)+\mathfrak{B}(Z,V_{n})
.
∎

Proposition D.5

(Amplitude-weighted compatibility after homogeneous gauge)
.

Let a genuinely surviving finite-mode flat branch be given. Fix a diagonal stage
specified by a window
\Lambda
, a finite order
K
, a finite projection
P_{N}
,
and an index
n
. Construct the triangular homogeneous-gauge corrected jets

\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{1,n},\ldots,\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{K,n}

by
Proposition

D.4
, and denote their compatibility
coefficients by

\widehat{\mathfrak{C}}^{\Lambda}_{k,n},\qquad 1\leq k\leq K.

For every
\varepsilon>0
, after increasing
N
and then taking
n
large along
an appropriate subsequence, one can make the projected part satisfy

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}\left\|P_{N}\widehat{\mathfrak{C}}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}\right\|_{Y_{N}}\leq\varepsilon

(D.85)

for every amplitude
0<\eta\leq 1
after decreasing
n
-dependent projected errors
appropriately. Moreover the high-frequency part obeys

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}\left\|(I-P_{N})\widehat{\mathfrak{C}}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\leq\omega_{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}C_{\Lambda,k,N,n},

(D.86)

where every
C_{\Lambda,k,N,n}
is finite for fixed

(\Lambda,k,N,n)
. Consequently, in the final diagonal construction one can choose

\eta
after
\Lambda,K,N,n
so that

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}\left\|\widehat{\mathfrak{C}}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\leq\varepsilon.

(D.87)

Proof.

The projected estimate follows from

Proposition

D.3
and

Lemma

D.11
, applied successively in the triangular
scheme. For each fixed
k\leq K
, the homogeneous quotient distance tends to zero,
and hence the homogeneous gauge can be chosen so that

\left\|P_{N}\widehat{\mathfrak{C}}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}\right\|_{Y_{N}}

is as small as desired for
n
large. Since only finitely many orders
k\leq K

are involved, the weighted projected sum can be made smaller than
\varepsilon
.

For the tail, each corrected coefficient is a finite sum of terms of the form

D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[H]
and
\mathfrak{B}(A,B)
, with all fields fixed at the present diagonal
stage. Applying
Lemma

D.12
gives

\left\|(I-P_{N})\widehat{\mathfrak{C}}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\leq\omega_{N}C_{\Lambda,k,N,n},

where the constant is finite but may depend on the chosen gauge correction and hence
on
n
. Uniformity in
n
is not required. After
\Lambda,K,N,n
have been
chosen, decrease
\eta
so that

\omega_{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}C_{\Lambda,k,N,n}\leq\varepsilon.

This proves (
D.87
).
∎

D.8
Diagonal approximate shadows and finite-stage nonlinear exactification

We now construct amplitude-weighted approximate strict shadows along a diagonal
sequence. The construction absorbs all finite losses by choosing the curve
amplitude after the window, order, projection, and selected index have been fixed.
This produces residuals which vanish. Ordinary compactness of a single diagonal
sequence would only recover the base shadow and would not by itself produce a family
of exact shadows with the prescribed first variation. The finite-stage argument below
therefore strengthens the diagonal construction in two ways: it records a
branch-native finite-power residual estimate, and it proves that the finite-stage
finite-stage inverse degenerates at most at a finite algebraic rate in analytic finite
charts. These two estimates, together with the surviving sharpness

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{R})
for every finite
R
, imply the finite-stage smallness
conditions needed for exactification.

D.8.1
Diagonal approximate curves

For a diagonal stage, fix a window
\Lambda
, an order
K
, a finite projection

P_{N}
, and an index
n
. Let

\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{1,n},\ldots,\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{K,n},\qquad\widehat{\Pi}^{\Lambda}_{1,n},\ldots,\widehat{\Pi}^{\Lambda}_{K,n}

be the triangular homogeneous-gauge corrected jets. For
0<\eta<1
, set

\widetilde{V}^{\eta,\Lambda,K,N}_{n}:=V_{n}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda}_{k,n},

(D.88)

\widetilde{Q}^{\eta,\Lambda,K,N}_{n}:=Q_{n}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}\widehat{\Pi}^{\Lambda}_{k,n}.

(D.89)

Lemma D.13

(Diagonal approximate strict shadows)
.

There exist diagonal choices

\Lambda_{j}\to\infty,\qquad K_{j}\to\infty,\qquad N_{j}\to\infty,\qquad n_{j}\to\infty,\qquad\eta_{j}\downarrow 0,

such that the approximate shadows

(\widetilde{V}_{j},\widetilde{Q}_{j}):=(\widetilde{V}^{\eta_{j},\Lambda_{j},K_{j},N_{j}}_{n_{j}},\widetilde{Q}^{\eta_{j},\Lambda_{j},K_{j},N_{j}}_{n_{j}})

satisfy

\left\|\widetilde{V}_{j}-V_{n_{j}}\right\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})}+\left\|\widetilde{Q}_{j}-Q_{n_{j}}\right\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})}\to 0,

(D.90)

\mathfrak{r}_{j}:=\left\|\mathcal{R}(\widetilde{V}_{j},\widetilde{Q}_{j})\right\|_{Z_{s}^{\prime}}+\left\|\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{V}_{j})\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\to 0,

(D.91)

and

\widetilde{V}_{j}(s_{n_{j}})=V(s_{*})+\eta_{j}W(s_{*})+o(\eta_{j})\quad\text{in }L^{2}_{\phi}.

(D.92)

Moreover the diagonal can be chosen so that, for every prescribed positive sequence

\alpha_{j}\downarrow 0
,

\mathfrak{r}_{j}\leq\alpha_{j}

(D.93)

provided the projected homogeneous quotient errors are taken sufficiently small at the
finite stage before
\eta_{j}
is chosen.

Proof.

Choose
\Lambda_{j}\to\infty
so that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left\|Z^{\Lambda_{j}}_{1,n}(s_{n})-W_{n}(s_{n})\right\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}\leq j^{-2}.

Set
K_{j}=j
. Choose
N_{j}\to\infty
. At the finite stage

(\Lambda_{j},K_{j},N_{j})
, apply the homogeneous finite-mode obstruction dichotomy and
then the triangular gauge construction through order
K_{j}
. Taking
n_{j}
large
makes all projected homogeneous quotient errors as small as needed. In particular,
we choose them small enough so that, after the weights
\eta_{j}^{k}
are later inserted,
the projected compatibility contribution is bounded by
\alpha_{j}/4
.

After
\Lambda_{j},K_{j},N_{j},n_{j}
have been fixed, all norms of the finitely many
corrected jets, gauge corrections, compatibility coefficients, and tail constants are
finite. Let
A_{j}
dominate these constants. Choose
\eta_{j}>0
so small that

\eta_{j}A_{j}\leq j^{-2},\qquad\eta_{j}A_{j}^{2}\leq j^{-2},

\sum_{k=1}^{K_{j}}\eta_{j}^{k}\left\|\widehat{\mathfrak{C}}^{\Lambda_{j}}_{k,n_{j}}\right\|_{Y_{s-2-a}}\leq\alpha_{j}/2,

and

\eta_{j}^{K_{j}+1}A_{j}^{K_{j}+1}\leq\alpha_{j}/4.

This is possible because only finitely many constants occur at the
j
-th stage.

The parabolic residual of
(\widetilde{V}_{j},\widetilde{Q}_{j})
consists of the first
linear residual, the coefficients canceled by the triangular jet equations, and the
truncation tail. The first residual tends to zero by
Proposition

D.2
;
choosing
n_{j}
larger if necessary makes its weighted contribution at most

\alpha_{j}/4
. The coefficient terms vanish by construction, and the truncation tail
is controlled by
C\eta_{j}^{K_{j}+1}A_{j}^{K_{j}+1}
. The compatibility residual is

\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{V}_{j})=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{j}}\eta_{j}^{k}\widehat{\mathfrak{C}}^{\Lambda_{j}}_{k,n_{j}}+O(\eta_{j}^{K_{j}+1}A_{j}^{K_{j}+1}),

which is bounded by
\alpha_{j}
after the preceding choices. This proves
(
D.91
) and the prescribed bound
(
D.93
).

The perturbation estimate (
D.90
) follows from

\sum_{k=1}^{K_{j}}\eta_{j}^{k}A_{j}^{k}\to 0
. For the trace, write

\widetilde{V}_{j}(s_{n_{j}})=V_{n_{j}}(s_{n_{j}})+\eta_{j}Z^{\Lambda_{j}}_{1,n_{j}}(s_{n_{j}})+\sum_{k=2}^{K_{j}}\eta_{j}^{k}\widehat{Z}^{\Lambda_{j}}_{k,n_{j}}(s_{n_{j}}).

The first term converges to
V(s_{*})
. The second term is

\eta_{j}W(s_{*})+O(\eta_{j}j^{-2})
. The remaining sum is bounded by

C\eta_{j}^{2}A_{j}^{2}=o(\eta_{j})
. This proves (
D.92
).
∎

D.8.2
Branch-native finite-power residuals

The qualitative diagonal in
Lemma

D.13
is enough to obtain

\mathfrak{r}_{j}\to 0
, but the finite-window correction requires a sharper scale relation.
For the finite-stage closure we keep the same frequency-split and homogeneous-gauge
construction, but we measure the finite-stage residual in the native variables of the
failed branch. Thus we retain the notation

U_{n}=V_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\varepsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},\qquad\rho_{n}=\ell_{n}^{1/6}+\ell_{n}^{-N}\delta_{n}^{b}.

For a fixed finite stage

a=(\Lambda,K,N),

let

\widetilde{Z}_{a,n}(\eta):=\left(\widetilde{V}^{\eta,\Lambda,K,N}_{n},\widetilde{Q}^{\eta,\Lambda,K,N}_{n}\right)

be the amplitude-weighted approximate shadow obtained from the branch-native
finite-window jets and the triangular homogeneous gauges. Its full strict defect is

r_{a,n}(\eta):=\left\|\mathfrak{F}(\widetilde{Z}_{a,n}(\eta))\right\|_{Y_{a}},

where

\mathfrak{F}(Y,R):=\bigl(\mathcal{R}(Y,R),\nabla_{h}\!\cdot Y_{h},\partial_{3}R,\mathfrak{C}(Y)\bigr).

(D.94)

Proposition D.6

(Branch-native finite-power residual)
.

Fix a finite stage
a=(\Lambda,K,N)
. Along a genuinely surviving finite-mode
flat branch, the approximate shadows can be constructed so that there are finite
constants
C_{a}\geq 1
and
p_{a}\geq 1
, depending on the finite stage but not on

n
or
\eta
, for which

r_{a,n}(\eta)\leq C_{a}\eta^{K+1}+C_{a}\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-p_{a}}\eta,\qquad 0<\eta<1.

(D.95)

Proof.

Use the branch-normalized equations rather than only the transported limiting field.
At first order, subtracting the strict equation for
(V_{n},Q_{n})
from the prepared
branch equation and dividing by
\varepsilon_{n}
gives

L_{V_{n}}W_{n}+\nabla_{h}\Pi_{1,n}=-\varepsilon_{n}\nabla_{h}\cdot(W_{n}\otimes W_{n})+\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}{\rm Raw}_{n},\qquad\left\|{\rm Raw}_{n}\right\|_{Z_{s}^{\prime}}\leq C\rho_{n}.

After localization and frequency truncation, the same bound holds in the fixed
finite-window residual norms, up to constants depending on
a
. At order
q
,
after subtracting the already constructed jets and dividing by
\varepsilon_{n}^{q-1}
,
the normalized remainder equation contains the raw term with loss at most

\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-q}
. Since the finite stage only uses
q\leq K
, all raw
branch errors are bounded by

C_{a}\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-p_{a}}

for one finite exponent
p_{a}
, for instance one may take
p_{a}=K+1
after
absorbing localization losses.

The triangular homogeneous gauges do not change the parabolic jet equations; they
only alter compatibility coefficients by terms in
D\mathfrak{C}_{V_{n}}[\mathcal{K}^{\rm hom}_{n}]
.
The projected homogeneous quotient errors are selected at the same finite-power
level, and the high-frequency compatibility tails are fixed once
(\Lambda,K,N)

is fixed. Hence every coefficient through order
K
is exact up to

C_{a}\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-p_{a}}
. When these coefficients are inserted in the curve

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}\widehat{Z}_{k,n}^{\Lambda}
, their contribution is bounded by

C_{a}\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-p_{a}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\eta^{k}\leq C_{a}\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-p_{a}}\eta.

The remaining terms are the algebraic truncation terms of order at least
K+1
in

\eta
. Since all constants at the fixed finite stage are finite, these are bounded by

C_{a}\eta^{K+1}
. This proves (
D.95
).
∎

D.8.3
Finite-dimensional inverse bounds in analytic finite charts

The next step is an optional finite-power route. It rules out super-algebraic degeneration of the finite-stage analytic inverse only under an explicit uniform analytic-germ and nondegenerate-minor hypothesis. The point is finite-dimensional: after the localized parabolic Stokes block and the finite set of homogeneous gauges have been fixed, the remaining finite-window correction matrix has analytic entries in the small branch parameters. If every maximal-rank minor vanished identically, the corresponding low-mode defect would be a visible finite-mode obstruction; such a branch has already been excluded by the finite-mode dichotomy. However, nonzero minors along compactness-extracted branches can still be super-algebraically small unless the uniform hypothesis below is imposed. The final trace-cost/vertical-duality closure therefore uses the amplitude-after-constants mechanism of Appendix
E
, not this optional finite-power inverse.

Definition D.1

(Admissible finite finite-stage correction chart)
.

Fix a finite stage
a=(\Lambda,K,N)
. A finite finite-stage correction chart is called admissible
along the branch if, after the localized parabolic block has been inverted, the
finite-dimensional low-mode strict defect is represented by an analytic matrix

\mathcal{A}_{a,n}(\varepsilon,\eta):E_{a}\to Y_{a},

where
E_{a}
is the finite-dimensional parameter space generated by the chosen
finite homogeneous gauges and
Y_{a}
is the corresponding finite-dimensional
projected defect space, and at least one maximal-rank minor of

\mathcal{A}_{a,n}
is not identically zero as a germ in
(\varepsilon,\eta)
.

Lemma D.14

(Existence of admissible charts on surviving branches)
.

Let the branch be genuinely surviving and finite-mode flat. For every finite stage

a=(\Lambda,K,N)
, after passing to a subsequence and, if necessary, enlarging the
finite list of homogeneous gauges inside the same finite projection, there is an
admissible finite finite-stage correction chart.

Proof.

For a fixed projection
P_{N}
, the finite-stage defect space is finite-dimensional.
The homogeneous quotient construction in
Proposition

D.3

and
Proposition

D.4
says that any surviving projected defect
coefficient lies in the closure of the projected homogeneous range. Since the target is
finite-dimensional, closure is ordinary Euclidean closure. Choose finitely many
homogeneous gauges whose projected images span the finite-dimensional subspace needed
to realize the projected coefficients occurring through order
K
. If no maximal-rank
minor for this chart were nonzero as an analytic germ, then the rank of the projected
homogeneous response would drop identically along the branch. The component of the
corresponding finite-stage defect transverse to this rank-deficient image would then have
positive finite-dimensional quotient distance. By the finite-mode obstruction dichotomy,
this gives

m_{n}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{\alpha}

for some
\alpha>0
, a positive finite-power selection estimate. This contradicts the
assumption that the branch is genuinely surviving. Hence an admissible chart exists.
∎

Lemma D.15

(Conditional finite-power analytic-minor inverse)
.

Fix an admissible finite finite-stage correction chart
a=(\Lambda,K,N)
. Assume that, after passing to the chosen branch and chart, the finite-stage matrices admit a uniform analytic germ in
(\varepsilon,\eta)
and possess a maximal-rank minor whose first nonzero coefficient is bounded away from zero uniformly in
n
. Then there exist finite exponents
q_{a},m_{a},s_{a},t_{a}\geq 0
, a constant
C_{a}\geq 1
, and a choice of
\lambda>0
outside a finite exceptional set, such that along the curve
\eta=\varepsilon^{\lambda}
the localized finite-stage right-inverse and the quadratic finite-stage constant satisfy

B_{a,n}(\eta)\leq C_{a}\varepsilon_{n}^{-q_{a}}\eta^{-m_{a}},

(D.96)

and

M_{a,n}(\eta)\leq C_{a}\varepsilon_{n}^{-s_{a}}\eta^{-t_{a}}.

(D.97)

Proof.

Under the uniform analytic-germ assumption, all operations entering the finite chart are compositions of localized linear parabolic solution operators, finite-dimensional projections, the bilinear map
\mathfrak{B}
, and polynomial algebraic operations in the finite gauge coordinates. Hence the matrix entries of
\mathcal{A}_{a,n}
admit a finite asymptotic analytic expansion in
(\varepsilon_{n},\eta)
on the chart, with leading coefficients controlled uniformly in
n
,

\mathcal{A}_{a,n}(\varepsilon_{n},\eta)=\sum_{\alpha+\beta\leq D_{a}}\varepsilon_{n}^{\alpha}\eta^{\beta}\mathcal{A}^{(a)}_{\alpha\beta}+O((\varepsilon_{n}+\eta)^{D_{a}+1}).

By the uniform nondegenerate-minor assumption, there is a maximal-rank minor
\Delta_{a,n}
whose leading nonzero coefficient is uniformly bounded away from zero. Thus

\Delta_{a,n}(\varepsilon_{n},\eta)=\sum_{\alpha+\beta\leq D^{\prime}_{a}}c^{(a)}_{\alpha\beta}\varepsilon_{n}^{\alpha}\eta^{\beta}+O((\varepsilon_{n}+\eta)^{D^{\prime}_{a}+1})

with at least one nonzero coefficient satisfying the stated uniform lower bound. Put
\eta=\varepsilon^{\lambda}
. The exponents
in the principal part become
\alpha+\lambda\beta
. Avoid the finite set of

\lambda
’s for which two distinct candidate leading exponents coincide and can
cancel. Then the leading exponent is unique, and the uniform lower coefficient bound gives
c_{a}>0
and
\gamma_{a}<\infty
such that

|\Delta_{a,n}(\varepsilon_{n},\varepsilon_{n}^{\lambda})|\geq c_{a}\varepsilon_{n}^{\gamma_{a}}

for all sufficiently large
n
. The standard adjugate estimate for a right inverse of
a finite-dimensional full-rank matrix gives

\|\mathcal{A}_{a,n}(\varepsilon_{n},\eta)^{\dagger}\|\leq C_{a}|\Delta_{a,n}(\varepsilon_{n},\eta)|^{-1}\leq C_{a}\varepsilon_{n}^{-\gamma_{a}}.

Since
\eta=\varepsilon_{n}^{\lambda}
, this is of the form
(
D.96
) after choosing finite
q_{a},m_{a}
.

The Lipschitz constant for
D\mathfrak{F}
on the same finite-stage correction ball is controlled
by the same finite list of chart norms. The strict defect map is at most quadratic in
the velocity variables and linear in the pressure variables, while the finite gauge
coordinates and parabolic solves have just been bounded by finite powers of

\varepsilon_{n}^{-1}
and
\eta^{-1}
. Hence the derivative Lipschitz constant also
has a finite-power bound of the form (
D.97
).
∎

D.8.4
Finite-stage diagonal compatibility

Proposition D.7

(Conditional finite-stage diagonal compatibility)
.

Let the nonzero finite-mode flat branch be genuinely surviving, so that

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{R})\qquad\text{for every finite }R.

(D.98)

Assume, in addition, that the selected finite-stage correction charts satisfy the uniform analytic-germ and uniform nondegenerate-minor hypotheses of
Lemma

D.15
. Then one can choose a diagonal sequence of finite stages
a_{j}=(\Lambda_{j},K_{j},N_{j})
, a subsequence
n_{j}\to\infty
, and amplitudes

\eta_{j}=\varepsilon_{n_{j}}^{\lambda_{j}}\downarrow 0
, such that the corresponding
finite-stage residuals
r_{j}:=r_{a_{j},n_{j}}(\eta_{j})
, right-inverse constants
B_{j}
,
and finite-stage constants
M_{j}
satisfy

B_{j}r_{j}=o(\eta_{j}),\qquad M_{j}B_{j}^{2}r_{j}\to 0.

(D.99)

Proof.

Fix a finite stage
a=(\Lambda,K,N)
, suppress the index
a
, and choose

\eta_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{\lambda}
, where
\lambda>0
is chosen as in

Lemma

D.15
. Combining
(
D.95
) and (
D.96
), we obtain

\frac{B_{n}(\eta_{n})r_{n}(\eta_{n})}{\eta_{n}}\leq C\varepsilon_{n}^{-q}\eta_{n}^{-m}\bigl(\eta_{n}^{K}+\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-p}\bigr).

Hence

\frac{B_{n}(\eta_{n})r_{n}(\eta_{n})}{\eta_{n}}\leq C\varepsilon_{n}^{\lambda(K-m)-q}+C\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-(p+q+\lambda m)}.

(D.100)

Choose
K
so large that

\lambda(K-m)>q+1.

The first term in (
D.100
) tends to zero. For the second term,
choose
R>p+q+\lambda m+1
. Since the branch is genuinely surviving,

\rho_{n}=o(\varepsilon_{n}^{R})
, and therefore the second term also tends to zero.
Thus

B_{n}(\eta_{n})r_{n}(\eta_{n})=o(\eta_{n}).

We next estimate the finite-stage contraction quantity. Using
(
D.97
), (
D.96
), and
(
D.95
),

M_{n}B_{n}^{2}r_{n}\leq C\varepsilon_{n}^{-s-2q}\eta_{n}^{-t-2m}\bigl(\eta_{n}^{K+1}+\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-p}\eta_{n}\bigr).

Substituting
\eta_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}^{\lambda}
gives

\displaystyle M_{n}B_{n}^{2}r_{n}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon_{n}^{\lambda(K+1-t-2m)-s-2q}

\displaystyle\quad+C\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-(p+s+2q)+\lambda(1-t-2m)}.

(D.101)

Choose
K
even larger, if necessary, so that

\lambda(K+1-t-2m)>s+2q+1.

Then the first term in (
D.101
) tends to zero. For the second term, use
(
D.98
) with any

R>p+s+2q+\lambda(t+2m-1)_{+}+1.

This makes the second term tend to zero as well. Hence

M_{n}B_{n}^{2}r_{n}\to 0.

A diagonal choice of
a_{j}
,
K_{j}\to\infty
, admissible
\lambda_{j}
’s, and

n_{j}\to\infty
gives (
D.99
).
∎

D.8.5
Nonlinear exactification without a separate compatibility axiom

Proposition D.8

(Finite-stage exactification preserves the first variation)
.

Let the diagonal sequence be chosen as in
Proposition

D.7
. Then there
exist exact strict shadows
(V^{\eta_{j}},Q^{\eta_{j}})\in\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}(Q_{\rm tar})
such
that

\left\|V^{\eta_{j}}-\widetilde{V}_{j}\right\|_{X_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})}+\left\|Q^{\eta_{j}}-\widetilde{Q}_{j}\right\|_{P_{s}(Q_{\rm tar})}\leq CB_{j}r_{j}=o(\eta_{j}),

(D.102)

and hence

\frac{V^{\eta_{j}}(s_{*})-V(s_{*})}{\eta_{j}}\longrightarrow W(s_{*})\quad\text{strongly in }L^{2}_{\phi}.

(D.103)

Consequently

W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}.

Proof.

Set
U_{j}^{(0)}=(\widetilde{V}_{j},\widetilde{Q}_{j})
,

e_{j}=\mathfrak{F}(U_{j}^{(0)})
, and
\|e_{j}\|_{Y_{j}}=r_{j}
. Let

L_{j}=D\mathfrak{F}_{U_{j}^{(0)}}
denote the linearized strict defect operator and let

G_{j}
be the localized right inverse supplied by the admissible finite finite-stage correction chart.
We solve

\mathfrak{F}(U_{j}^{(0)}+h)=0.

Writing the Taylor expansion gives

L_{j}h+e_{j}+N_{j}(h)=0,\qquad\|N_{j}(h)\|_{Y_{j}}\leq M_{j}\|h\|_{X_{j}}^{2}.

The fixed point map is

\mathcal{T}_{j}(h):=-G_{j}(e_{j}+N_{j}(h)).

On the ball
\|h\|_{X_{j}}\leq 2B_{j}r_{j}
,

\|\mathcal{T}_{j}(h)\|_{X_{j}}\leq B_{j}r_{j}+4M_{j}B_{j}^{3}r_{j}^{2}.

Since
M_{j}B_{j}^{2}r_{j}\to 0
, this maps the ball into itself for all large
j
. Similarly,
for
h,k
in this ball,

\|\mathcal{T}_{j}(h)-\mathcal{T}_{j}(k)\|_{X_{j}}\leq 4M_{j}B_{j}^{2}r_{j}\|h-k\|_{X_{j}},

which is a contraction for large
j
. Banach’s fixed point theorem gives a solution

h_{j}
with

\|h_{j}\|_{X_{j}}\leq 2B_{j}r_{j}=o(\eta_{j}).

Define

(V^{\eta_{j}},Q^{\eta_{j}}):=U_{j}^{(0)}+h_{j}.

Then this pair is an exact strict shadow on the smaller cylinder. The admissibility
bound defining
\mathcal{L}^{\rm str}_{M}
is preserved after the fixed local enlargement used
throughout the program, because
U_{j}^{(0)}\to V
locally and
h_{j}=o(1)
.

The trace map on the finite finite-stage correction chart is continuous, and its norm is absorbed into

B_{j}
. Thus

\|h_{j}^{V}(s_{*})\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}=o(\eta_{j}).

Combining this with (
D.92
) gives

V^{\eta_{j}}(s_{*})=V(s_{*})+\eta_{j}W(s_{*})+o(\eta_{j})\quad\text{in }L^{2}_{\phi}.

Dividing by
\eta_{j}
proves (
D.103
).
∎

D.9
Tangent-cone inclusion

Theorem D.1

(Conditional frequency-split tangent-cone inclusion)
.

Let (
D.12
) be a nonzero finite-mode flat branch which survives every
positive finite-power alternative. Assume the localized strict first-order realization
of
Proposition

D.2
, the homogeneous finite-mode obstruction
dichotomy
Proposition

D.3
, and the uniform finite-power analytic-minor hypotheses needed by

Lemmas

D.15
and
D.7
.
Then

W(s_{*})\in T^{\rm int}_{V(s_{*})}\mathcal{M}^{\rm str}_{s_{*}}.

(D.104)

Proof.

Construct the diagonal approximate strict shadows by
Lemma

D.13
.
The branch-native residual estimate and the finite-power analytic-minor inverse give the
diagonal compatibility by
Proposition

D.7
. Applying

Proposition

D.8
, we obtain exact strict shadows satisfying

\frac{V^{\eta_{j}}(s_{*})-V(s_{*})}{\eta_{j}}\to W(s_{*})\quad\text{strongly in }L^{2}_{\phi}.

This is precisely (
D.104
).
∎

Corollary D.1

(Conditional exclusion of the finite-mode flat non-tame branch)
.

Under the hypotheses of
Theorem

D.1
, a nonzero finite-mode flat branch cannot produce a genuine failed sharp finite-power
selection sequence.

Proof.

Appendix
B
gives first-variation orthogonality of the blow-up trace
W(s_{*})
to the
closure of integrable strict tangent traces. By
Theorem

D.1
,

W(s_{*})
belongs to that closure. Hence

\int\phi|W(s_{*})|^{2}\,dx=0.

This contradicts the strong trace non-loss identity (
D.16
). Thus
the branch is excluded.
∎

D.10
Consequences for the logarithmic program

Under the optional uniform analytic-minor hypotheses, the theorem above supplies the tangent-cone inclusion for the only branch not
closed by Appendix
C
. The remaining implications are exactly those established in Appendix
B
. Without those uniform hypotheses, this appendix should instead be read as providing the finite-window branch-native residuals used by the trace-cost/vertical-duality route.

Theorem D.2

(Conditional strict-shadow reduction via the finite-power analytic-minor route)
.

Assume the conclusions of Appendices
A
–
B
, including the high-frequency trace-drop
mechanism, the finite-mode obstruction dichotomy, and the high-frequency compatibility
tail estimate. Assume also the uniform analytic-germ and nondegenerate-minor hypotheses required by
Lemma

D.15
. Then every failed sharp finite-power selection sequence is excluded.
Consequently, the subcritical covariance-calibrated finite-power selection estimate holds:

\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|U^{\ell}(s_{\ell})-V^{\ell}(s_{\ell})|^{2}\,dx+\int\phi\kappa^{\ell}(s_{\ell})\,dx\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b},\qquad 0<\mu<\frac{1}{6}.

(D.105)

Proof.

Let a failed sharp finite-power selection sequence be given. Passing to the normalized
blow-up produces a strict base
V
and a normalized direction
W
. Appendix
C
treats
fully regular strata, the zero-shadow branch, and tame nonzero singular strata. If a finite
finite-mode obstruction is visible in the remaining non-tame branch, the finite-mode
obstruction dichotomy gives a positive finite-power selection estimate, contradicting
failure. Thus any surviving branch is finite-mode flat. By

Theorem

D.1
, the blow-up trace belongs to the integrable
tangent cone. The Appendix
B
first-variation contradiction then excludes the branch.
Hence no failed sharp finite-power selection sequence exists, and
(
D.105
) follows.
∎

Corollary D.2

(Conditional logarithmic harmonic-pressure approximation and radius bound)
.

Under the assumptions of
Theorem

D.2
, there exist constants
C_{M,\theta}\geq 1
,
\sigma>0
, and

\delta_{M,\theta}>0
such that every suitable weak solution in
Q_{1}
satisfying

\Phi(1)\leq M,\qquad 0<C_{3}(1)=\delta\leq\delta_{M,\theta},

obeys

\mathcal{X}^{\rm har}_{\theta/4}(u,p;M)\leq C_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma},

(D.106)

and

r_{\rm reg}(0,0)\geq c_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma/3}.

(D.107)

Proof.

Appendix
B
proves that the subcritical finite-power selection estimate
(
D.105
) implies the separated estimate

\mathcal{X}^{\rm har}_{\theta/4}(u,p;M)\leq C\ell^{a_{*}}+C\ell^{-N}e^{C\ell^{-N}}\delta^{b}.

Choosing
\ell\sim|\log\delta|^{-1/N}
gives (
D.106
). The
harmonic-pressure comparison step and the Caffarelli–Kohn–Nirenberg epsilon
regularity criterion then yield (
D.107
).
∎

D.11
Role in the final dependency chain

This appendix records the frequency-split finite-window construction for the flat non-tame branch. Its durable contribution is the reduction from all-frequency realization to fixed finite stages. At each finite stage the selected first-order trace is transported to the moving bases, visible low-mode obstructions are separated in the homogeneous quotient, high-frequency tails are controlled by trace drop and Sobolev compactness, and the remaining residual is expressed in branch-native variables as

r_{a,n}(\eta)\leq C_{a}\eta^{K+1}+C_{a}\rho_{n}\varepsilon_{n}^{-p_{a}}\eta.

The finite-stage exactification discussion in this appendix should be read as a finite-stage exactification mechanism, not as the final global closure of the logarithmic program. A full all-frequency strong-inverse or non-phantom statement is too strong for arbitrary compactness-produced branches. Appendices
E
and
F
therefore refine the conclusion: Appendix
E
keeps only the selected-time trace cost and identifies the possible all-order flat trace-quotient obstruction; Appendix
F
shows that this obstruction is removed for Navier–Stokes-derived branches under the vertical-duality active-residual estimate. Thus Appendix
D
supplies the finite-window objects and algebraic branch-native residuals that the trace-cost/vertical-duality closure uses, while the final theorem remains conditional on VD rather than on a hidden global finite-window inverse.

D.12
Conclusion

This appendix identifies the correct finite-window structure of the flat non-tame branch. The mechanism is not all-frequency bounded realization: the linearized pressure-compatibility operator loses ellipticity on horizontal divergence-free high-frequency modes, so an all-frequency approximate-kernel exclusion would be too strong. The frequency-split construction realizes the selected first-order blow-up trace in fixed windows, removes visible low-mode obstructions through the homogeneous quotient, controls high-frequency tails, and builds amplitude-weighted approximate strict shadows.

The output of this appendix is therefore a finite-stage exactification package and a branch-native residual scale. By itself this does not give an unconditional strict-shadow closure; the possible remaining defect is an all-order flat trace quotient obstruction. Appendix
E
reformulates that obstruction in selected-time trace-cost language, and Appendix
F
proves that the vertical-duality active-residual estimate is sufficient to make the trace cost negligible. In the unified manuscript, Appendix
D
is consequently one step in the reduction to VD, not a standalone proof of the full logarithmic theorem.

Appendix E
Trace-cost exactification and adjoint trace descent

E.1
Introduction

The goal of the preceding parts is a logarithmic finite-scale one-component regularity theorem. In local scale-invariant notation, the desired conclusion is

\Phi(1)\leq M,\qquad C_{3}(1)=\delta\ll 1\quad\Longrightarrow\quad r_{\mathrm{reg}}(0,0)\geq c_{M,\theta}|\log\delta|^{-\sigma/3}.

Appendices
A
and
B
reduce this conclusion to a subcritical covariance-calibrated strict shadow selection principle. Given the prepared horizontal trajectory

U^{\ell}=(U_{h}^{\ell},0),\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot U_{h}^{\ell}=0,

and its positive covariance stress

\tau^{\ell}=S_{\ell}(u_{h}\otimes u_{h})-S_{\ell}u_{h}\otimes S_{\ell}u_{h},\qquad\kappa^{\ell}=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\tau^{\ell},

one needs to select a good time
s_{\ell}
and a strict shadow
(V^{\ell},Q^{\ell})
such that

\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|U^{\ell}(s_{\ell})-V^{\ell}(s_{\ell})|^{2}\,dx+\int\phi\kappa^{\ell}(s_{\ell})\,dx\leq C_{M,\theta}\ell^{\mu}+C_{M,\theta}\ell^{-N}\delta^{b},\qquad 0<\mu<\frac{1}{6}.

Appendix
B
shows that this finite-power selection implies the logarithmic harmonic-pressure approximation and the logarithmic Caffarelli–Kohn–Nirenberg radius bound. The next structural input is the singular geometry of the strict shadow trace class.

The strict two-and-a-half-dimensional shadow class is constrained by

V=(V_{h},0),\qquad\nabla_{h}\!\cdot V_{h}=0,\qquad\partial_{3}Q=0,

and

\partial_{t}V_{h}-\Delta V_{h}+\nabla_{h}\cdot(V_{h}\otimes V_{h})+\nabla_{h}Q=0.

Taking the horizontal divergence gives

-\Delta_{h}Q=\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b}),\qquad a,b\in\{1,2\},

so, modulo horizontal harmonic pressures, the strict pressure condition is encoded by

C(V)=\nabla_{h}\partial_{3}\Delta_{h,\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}(V_{a}V_{b})=0.

This nonlinear compatibility constraint is the source of the singular-stratum problem.

Appendix
C
treats fully regular strata, the zero-shadow branch, and tame nonzero singular strata. The branch passed forward is the nonzero finite-mode flat non-tame branch. Appendix
D
introduces a frequency-split exactification mechanism. Low-frequency traces are realized in strong spaces, high-frequency trace loss is removed by parabolic trace drop, and high-frequency compatibility tails are controlled by Sobolev estimates. The final Appendix
D
step then used a finite-stage finite-window correction and required a finite-power full strong inverse.

This appendix revises that last step. The main observation is that the full strong inverse is not the right quantity. The sharp selection problem is a selected-time problem. It only sees the localized
L^{2}_{\phi}
trace. Therefore one may allow the finite-window correction to be large in strong finite-window norms, provided its selected-time trace cost is sufficiently small.

E.1.1
Why the full strong inverse condition is too strong

A nonzero finite-dimensional matrix minor along an arbitrary sequence need not have a finite-power lower bound. For example,

\Delta_{n}=e^{-1/\epsilon_{n}^{2}}

is nonzero but satisfies
\Delta_{n}=o(\epsilon_{n}^{L})
for every finite
L
. The inverse loss is then faster than every finite power. The genuinely surviving sharpness condition

\rho_{n}=o(\epsilon_{n}^{R})\qquad\text{for every finite }R

cannot beat such a loss.

Thus the statement

\text{nonzero strong inverse}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\text{finite-power inverse}

is false for arbitrary compactness-extracted branches. It becomes true along analytic arcs, or under an explicit finite-power non-phantom condition, but the failed-selection branch is produced by minimization and compactness rather than by an a priori analytic parametrization. Therefore a proof of the unconditional theorem should not rely on a full non-phantom strong inverse unless that structure is independently established.

E.1.2
Trace-cost replacement

Let

U_{n}(s_{n})=V_{n}(s_{n})+\epsilon_{n}W_{n}(s_{n}),\qquad\epsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},

where
V_{n}
is a sharp almost minimizer. Suppose an approximate strict curve has selected-time expansion

\widetilde{V}_{n,\eta}(s_{n})=V_{n}(s_{n})+\eta W_{n}(s_{n})+O(\eta^{2}).

Let
h_{n,\eta}
be a finite-window correction which exactifies the curve. In Appendix
D
one demanded

\|h_{n,\eta}\|=o(\eta).

This preserves the first variation exactly, but it requires a strong inverse estimate.

The sharp selection functional only needs a weaker condition. If

\|h_{n,\eta}(s_{n})\\
|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}=o(\epsilon_{n}\eta)

and

\left|\int\phi W_{n}(s_{n})\cdot h_{n,\eta}(s_{n})\,dx\right|=o(\eta),

then the exact competitor decreases the selected-time energy by

-\epsilon_{n}\eta\|W_{n}(s_{n})\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}+o(\epsilon_{n}\eta),

contradicting sharp minimality and trace non-loss. This is the trace-cost mechanism.

E.1.3
Main output

This paper proves four precise statements.

First, a threshold trace-cost right inverse exists in every finite window after quotienting out homogeneous trace directions and zero-trace defect directions. This is a pure finite-dimensional theorem.

Second, the dangerous
W_{n}
-trace pairing of a finite-window correction is controlled by a backward adjoint identity, because
W_{n}
satisfies the normalized linearized strict equation up to a vanishing finite-window residual.

Third, if a trace-cost admissible amplitude sequence exists, then a failed sharp branch is contradicted by an exact strict competitor.

Fourth, any finite-power trace-visible quotient obstruction gives a positive finite-power selection estimate. Hence the only possible remaining obstruction is all-order flat in the selected-time trace quotient.

The result is not yet an unconditional proof of the strict logarithmic theorem. It is a sharper reduction: the remaining obstruction is no longer an arbitrary full strong inverse, but an all-order flat trace quotient obstruction.

E.2
Setup and inherited objects

We use the notation of Appendices
A
–
D
. Let

Q_{\theta/4}\Subset Q_{\rm tar}\Subset Q_{\rm sh}\Subset Q_{\rm str}\Subset Q_{\rm prep}\Subset Q_{3/4}

be a fixed interior cylinder chain, and let
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(Q_{\rm sh})
,
0\leq\phi\leq 1
, be identically one on
Q_{\rm tar}
. All constants may depend on
M,\theta
, and on this cylinder chain.

A failed sharp finite-power selection branch has the form

U_{n}=V_{n}+\epsilon_{n}W_{n},\qquad\epsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},

where
(V_{n},Q_{n})\in L_{M}^{\mathrm{str}}(Q_{\rm str})
are exact strict shadows and
m_{n}
is the sharp squared covariance-calibrated distance. The raw defect scale is

\rho_{n}:=\ell_{n}^{1/6}+\ell_{n}^{-N_{0}}\delta_{n}^{b}.

A branch is called genuinely surviving if it survives every positive finite-power alternative, equivalently

\rho_{n}=o(\epsilon_{n}^{R})\qquad\text{for every finite }R>0.

Indeed, if
(2.1)
fails for one finite
R
, then

m_{n}=\epsilon_{n}^{2}\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2/R},

which is already a positive finite-power selection estimate.

Appendix
B
gives trace non-loss:

\|W_{n}(s_{n})\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}\to c_{0}>0.

It also gives the first-variation orthogonality of the limiting blow-up trace to integrable strict tangent traces. The strict-shadow contradiction is obtained once one constructs exact strict competitors whose selected-time displacement has positive pairing with
W_{n}(s_{n})
.

E.3
What the trace-cost formulation adds

The preceding finite-stage formulation treated the last finite-stage exactification step as if the relevant object were the
full inverse norm of a finite finite-window correction matrix. This abstraction is too pessimistic. The failed
branch is not an arbitrary compactness sequence: it is a sharp minimizing branch produced by
the covariance-calibrated selection problem, and the selection functional sees only a selected-time
localized trace. The correct question is therefore not whether the full strong inverse is bounded
by a finite power, but whether the
active residual
can be corrected with sufficiently small
selected-time trace cost.

There are three pieces of structure that were not used sufficiently in the full strong-inverse formulation.
First, sharp minimality gives finite-
n
variational inequalities, not only a limiting first-variation
orthogonality statement. Second, the prepared branch comes from the Navier–Stokes low-frequency
package and is not an arbitrary sequence of finite matrices. Third, the vertical pressure defect is
not an arbitrary compatibility error: it comes from the vertical momentum balance. The present
paper focuses on the first of these structures, namely the finite-
n
trace-energy variational
information.

The output is a replacement of the full inverse requirement

\|h_{n}\|=o(\eta_{n})

by the weaker trace-cost requirements

\|h_{n}(s_{n})\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}=o(\epsilon_{n}\eta_{n}),\qquad\langle W_{n}(s_{n}),h_{n}(s_{n})\rangle_{L^{2}_{\phi}}=o(\eta_{n}).

These are exactly the conditions needed for the competitor to decrease the sharp selected-time
energy. They are also the natural conditions for excluding only those phantom directions that
are visible in the selected-time trace quotient.

E.4
Navier–Stokes-derived structure missed by the full strong-inverse formulation

The full strong-inverse formulation treats the final finite-window correction matrix as if it were an arbitrary finite-dimensional sequence. This is too pessimistic. The branch produced in Appendices
B
–
D
is a sharp minimizing branch generated by a Navier–Stokes preparation. Several structures are lost if one only studies the full inverse norm of a finite finite-window correction matrix.

First, sharp minimality gives finite-
n
variational information. In Appendix
B
it is used mainly after passing to the limit, where it yields orthogonality of the blow-up trace to all integrable strict tangent traces. For trace-cost exactification one needs the finite-
n
version: fixed-window near-integrable directions are already tested by the almost minimality of
V_{n}
.

Second, the moving bases
V_{n}
are not arbitrary. They are tied to the prepared fields by

U_{n}=V_{n}+\epsilon_{n}W_{n}.

The fields
U_{n}
come from the low-frequency Navier–Stokes preparation, whose residual splits into an additive Reynolds covariance and a genuine one-component residual carrying a positive power of
C_{3}(1)
. Thus the branch has more structure than a general compactness subsequence.

Third, the vertical pressure-compatibility defect is not an arbitrary element of the defect space. It comes from the vertical momentum balance,

\partial_{3}P=-\partial_{t}u_{3}+\Delta u_{3}-\nabla_{h}\cdot(u_{h}u_{3})-\partial_{3}(u_{3}^{2})-\partial_{3}p^{\rm rem}.

This suggests that trace-visible compatibility obstructions should be testable against the small vertical component, rather than treated as abstract algebraic defects.

Fourth, the final theorem is logarithmic. The finite-power selection principle is a sufficient gate, but not necessarily the only path to the logarithmic estimate. This appendix stays within the finite-power blow-up framework, but it records that one should not prove more than what the selected-time energy actually requires.

The conclusion is that the key anti-phantom object is not the full inverse

A_{a,n}^{\dagger}
. The relevant question is whether the active residual creates unavoidable selected-time trace displacement at a scale visible to sharp minimality.

E.5
Finite-
n
first variations

We begin by recording the finite-
n
form of the first-variation argument. This is the
basic variational input behind the trace-cost formulation.

Lemma E.1

(Finite-
n
exact-curve first variation)
.

Let

U_{n}(s_{n})=V_{n}(s_{n})+\epsilon_{n}W_{n}(s_{n}),\qquad\epsilon_{n}=m_{n}^{1/2},

and suppose that
V_{n}
is a sharp almost minimizer:

\mathcal{E}_{n}(V_{n})\leq\mathcal{E}_{n}(V)+\zeta_{n}\epsilon_{n}^{2}

for every exact strict competitor
V
, with
\zeta_{n}\to 0
. Suppose that for each
n

there is an exact strict curve
(V_{n,\alpha},Q_{n,\alpha})
with

V_{n,0}=V_{n}

and

V_{n,\alpha}(s_{n})=V_{n}(s_{n})+\alpha H_{n}(s_{n})+R_{n,\alpha}(s_{n}),\qquad\|R_{n,\alpha}(s_{n})\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}\leq C_{H}\alpha^{2}.

Then, for
\alpha=\lambda\epsilon_{n}
,

\left|\int\phi W_{n}(s_{n})\cdot H_{n}(s_{n})\,dx\right|\leq C\lambda\|H_{n}(s_{n})\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}+C_{H}\lambda\epsilon_{n}+C\frac{\zeta_{n}}{\lambda}.

Consequently, after first letting
n\to\infty
and then
\lambda\downarrow 0
, every strong
trace limit of such exact tangent directions is orthogonal to the limiting blow-up trace.

Proof.

Use
V_{n,\alpha}
and
V_{n,-\alpha}
as competitors. Since the variance term in

\mathcal{E}_{n}
is independent of the competitor, it cancels. For the positive perturbation,
sharp minimality gives

0\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\epsilon_{n}W_{n}-\alpha H_{n}-R_{n,\alpha}\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|\epsilon_{n}W_{n}\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}+\zeta_{n}\epsilon_{n}^{2}.

Expanding the square and dividing by
\alpha\epsilon_{n}
gives one side of the desired
estimate. The competitor with
-\alpha
gives the opposite side. Setting

\alpha=\lambda\epsilon_{n}
yields the stated bound.
∎

The next version allows the curve to be approximate, provided it can be exactified with a
controlled trace error.

Lemma E.2

(Approximate finite-window first variation)
.

Let
(\widetilde{V}_{n,\alpha},\widetilde{Q}_{n,\alpha})
be an approximate finite-window strict
curve with

\widetilde{V}_{n,0}=V_{n},\qquad\widetilde{V}_{n,\alpha}(s_{n})=V_{n}(s_{n})+\alpha H_{n}(s_{n})+R_{n,\alpha}(s_{n}),

where

\|H_{n}(s_{n})\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}\leq M_{H},\qquad\|R_{n,\alpha}(s_{n})\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}\leq M_{H}\alpha^{2}.

Assume that the approximate curve can be exactified to an exact strict shadow

(V^{\rm ex}_{n,\alpha},Q^{\rm ex}_{n,\alpha})
satisfying

\|V^{\rm ex}_{n,\alpha}(s_{n})-\widetilde{V}_{n,\alpha}(s_{n})\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}\leq e_{n,\alpha},

and assume the same estimate for the negative direction
-\alpha
. Then

\displaystyle\left|\int\phi W_{n}(s_{n})\cdot H_{n}(s_{n})\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\alpha}{\epsilon_{n}}M_{H}^{2}+C\frac{e_{n,\alpha}+M_{H}\alpha^{2}}{\alpha}

\displaystyle+CM_{H}\frac{e_{n,\alpha}+M_{H}\alpha^{2}}{\epsilon_{n}}+C\frac{(e_{n,\alpha}+M_{H}\alpha^{2})^{2}}{\epsilon_{n}\alpha}+C\frac{\zeta_{n}\epsilon_{n}}{\alpha}.

In particular, for
\alpha=\lambda\epsilon_{n}
, if

e_{n,\lambda\epsilon_{n}}=o(\epsilon_{n})

for every fixed
\lambda>0
, then the corresponding exactified finite-window tangent traces
are asymptotically orthogonal to
W_{n}(s_{n})
after taking
n\to\infty
and then

\lambda\downarrow 0
.

Proof.

Set

D_{n,\alpha}:=V^{\rm ex}_{n,\alpha}(s_{n})-V_{n}(s_{n})=\alpha H_{n}(s_{n})+S_{n,\alpha},

where

\|S_{n,\alpha}\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}\leq M_{H}\alpha^{2}+e_{n,\alpha}.

Sharp minimality gives

0\leq-\epsilon_{n}\left\langle W_{n},D_{n,\alpha}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{\phi}}+\frac{1}{2}\|D_{n,\alpha}\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}+\zeta_{n}\epsilon_{n}^{2}.

Substituting
D_{n,\alpha}=\alpha H_{n}+S_{n,\alpha}
, dividing by

\alpha\epsilon_{n}
, and using the normalized trace bound for
W_{n}
, gives one side of
the estimate. Repeating the argument in the negative direction gives the absolute value bound.
∎

Lemma E.3

(Trace-orthogonal correction still gives a contradiction)
.

Suppose there are exact strict shadows
V_{n}^{\eta_{n}}
satisfying

V_{n}^{\eta_{n}}(s_{n})=V_{n}(s_{n})+\eta_{n}W_{n}(s_{n})+\eta_{n}Z_{n}(s_{n})+o(\eta_{n})\quad\text{in }L^{2}_{\phi},

with

\left\langle W_{n}(s_{n}),Z_{n}(s_{n})\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{\phi}}\to 0.

Then the failed sharp branch cannot exist.

Proof.

The exact difference quotient

\frac{V_{n}^{\eta_{n}}(s_{n})-V_{n}(s_{n})}{\eta_{n}}=W_{n}(s_{n})+Z_{n}(s_{n})+o(1)

is an integrable strict tangent trace. The finite-
n
first variation, or the limiting
first-variation argument after passing to the limit, gives zero pairing with
W_{n}(s_{n})
. But
trace non-loss gives

\|W_{n}(s_{n})\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}\to c_{0}>0,

and the assumed orthogonality of
Z_{n}
gives a nonzero limiting pairing. This is a
contradiction.
∎

E.6
Sharp minimality and trace-cost descent

For a strict shadow
V
, define the selected-time energy

\mathcal{E}_{n}(V):=\frac{1}{2}\int\phi|U_{n}(s_{n})-V(s_{n})|^{2}\,dx+\int\phi\kappa_{n}(s_{n})\,dx.

The variance term is independent of
V
. We choose
V_{n}
as a sharp almost minimizer:

\mathcal{E}_{n}(V_{n})\leq\mathcal{E}_{n}(V)+\zeta_{n}\epsilon_{n}^{2}

for all admissible exact strict shadows
V
, where
\zeta_{n}\to 0
. Since the minimizer is chosen with
o(m_{n})
error,
\zeta_{n}
may be taken smaller than any prescribed positive sequence after all finite-stage constants have been fixed.

Lemma E.4

(Trace-cost descent criterion)
.

Assume that there exists an exact strict shadow
V_{n}^{\rm ex}
with selected-time displacement

D_{n}:=V_{n}^{\rm ex}(s_{n})-V_{n}(s_{n})=\eta_{n}W_{n}(s_{n})+h_{n}^{\rm tr}+r_{n}^{\rm tr},

where

0<\eta_{n}<\epsilon_{n},\qquad\|r_{n}^{\rm tr}\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}=o(\eta_{n}),

\|h_{n}^{\rm tr}\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}=o(\epsilon_{n}\eta_{n}),

and

\left|\int\phi W_{n}(s_{n})\cdot h_{n}^{\rm tr}\,dx\right|=o(\eta_{n}).

If also

\zeta_{n}\epsilon_{n}=o(\eta_{n}),

then the failed sharp branch cannot exist.

Proof.

By sharp minimality
(3.1)
,

0\leq\mathcal{E}_{n}(V_{n}^{\rm ex})-\mathcal{E}_{n}(V_{n})+\zeta_{n}\epsilon_{n}^{2}.

The variance term cancels, and

U_{n}(s_{n})-V_{n}(s_{n})=\epsilon_{n}W_{n}(s_{n}).

Thus

0\leq-\epsilon_{n}\left\langle W_{n}(s_{n}),D_{n}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{\phi}}+\frac{1}{2}\|D_{n}\|_{L^{2}_{\phi}}^{2}+\zeta_{n}\epsilon_{n}^{2}.

Using
(3.2)
–
(3.5)
and trace non-loss
(2.2)
,
